**Purpose & context**

Aaron is sole developer of the FF8 Accessibility Mod — a `dinput8.dll` injection for FF8 Steam 2013 (App ID 39150, FF8_EN.exe + FFNx v1.23.x) making the game playable for blind players via SAPI TTS, navigation assistance and entity catalogs. Aaron is blind, uses NVDA, and is the primary tester.

**Root:** `C:/Users/ampag/OneDrive/Documents/FFVIII-Accessibility-Mod/FF8_OriginalPC_mod/` · **GitHub:** `ampage87/FFVIII-Accessibility-Mod`. Aaron pushes via `Utilities/push_to_github.ps1`; **Claude never pushes**; diagnostic builds stay LOCAL.

History: `CHANGELOG.md` (authoritative) + `DEVNOTES_HISTORY.md`. This file = live state + rules; under 10 KB.

---

## Where we are at session open

**★ LATEST (v0.42.0 — #103: THE PASSWORD COUNTER WAS COUNTING FRAMES, AND SET TARGET ANSWERS IN A PICTURE).** v0.41.0 advanced the position once per ASK/AASK **opcode firing**, on the assumption that an opcode fires once per question. The BAT: **forty `[REPEAT]` lines in three seconds**, cycling "Letter 1 of 4" through "Letter 4 of 4" over and over while not one letter had been entered. What was being counted was FRAMES. **A blocking field opcode re-runs from the same instruction every frame, and the dispatcher says so in four instructions** (`0x0052A621`): `mov cx,[esi+0x176]` / `call [0x00B8DE94+op*4]` / `test al,2` / `je` **past** `inc word ptr [esi+0x176]` — and AASK's waiting path (`0x00529755`, from `execute_opcode_table[0x6F] = 0x005296C0`) is `mov eax,5 / ret`. **5 & 2 == 0, so the IP does not move.** So the discriminator is the INSTRUCTION POINTER: same `(context, IP)` = the same question still unanswered, say nothing and count nothing; a different IP = the script advanced. Position is **where that IP sits in the order the sites were first seen**, which makes a retry come out right for free — a wrong password jumps back to the first AASK, already site 1, so it says "Letter 1 of 4" again rather than "Letter 5 of 4". **Nothing about the four call sites is written down; the script's own addresses are the table.** The probe decodes all three exe fragments (both `[esi+disp32]` displacements out of the instruction bytes, and the `B8 imm32` in AASK's epilogue) instead of trusting the comment, and puts the IP into its fake contexts at the **literal** `0x176` — a fixture writing it through `REPEAT_PROMPT_IP_OFFSET` would agree with the constant under test by construction. Mutations: count-firings **40**, wrong offset **8**, ignore-context **1**. **AND SET TARGET REFUSES IN A PICTURE.** Aaron: *"No announcement of the error that appears when you try to set target."* `settarget::targetmenu` cursor 0 + confirm is `REQ(seigyo,344)`, and script 344 (`seigyo::settargetselect`) is forty-six dwords whose entire visible effect is `REQ(caution,101)` / `WAIT 90` / `REQ(caution,102)` — raise the CAUTION layer, take it down. **No window and no message: none of `gmmoni1.msd`'s thirty-five strings is that text, because it is baked into the background graphic.** The player picks the one item the scene is named after and the game answers silently. Now transcribed (*"Caution. Authorization required. This control panel is only available to the System Administrator or Commander Okamoto. Enter the password now, or you can not use this system."*), keyed on the **rising edge** of confirm with the submenu already open — `targetmenu` clears `var[1029]` AFTER its blocking REQ rather than before it, so confirm stays 1 for the whole ~8 s the layer is up and a level test would fire on every frame. That same edge is also how the mod learns the submenu opened, since it opens with its cursor already at 0. All twenty-one gates. **BAT: the terminal again — SET TARGET should read the caution; the password should read "Letter 1 of 4" through "Letter 4 of 4", once each, with nothing in between while you are choosing.**

**Prior (v0.41.0 — #102: THE MISSILE BASE PASSWORD, AND THE ONE PATH WHERE IT SAID NOTHING AT ALL).** The password is **not a keyboard** — it is four ordinary AASK choice dialogs in a row on ONE message (`gmtika4.msd` #23, *"Please enter your password. / A / B / C / D / E / F"*). `AASK(win 0, msg 23, firstLine 2, lastLine 7, default 2, cancel 2, 20, 20)`, so the six choices are lines 2–7 and the returned value is the **0-based choice index**; the script at dwords 2189–2320 checks **4, 3, 4, 0** over A–F = **E, D, E, A**. AASK is already hooked, so the prompt and letters read — what was missing was **position**: four identical prompts with nothing to say which letter you are entering. `src/field_repeat_prompt.inl` counts consecutive OPCODE-driven openings of the same prompt and prefixes "Letter N of 4" (the 4 is the script's), restarting at one after a wrong password rather than running on to "Letter 5 of 4". **AND ON ONE PATH THE MOD SAID NOTHING WHATSOEVER:** the four AASKs reuse the same window without closing it, so each opens with the cursor reset to the default — line 2, **"A"** — and the scanner's dedup is `prompt == lastChoicePrompt && curChoice == lastSpokenChoice`, so **choosing A for any letter makes the next prompt byte-for-byte the state it just spoke and it says nothing at all**: no prompt, no letter, no cue that a new entry has begun. **The password ENDS IN A**, so the last letter of the only password in the game hit that path every single time. The position line is now spoken on that branch too — which is exactly why the counter is a prefix owned by its own file rather than a tweak inside the dedup. **SCOPE:** one `(field, prompt substring)` table entry (`gmtika4` + "password"), and only when an ASK/AASK **opcode** fired — not the poll rescan, which would re-count the same dialog every frame. The probe pins the negatives (same prompt on another field, unrelated prompt on the same field, no field name) and three mutations fail it one, two and three ways. `field_dialog.cpp`'s include chain is now ordered by dependency with a note saying so. All twenty-one gates. **BAT: rides along with the terminal — "Letter 1 of 4" through "Letter 4 of 4", and A should still speak.**

**Prior (v0.40.1 — #101: THE THREE SILENT SCREENS, AND THE LABELS WERE BEING SAID TWICE).** The terminal BAT: everything it *does* read works (four main labels, submenu, percentage 4→100 with "maximum" at −150, both outcomes, the yes/no). Three screens said nothing — boot self-check, simulation, upload progress bars. **All three are background layers with baked-in text, so there is no window to read — but all three are FIXED, and the scripts prove it: script 336 (simulation) = 153 dwords, 0 branches, 0 variable reads; script 359 (upload) = 96 dwords, 0, 0.** Not one conditional between them, and Aaron's capture confirms it from the other side: **he ran the simulation with the error ratio at 100% and the report still read "Error Ratio 65~80%"** — a canned demo, not a calculation. That is what makes a fixed transcription honest here rather than a guess, and the only reason those strings are allowed to be literals. **UPLOAD:** `control::uploader`'s Yes branch is `WINCLOSE / REQ(seigyo,359) / if (var[484]&1) var[484]|=2 / var[1029]=0` and **that REQ BLOCKS for the whole animation**, so `var[1029]` stays 1 for its entire ~25 s duration — the one persistent signal either animation offers. **SIMULATION:** there `var[1029]` is cleared BEFORE the REQ, so it is only a start signal; the report is anchored to the game's own **"End simulation"** prompt — the one thing on that screen that is a real window, hence the one moment the report is certainly on display — and since the same confirm both starts and dismisses the run, *the end prompt being absent* is what separates them. **AND THE MENU LABELS ARE NO LONGER SPOKEN HERE:** v0.40.0 said them on the theory that depending on another subsystem to catch the window is working by luck; the log settled it — `[AMES] win[1] Speaking: "SET TARGET" / "CONFIRM EQUIPMENT" / "SIMULATION" / "EXIT" / "DATA UPLOAD"` on every cursor move — so the copy was redundant, and because **AMES interrupts while this file queues, the copy was being discarded anyway**. Cursors are still tracked (they are what identifies the screen); only the speech and its `SnapshotWindowsAsSpoken` call are gone. All twenty gates. **BAT: the terminal again — boot summary on arrival, "Running simulation" then the report, "Uploading… please wait" then "Coordinate data uploaded.", and each label exactly once. Still open: the `gmtika4` password counter (four AASK dialogs, answer E D E A).**

**Prior (v0.40.0 — #101: THE MISSILE BASE TERMINAL — FIVE SCREENS, AND THREE OF THEM HAVE NO TEXT AT ALL).** Field `gmmoni1` is built entirely from field entities and background layers — **no menu module** — which is why nothing in the mod could see it. Every screen is a loop over FIELD VARIABLES at `0x01CFE9B8 + index`: main menu `var[1024]` (0x01CFEDB8) · SET TARGET submenu `var[1025]` (0x01CFEDB9) · ratio bar `var[482]` **signed word** (0x01CFEB9A, 0…−156 in steps of 6) · upload yes/no **and** CONFIRM EQUIPMENT sharing `var[1028]` (0x01CFEDBC) · progress flags `var[484]` (0x01CFEB9C, bit0 ratio, bit1 uploaded). **CURSOR VALUE == MESSAGE ID on both menus** — each block ends `MES(win 1, msg n, 16, 160)` — so the readout is a lookup, and the probe proves it the only way worth proving: it holds **the encoded bytes of messages 0–5 out of `gmmoni1.msd`**, decodes them with the real decoder, and requires the labels to match. Reading that .msd with its first dword as a COUNT instead of an OFFSET — the mistake that turns "SET TARGET" into "CONFIRM EQUIPMENT" — fails it. **THREE THINGS HAVE NO TEXT, WHICH IS THE WHOLE BUG:** the bar is a sprite position with no number anywhere; the yes/no is two sprites lit by `upbyesno` with no window; and **CONFIRM IS SILENT WHEN IT REFUSES** — `if (var[482] <= -150) var[484] |= 1;` and **otherwise nothing at all**, so a refused ratio is indistinguishable from an accepted one, and choosing Yes before the ratio is set is equally silent. So: the bar reads as a percentage, throttled to 250 ms because holding the button walks 27 positions — **except the step that crosses into acceptable, which is never throttled**; "maximum" the instant `var[482] <= -150`; the yes/no named by **what confirming does** rather than by a sprite the mod cannot see; and each screen's outcome reported on exit by diffing `var[484]` against what it was on ENTRY, so a bit set on an earlier visit is not claimed twice. `/` reads back screen + value + both flags, bound only while this field is loaded. **ONE DECODER CHANGE, NOT COSMETIC:** `0xAE`/`0xAF` are an EMPHASIS PAIR (`{AE}Save Point{AF}`, `{AE}CONFIRM EQUIPMENT{AF}`), blank in the Deling grid because they draw no glyph — and they were being counted as **two lost bytes on every such string**, which is exactly the `dropped` signal a caller like the shop reader uses to decide it is holding a fragment. Now consumed silently like colour and icon codes. (The first fixture for that assertion was hand-encoded and put an `L` where the `P` belongs; the bytes now come from `gproof2.msd`, like every other fixture in that file.) Polled at the **top** of `PollWindows`, before the window scan, marking what it says as already-spoken so labels read deterministically and never twice. All twenty gates. **BAT: the terminal — four main items, Set target, ratio to maximum (percentage climbing, then "maximum"), upload, and try Exit early so the refusals read; `/` anywhere. Same trip: does the `gmtika4` PASSWORD screen read? It is four ordinary AASK dialogs (answer E, D, E, A) and AASK is already hooked, so it may already work — if it does, the only gap is knowing which of the four letters you are on.**

**Prior (v0.39.0 — #100: "MOVE RIGHT", AND THE AUDIO DESCRIPTION WILL STOP TALKING OVER THE GAME).** The prison escape **could not be settled from the executable** and that is a finding, not a failure: `gpexit2` polls no buttons, and **all twenty references to the three pad masks in FF8_EN.exe** (`0x01CE48B0` held, `0x01CE48B4`, `0x01CE48B8` pressed) **live in the field module, the two JSM button opcodes and the interpreter — none in the movie player** at `0x0055A140`. There is no interactive-FMV code path; the player is walking a field with a movie behind it. **So the BAT settled it, to the second:** `22:25:44` gpexit2 loads · `22:26:10` RAMESW Rinoa *"Squall!!! Hold on! Over here! Hurry!"* and `disc01_03h` starts · `22:26:12` Aaron's screenshot *"when the player should start moving right"* · `22:26:40` movie ends · `22:26:45` MAPJUMP from (3817,115) → `gppark1`, he made it. **Trigger = field `gpexit2` + movie `disc01_03h`**, two facts the mod already logs. New `src/field_urgent_prompt.inl` holds it as a TABLE (`{field, armAvi, delay, repeat, cap, text}`) so the next such scene is data. **THE CUE ARMS ON THE MOVIE AND LIVES ON THE FIELD** — the movie ends five seconds before the map jump and he was still walking, so a cue that died with the movie would go quiet exactly when it was still needed; **leaving the field IS success**, and that is what ends it. **Polled at the TOP of `PollWindows`, above that function's FMV early-out** — the tail, where it naturally wanted to go, is the one place it could never fire. **AND THE AUDIO DESCRIPTION WAS CUTTING THE GAME OFF:** the same log, one second apart — `22:26:10` Rinoa, `22:26:11` *"High angles look down the prison's giant drill-leg."* **(interrupt)**. `FmvAudioDesc` spoke every cue with `interrupt=true`, and **a cue at 0.0s ALWAYS lands on top of the dialogue that triggered the movie** — so this was never a one-scene accident, it is every FMV that follows a line, for as long as the feature has existed. Cues now QUEUE and log when they queue behind speech in progress; the prompt obeys the same rule from the other side (never interrupts, and skips its slot outright while anything is speaking). **THE PROBE IS THE BAT:** `tests/urgent_cue_compile.cpp` replays that timeline in milliseconds — silent through the head start, silent at +3s *while Rinoa is speaking*, speaking at +3s once she is not, live at +30s when the movie ends, dead the instant the field is `gppark1`, dead at the cap, correct across a `GetTickCount` wrap — and pins the near-misses (`disc01_02h`, `gpexit1`, `gpexit20`, `disc01_03h.avix`). Mutations: kill-with-movie fails 4 assertions, talk-over-speech fails 1. All nineteen gates. **BAT: the prison again if convenient — "Move right" a couple of seconds after Rinoa, repeating until the map changes, and Rinoa's whole line first. The AD fix needs no prison; any FMV that follows dialogue shows it.**

**Prior (v0.38.3 — #99: SEIFER AND EDEA, SETTLED OUT OF THE SHIPPED FILES).** Aaron: *"Seifer is No Mercy and Edea is Ice Strike... hoping you can verify without me having to BAT those two."* **KERNEL SECTION 0** (312 bytes = 39 × 8) carries menu-kind `0x87` at +5 for exactly five commands — **17 Fire Cross (Seifer), 18 Sorcery (Edea), 20/21/22 Limit (Laguna/Kiros/Ward)** — five commands, five characters. (The model had written the set as "20-22": right characters, wrong ids; 19 in between is Rinoa's Combine.) `0x87 & 0x1F` = 7 → `[0x004BCA58+28]` = `0x004C8190`, which **pushes 0** and installs `0x0047E6B0`/`0x0047E6E0` — kind 0. `0x0047E6B0(i)` is `word[0x01CF82C8 + i*24]` = **kernel section 18**, 120 bytes = 5 records, text in section 48 at 0x89C0: **0 No Mercy, 1 Ice Strike**, 2 Desperado, 3 Blood Pain, 4 Massive Anchor — and searching kernel.bin for each name's encoded bytes lands on the same five offsets from the other direction, which is what makes it a reading rather than a guess. **NOTHING IN THE READER IS KIND-0-SPECIFIC**, so this needed no battle — **but a ONE-ROW list is exactly the shape that broke v0.14.13** (Quistis had one Blue Magic spell and the reader announced the command and stopped, for twenty-two builds), and one row is Seifer's and Edea's permanent case, so it is the first thing the probe checks. **The probe plants section 18's ACTUAL BYTES**, not re-encoded ASCII: the descriptions are full of ≥0xE8 pair codes — *"Damage all enemies"* is eighteen characters out of fourteen bytes — so a reader that lost the pair table cannot fake it. **The bank entries go in at LITERAL indices and the command byte at a LITERAL address**, because the first draft planted them through `LIMIT_KIND0_SEIFER` / `LIMIT_CMD_ORIGIN` and both mutations passed: a fixture addressed through the constant under test agrees with it by construction. With literals, the two mutations produce eight failures. **AND THE TURN LINE STOPS SAYING "LIMIT BREAK":** when the toggle at `0x01D7684A` is set the game does not draw "Attack" on row 0 — `0x004BCE80` branches and `0x004BCEFC` does `mov al,[ebx] / call 0x0047EBD0` with `ebx = [0x01D76838]`, which `0x004BB77E` sets to `slot*464 + 0x01CFF02E`. The limit command is **one byte**, named by the engine's own resolver: "Renzokuken", "Duel", "Fire Cross", "Sorcery". **That is the answer to "verify without me having to BAT those two" — Squall stands in for Seifer:** same byte, same resolver, same sentence. All eighteen gates. **BAT: any battle with a full limit gauge — the turn line should name the actual limit command.**

**Prior (v0.38.2 — #98: THE MAGAZINE HAD A SECOND NAME TABLE, AND IT ONLY KNEW THE PARTY).** Aaron: *"I did not hear the name Angelo when reading the Pet Pals magazine. It instead said 'Character' in its place."* The log carried it verbatim — `[TutorialTTS] item magazine id=189 page 33 ... "It's called.. the character Strike!"`. **THE BYTES ARE THE GAME'S OWN:** Pet Pals Vol.1 in `mngrp.bin` ends `06 25 | 03 40 | 20 57 72 70 67 69 63 | 06 27 2E` — colour, **`03 40`**, " Strike", colour, "!" — the identical name insert the field and battle paths use, **0x40 = Angelo**. **But `TutExpand` has never gone through the mod's ladder.** It carried its own lookup, `names[id - 0x30]` over an eight-entry party array, and `TutBuildNames` only ever filled slots 0 and 4 (Squall and Rinoa, the two the savemap holds directly). So 0x40 fell off the end into the placeholder — **and so did Griever (0x50), Boko (0x60) and party slots 1,2,3,5,6,7**: Zell, Irvine, Quistis, Selphie, Seifer and Edea have been reading as "the character" in tutorial and magazine text for as long as that path has existed. **This is the same shape as v0.38.1's bug one day later — a second table, built from a plausible model of the id space, sitting beside an engine ladder that never matched it. Two in two versions; the rule is now: a `0x03` id has ONE resolver.** `TutExpand` takes a `TutNameLookup` the view supplies; the view implements it with `FF8TextDecode::Decode({03,id,00})`, so the five renameable names come live out of the savemap and the other ten off the kernel list. The eight-entry array survives only as the no-resolver fallback (`TutBuildNames` decoding the name buffers themselves, which carry no inserts). **THE REGRESSION TEST IS THE GAME'S BYTES, NOT MINE:** `menu_sim` feeds the real mngrp sequence and requires *"Angelo Strike!"*, and separately pins the no-resolver output as *"the character Strike!"* so the degrade is documented rather than accidental — a fixture built out of party ids would have passed identically before and after. Mutation (ignore the resolver) → two failures. `menu_tutorial_compile` links the real decoder now instead of compiling without it. All eighteen gates. **BAT: Pet Pals Vol.1 and Vol.2 in the magazine viewer — "Angelo Strike" and "Angelo Recover".**

**Prior (v0.38.1 — #98: THE NAMING BYPASS HAS BEEN READING THE WRONG TABLE FOR ELEVEN VERSIONS).** Chasing *"identify all cases where Name 40 may come up"* one step past the decoder landed in `Hook_opcode_menuname`, the mod's **second** number→name table. **`opcode_menuname` is `0x00521DA0`:** `cmp edx,0x14 / ja default / jmp [0x00521FB8 + edx*4]` — twenty-one cases, and **sixteen of them `push <gf> / mov word [0x01CE4762],<kind> / call 0x0047E480`**, where `0x0047E480` is `lea eax,[ecx*4 + 0x01CFDCB9] / or cl,1 / mov [eax],cl` = savemap + 0x4C + 0x11 at stride 68 = **the GF OBTAINED FLAG. So its argument is a GF INDEX and params 3..18 are GF namings.** The old hook read the parameter as a party index and spoke `{Squall,Zell,Irvine,Quistis,Rinoa,Selphie,Seifer,Edea}[param]`, so the classroom study panel — which names **Quezacotl and Shiva** — announced **"Quistis" and "Rinoa"**, and eleven of the sixteen GF namings said nothing at all. **The old comment had already rationalised the symptom** (*"Quistis/Rinoa at study panel"*) rather than check it against the switch: the wrong model was fitted to the evidence instead of being falsified by it. **KIND == GF + 5 for all sixteen without exception; the JUMP TABLE is the irregular thing**, transposing params 8/9 (Carbuncle/Diablos) and 14/15 (Bahamut/Doomtrain) — the handlers sit in kind order, the table entries do not. **So `tests/menuname_compile.cpp` embeds 480 BYTES OF FF8_EN.exe** (`0x00521DD6..0x00521FB6`) plus the real 84-byte table and **decodes `push imm8` / `mov word [0x01CE4762],imm16` / `call 0x0047E480` out of the machine code** before comparing — because *a fixture retyped from the model would have copied the transposition along with it*. Swapping 8/9 in the model → exactly two failures. **THE FIVE NON-GF PARAMS ARE A CLOSED SET AND AN OPEN ORDER:** 16 GFs + 5 nameables = 21 = the case count, so params 0,1,2,19,20 ARE Squall/Rinoa/Angelo/Boko/Griever. Param 0 = Squall is confirmed from the mod's own logs; 1 and 2 are Rinoa and Angelo **by savemap slot order (+0x14/+0x20/+0x2C, stride 12) and are labelled INFERRED**, because the naming screen's destination buffer lives in a **menu.fs overlay, not in the exe** — it cannot be settled statically. **Params 19/20 stay SILENT rather than speak a coin flip** and log themselves as the thing to pin down. Names are now read LIVE through the v0.38.0 savemap path (`03 <id> 00` → `FF8TextDecode::Decode`), so a renamed character is announced by the player's own name. New: `src/field_menuname_model.inl`, `tests/menuname_compile.cpp`. All eighteen gates. **BAT: nothing to force — next naming screen that goes by, `[MENUNAME] param=N -> kind=0xKK gf=G nameId=0xII` next to what was spoken is the confirmation.**

**Prior (v0.38.0 — #97: `[Name40]` IS ANGELO — AND IT WAS NEVER A PARTY MEMBER AT A MISSING INDEX).** Aaron: *"when the limit triggers it is saying 'Name 40' instead of Angelo... I think it also said Name 40 in the magazine viewer with Pet Pals. Let's identify all cases where Name 40 may come up."* **`[Name%02X]` has exactly ONE producer in the whole mod** — `FF8TextDecode::DecodeByte`'s `0x03` branch — so every screen that showed it showed it for the same reason, and one fix covers all of them. **THE ENGINE'S DISPATCH IS A LADDER, NOT A TABLE:** `0x004B8D94` takes 0x30..0x3F through `0x0049A840` → `0x0047EB50(idx)` — **idx 0 → `0x01CFDC70` Squall, idx 4 → `0x01CFDC7C` Rinoa** (the two renameable party members), everything else a kernel table at `0x01CF75EC + idx*36` — and then `0x004B8DAC` **`cmp ecx, 0x40 / je → 0x01CFDC88` = ANGELO**, `0x50 → 0x01CFE754` GRIEVER, `0x60 → 0x01CFDC94` BOKO, otherwise the scratch buffer `0x01D76624`. **All five addresses read back exactly those names from Aaron's own save file**, which is what turns a plausible ladder into a settled one. **So `0x40` IS NOT AN INDEX INTO THE PARTY TABLE at all** — it is a separate branch of the ladder, and so are `0x50` and `0x60`, which would have read as `[Name50]` and `[Name60]` the moment a Griever or Boko line came up. Subtracting `NAME_ID_BASE` from any of them was always going to produce a placeholder. **THE FIVE ARE READ LIVE, NOT HARDCODED**, because they are precisely the names a player can change — the probe renames Angelo and requires the new name back; the other ten come from a kernel table and stay on the built-in list. **WHERE IT SHOWS UP:** Rinoa's Combine row (kernel section 24 entry 0 is literally the bytes `03 40`), the magazine viewer's Pet Pals pages, and any field or battle line that names the dog. **AND ONE MORE, UNASKED FOR: `field_dialog_scan.inl` treats a `[NameXX]` literal as an IMMEDIATE DISQUALIFIER for garbage text**, so a field message mentioning Angelo was being thrown away as noise — it no longer contains one. An id in no branch of the ladder still reads as `[NameXX]` rather than an invented name, and now **counts itself as dropped** so a caller reading the line aloud knows it is incomplete. **All seventeen gates.** Verified by removing the `0x40` case: the probe reports three failures instead of passing. **BAT: Rinoa's limit, and a Pet Pals page in the magazine viewer. Both should say Angelo.**

**Prior (v0.37.4 — #96: THE LEARN NOTICE, READ OFF THE SCREEN — AND v0.37.3'S GUESS ABOUT WHERE IT LIVED WAS WRONG).** v0.37.3 wired the notice to `menu_dialog.inl` on the strength of two calls to `0x004C2B10` inside the item module. **The reader fired ZERO times** — those two call sites are other messages. **TWO CALL SITES IN THE RIGHT MODULE IS A LEAD, NOT AN IDENTIFICATION**, the same mistake shape as `sub_483470` being named the turn dispatcher because it sat next to one. **WHAT THE BAT DID ESTABLISH:** `[MenuTTS] Item focus: 11 -> 111` is the notice state (and `111 -> 4` closes it), and `[MenuGCW]` holds `"...TutorialSaveQuistis learned Electrocute!!!Quistis can learn..."`. **The sentence is COMPOSED, NOT STORED** — mngrp holds the template `<03 33> learned <0A 29>...` (a character-name code, the word, a value insert, "!!!"), so there is no menu-text id to resolve and the finished sentence exists **only in the draw path**. That is precisely the case the project's own rule exists for: **hook the display pipeline, never infer from upstream memory.** The GCW buffer is that pipeline, already captured for the magazine reader. **THE FIRST FRAME IS A TRAP AND THE LOG CAUGHT IT:** `"Quistis learned !!!Junction..."` is drawn before the spell is substituted, so a candidate whose middle is empty is rejected and the scan carries on — the same pre-write frame that made the refine quantity screen read "0" (v0.33.1) and the Slot announce a spell of id 0 (v0.36.0). **THE BUFFER HAS NO SEPARATORS:** "TutorialSave" runs straight into "Quistis", so walking back over letters swallows the whole tab row (the first attempt did exactly that). Two boundaries fix it and **both describe what the buffer IS rather than what this screen happens to contain**: the last `"Save"` (the final main-menu tab, always drawn immediately before this window) and the last lower-to-upper transition (one drawn string ending, the next beginning) — no character-name table, so a renamed character still works. **SCOPE, STATED PLAINLY:** this reads the LEARN notice, keyed on the word the template itself contains; other notices in state 111 (the "cannot learn" message) are NOT covered, and rather than invent a general shape for a window family nobody has surveyed, an uncovered notice **logs its drawn text** so the next one can be added from evidence. `menu_item_compile` asserts the extraction against **the BAT's own two GCW frames, verbatim**, plus the pre-substitution frame and a buffer with no notice in it. **All seventeen gates.** **BAT: use a Blue Magic item on Quistis — the notice should read once. If some other item puts up a notice this does not cover, the log says so with the drawn text beside it.**

**Prior (v0.37.3 — #96: THREE ROOT CAUSES, ALL READ OUT OF THE EXE, ALL CONFIRMED BY AARON'S SCREENSHOTS).** **(1) "ELECTROCUTE" WAS A DIAGNOSTIC THAT NEVER STOPPED TALKING.** v0.14.23 left a block in `battle_tts_diagnostics.inl` that **spoke** on every limit-submenu cursor move: it read a byte at `0x01D76860`, looked it up in a scanned "auto-built" Blue Magic table and announced the result. **Nine of that table's ten entries carry id `0x00`**, so the lookup always returned the first — Electrocute — on every move, including onto empty cells. That is *"Electrocute repeats"*, *"empty slots just read as Electrocute"*, and, where the real reader also spoke, *"the correct spell name alongside Electrocute"*. **v0.36.0 retired this file's submenu-OPEN announce and left the cursor-MOVE one behind: HALF A RETIREMENT IS WORSE THAN NONE, because the surviving half was wrong and now had a correct voice to argue with.** **(2) EMPTY CELLS ON THE LAST PAGE NOW SAY "Empty".** The screenshot shows the window titled **"SPECIAL P. 1"** with four spells in **one column**, and the geometry log agrees (`rows=5 vis=4 pages=2 last=4`); Down from row 3 took the cursor to **7** and Left walked it 7→6→5→4 where 4 is Gatling Gun, so **`cursor = page*4 + rowWithinPage`** and page 2 of a five-spell list has three cells that exist on screen and hold nothing. Silence there is indistinguishable from the mod having broken (the v0.28.1 lesson) and is exactly the gap the stray "Electrocute" was filling with a wrong answer. **(3) THE USE-TARGET CURSOR IS A PACKED POSITION FOR CHARACTERS, NOT A BIT INDEX.** v0.30.0 read `+0x58` as the bit index for both halves; for GFs that is right, for characters `0x004F88EC` says otherwise in three instructions — `movsx eax, [esi+0x58]` / `mov ecx, [esi+0x38]` / **`call 0x004ABC40`**, the same Nth-set-bit helper the refine screen and Junk Shop pickers use — storing the answer at `+0x62`, which `0x004F8920` shifts into the action's target mask. The GF branch at `0x004F88CF` is the other shape entirely (`cursor + 0x10`, shift, test). **Characters are COMPACTED; GFs are not.** Mask `0x08` (bit 3 alone — `0x004F86A2` is literally `and ebp, 8` for that item class) with the cursor at 0 drew a NAME panel with exactly one row, Quistis, cursor on her: position 0 of a compacted list. **v0.37.2 MADE THIS WORSE AND THAT IS THE LESSON:** it patched the ARRIVAL announcement to fall back to the first set bit and left the cursor-MOVE path on the old model, so the entry line said "Use on Quistis" and was overwritten by "Squall, not available" from the very next poll. ***Patching one of two callers of a wrong model makes it wrong AND inconsistent.*** Fixed once now, where both callers read it; the MOVE is detected on the raw cursor and the NAME comes from the resolved id. **(4) THE LEARN NOTICE IS THE SHARED DIALOG.** *"Quistis learned Electrocute!!!"* is not its own window: `0x004F8497` builds the body via `0x004BD630(1,9,8,0,0x64)` and `0x004F84A0` calls **`0x004C2B10`** — exactly the opener whose three globals `menu_dialog.inl` has read since v0.29.0, i.e. **one of the sixteen call sites the #88 audit listed as still unwired.** Announced on a CHANGE of the composed body (the globals keep their contents after the window closes); options are not spoken because this window has none and the option globals still hold the previous dialog's. **All seventeen gates.** The item probe's gapped-mask assertions were **rewritten against the exe rather than against the old implementation**, and it now includes the real `menu_dialog.inl` instead of stubbing it. **BAT: Blue Magic in battle — walk BOTH pages including the empty cells on page 2 (they should say "Empty", and nothing should say "Electrocute"). Then a Blue Magic item from the Item menu: it should say Quistis once, KEEP saying Quistis, and read the notice afterwards.**

**Prior (v0.37.2 — #96: TWO QUISTIS BUGS — ONE FIXED AT THE ROOT, ONE HALF-KNOWN AND NOW INSTRUMENTED RATHER THAN GUESSED AT).** **IRVINE IS CONFIRMED DONE:** *"Shot. Normal Ammo, 20 left"*, then *"15 left"* after a round, no "Miss", nothing saying "Attack" over it — **still untested with more than one ammo type.** **(1) THE USE-TARGET LIST NAMED A CHARACTER WHO WAS NOT ON SCREEN.** Teaching Quistis a Blue Magic from the Item menu: `Use target entered [slot2]: char slot 0 avail=0 mask=0x00000008 -> "Use on Squall, not available"`. **THE MASK IS RIGHT** — `0x004F86A2` is literally `and ebp, 8`: an item carrying flag bit `0x40`, with `[0x01CFE97A] & 1` clear, has its target mask ANDed down to bit 3 alone, Quistis. One row on screen and it is hers. **THE CURSOR IS WHAT IS NOT RIGHT** — `+0x58` is initialised from `0x004ABC40`/`0x004ABC70` at `0x004F8710`, **but only when the item's flag bit `0x20` is set** (`0x004F86C2` jumps the whole block otherwise). For this item the block is skipped, `+0x58` keeps whatever was in it (zero), and the mod named bit 0, found it clear, and announced "Squall, not available" **about a row that is not drawn at all.** On **arrival only**, a cursor sitting on a clear bit now falls back to the FIRST SET BIT — *not* a guess about where the engine keeps its highlight, but the observation that it cannot be highlighting a row whose bit is clear, and a single-bit mask leaves exactly one row it can be on. **Where the highlight really lives while `+0x58` is stale has NOT been found**, so every cursor MOVE keeps the v0.30.0 behaviour untouched (including "not available" on a slot genuinely moved onto), and the log carries the raw cursor beside the resolved one. **(2) BLUE MAGIC PAGING — WHAT THE LOG ACTUALLY SHOWS.** Every row Quistis has read correctly by name and description, **including the one on the second page** (`Gatling Gun`, cursor 4 of 5). The engine's own geometry backs the model: `0x004FF170` computes `[0x01D768F1] = (lastUsable + 4)/4` pages and hands the renderer `[0x01D768E5] = min(count,4)` visible rows, while the row drawers at `0x004C8000` / `0x004C8090` index the list by the **absolute** row (`listBase + row*5`) and use `row & 3` only for the Y position — so the cursor being the absolute entry index is right. **What the log DOES show is a real robustness defect: `0x01D768EC` read 5 with five rows, and v0.36 refused the WHOLE VIEW on an out-of-range cursor** — tearing the session down, so the next readable frame re-announced the **title** instead of the row, mid-walk. **Identification and cursor validity are different questions:** the view survives now, the row is simply not named, and the probe asserts the session lives through it. **NOT CLAIMED: that this is the whole of the paging problem.** `[LIMIT]` lines carry `col`/`vis`/`pages`/`last`/`inRange` now and an unreadable cursor logs itself instead of vanishing, so a BAT on a Quistis with more Blue Magic settles the rest **from the log rather than from a third theory about this screen**. **All seventeen gates.** **BAT: the Blue Magic list with as many spells as you can give her, walking onto and around the later pages — send the `[LIMIT]` lines whether it sounds right or not, the geometry is the point. Then a Blue Magic item from the Item menu (it should say Quistis). And Irvine with a second ammo type if you can buy one.**

**Prior (v0.37.1 — #95: THE STATUS HOLD WORKS, AND "MISS ON IRVINE" WAS NEVER A MISS).** **THE v0.37.0 FIX, MEASURED — the BAT's own numbers with Aura up on two characters:** `[STATUS-TIMER] calls=962 held=849 ran=113 hold=1 | Aura s0=272 s1=-1111 s2=162`, **identical across three consecutive seconds**. 849 of 962 ticks held, and the live Aura counters stand still while the freeze is on (`-1111` is `0xFBA9` read as a signed word — the engine's "no timer" sentinel, so slot 1 simply had no Aura). That is the predicted signature exactly, and Aaron got BOTH limits tested on the one Aura. Both readers clean: *"Slot. Thundara, 1 time. Do over"*, *"Cast"* on the other option, *"Shot. Normal Ammo, 20 left"*. **text_id 0xED IS A LIMIT-BREAK TRIGGER POPUP, NOT A MISS.** v0.13.71 adopted `tid == 0xED` as the miss sprite; the BAT put **eight "Miss on Irvine" announcements on him while every round of Shot was landing** — `[POPUP-MISS] Miss on Irvine` followed each time by `[HP-TRACK] Thrustaevis takes 104 damage`, six shots and six damage events, then two more after the enemy was already dead. **A byte-scan of the whole image for a pushed 0xED settles it: in the battle code there is EXACTLY ONE PRODUCER** — `0x00485242`, the `arg0 == 0` branch of `0x00485220` (its `arg0 != 0` branch pushes 0xEE) — **and `0x00485220` is called from exactly two places, `0x004ADA9C` and `0x004ADB20`, both inside the state machine on `[0x01D7675A]` that reads the input bits and plays a sound on each press: the trigger-driven part of a limit break.** So it cannot ever have been a miss; it fires once per trigger pull. **DELETED, NOT RE-POINTED AT ANOTHER ID.** The real miss text id is **not known** — the damage path builds its popups from a queued record (`0x00485933` passes `[esi+1]` as the text id), so it cannot be enumerated from the call site, and **substituting a second guess is exactly how the first one survived eleven versions.** A throttled `[POPUP-SHOT]` line now carries the return address on every 0xED, so the next BAT containing a REAL miss identifies the true id from the log rather than from a theory. **Nothing is lost: since 0xED only ever came from the limit-break trigger, no real miss has ever been announced by it.** **All seventeen gates.** **BAT: Irvine's Shot again — the shots should be silent apart from the damage lines, with no "Miss". Quistis and Rinoa are still unexercised by any BAT if you want to swap them in.**

**Prior (v0.37.0 — #95: ENHANCED WAIT MODE WAS AGEING EVERY BUFF WHILE IT HELD THE ATB).** Aaron: *"when I cast Aura twice it ended before the character's ATB gauge filled... I am curious if the Enhanced Wait Mode system might not be accounting for status effects / buffs."* **He was right, and it comes down to a single flag.** **THE ENGINE KEEPS THE ATB AND THE STATUS TIMERS ON ONE CLOCK:** `0x004842BD`/`0x004842EE` set `[0x01D28DEB]` to 0 on entry and to 1 only when the ATB loops actually run; the battle state machine at `0x0047D7CD` reads it and skips `call 0x00483470` when it is clear. **Those four are the ONLY references to that byte in the whole executable.** So in vanilla, whenever the ATB does not advance — Wait mode with a menu open, an action in progress — the status timers do not advance either. **EWM BROKE THE LOCKSTEP:** `HookedATBUpdate` saves every gauge, calls the original (which sets the flag, because its loops really did run), then restores the saved values — the gauges do not move, but the engine downstream has been told they did, so **every timed status ages on a frame where no ATB progress happened at all.** **A blind player spends far longer in the menus than a sighted one** — reading a magic list, walking a target row, hearing a description — **and every one of those seconds was being charged to Aura, Haste, Protect, Shell, Regen and Reflect while the gauges stood still.** Fifteen seconds of Aura in the 2026-08-19 log. **`sub_483470` IS THE TIMED-STATUS TIMER, NOT THE "process ready characters / dispatch turns" FUNCTION v0.13.55 NAMED IT:** it walks all seven entities and, for each, **fourteen `int16` durations at entity+0x4C** indexed by the bit position of the status flag at entity+0x00 (`sub word ptr [eax], si` @ `0x004836C1`, decrement 2 / 3 with Haste / 1 with Slow; flag clear `not ebx / and edx, ebx` @ `0x004835E7`; `cmp ecx, 0x0E` = fourteen), with bit 4 queueing a periodic heal (Regen), bit 10 a death on expiry (Doom) and bit 12 setting Petrify. **Aura is bit 8, so its counter is entity+0x5C.** **v0.13.55's OWN BAT ALREADY REFUTED THE READING** — it blocked 6 of 6 calls and the enemy attack landed anyway — **and the conclusion drawn was "hook `sub_482F80` as well" rather than "this is not the dispatcher".** **AND THE HOOK WAS NEVER INSTALLED:** `EWM_InstallProcessReadyHook()` and `EWM_InstallActionExecuteHook()` were defined in v0.13.56 and **called from nowhere**; no shipped build has ever had either, the startup banner lists only the ATB and GF hooks, and there is not one `[DISPATCH]` line in 344 KB of battle log. **`s_blockProcessReady` was maintained faithfully for eleven versions gating a hook that did not exist.** **THE FIX: hold the status timers exactly when the ATB is held** — the same shape as the ATB freeze and the GF-loading freeze already sitting beside it; `sub_483470` does nothing else, so skipping it holds status time and touches nothing. **`EWM_SetFreeze()` is now the only thing that may write either flag, and `tests/lint_freeze.py` FAILS THE BUILD if anything else does** — the risk is not this fix being wrong, it is the two flags **drifting apart** later, which is exactly what happened to `s_blockProcessReady`. **DELIBERATELY NOT DONE: clearing `0x01D28DEB`** — that would be the faithful "the ATB did not advance" signal, but the same gate guards `sub_482F80` on the very next instruction and what that function does has not been read out of the exe; blocking a function nobody has read, on a frame where the active character's own gauge IS advancing, is how turns get stuck. The `sub_482F80` hook is **deleted rather than left dormant** — dead code carrying an unverified belief is worse than no code. **MEASURABLE, NOT ASSERTED:** a new per-second `[STATUS-TIMER] calls=N held=H ran=R hold=1 | Aura s0=.. s1=.. s2=..` line — if `held` climbs while the Aura counters stand still, the hold works. **NOT CLAIMED:** that Aura's intended duration exceeds the fifteen seconds measured; its base table at `0x01CF8B14` and multiplier at `0x01CFE738` are runtime-populated and unreadable from the file. Full findings: `docs/EWM_STATUS_TIMERS.md`. **All seventeen gates.** **BAT: any battle — cast Aura (or Haste/Protect) and then take your time in the menus; the buff should survive the reading. Grep `[STATUS-TIMER]`. Then confirm nothing regressed: turns still come round, enemies still act, GF summons still work.**

**Prior (v0.36.2 — #94: BOTH SCREENS CLEAN; THE SLOT WAS READING EACH OPTION'S EXPLANATION INSTEAD OF ITS NAME).** **BAT: IRVINE IS DONE** — *"Shot. Normal Ammo, 20 left"* straight into *"All enemies"*, nothing announcing "Attack" over it, **not one competing `[SUBMENU]` line in the window** (the v0.36.1 `LimitMenuIsOpenNow()` gate holds on both the entry and exit paths). **AND SELPHIE'S SLOT READS** — it opened, walked both options, and announced fourteen re-rolls by spell and count, every one named correctly (Sleep, Blizzard, Wall, Thundaga, Full-Cure). **ONE DEFECT LEFT, AND IT IS TWO CALL SITES ONE STEP APART.** Kernel section 30 stores each option's NAME and its EXPLANATION two entries apart — **66 "Cast" / 68 "Use indicated magic"**, **67 "Do over" / 69 "Turn the slot again"** — and the engine has two calls that differ by exactly that: `0x004C7A92` **the row drawer** uses `0x0047EC70(id)` with **no offset**, while `0x004C7538` **the help line** uses `id + 2` and writes to `0x01D76860`. **v0.36.1 read `id + 2` for the label — I traced the help-line call and took it for the drawer** — so every option announced its own explanation (*"Slot. Sleep, 2 times. Turn the slot again"*) and `/` ran two further on into "AP received". **THE BAT ALSO SETTLED WHICH ROW IS WHICH:** the player picked cursor 1 and the popup carried `value=0x33`, Full-Cure — **so cursor 1 is Cast and cursor 0 is Do over**, and the list holds 67 at entry 1 and 66 at entry 2. **WHY THE PROBE PASSED THE BUG: ITS FIXTURE PLANTED ENTRY IDS 64/65 AND ASSERTED THE RESOLVER WAS ASKED FOR 66/67 — NUMBERS CHOSEN TO MATCH THE CODE RATHER THAN THE GAME.** *A fixture written from the implementation tests only that the implementation is self-consistent.* It now uses the real section-30 indices and the entry order the BAT observed, so it is a statement about FF8 and not about this file; verified by re-introducing the bug (`SlotOptionLabelIndex(id) = id + 2` turns a clean run into two failures). Also: *"Wall, 1 times"* is now *"1 time"* — a screen reader gives you no punctuation to soften that. **All sixteen gates.** **BAT: Selphie's Slot only — the options should say "Cast" and "Do over", `/` should give "Use indicated magic" / "Turn the slot again", and a single-cast roll should read "1 time". Quistis and Rinoa are still unexercised by any BAT if you want to swap them in. Grep `[LIMIT-SLOT]`.**

**Prior (v0.36.1 — #94: TWO DEFECTS, AND THE LOG HAD BOTH OF THEM IN PLAIN SIGHT).** Aaron: *"Did just Irvine and Selphie. Neither limit announced correctly."* Right on both counts, for two different reasons. **(1) THE INDEX IS A PARTY SLOT, NOT A CHARACTER INDEX.** Irvine's Shot DID open and read correctly — `[LIMIT] open kind=3 char=0 cmd=14 rows=1 -> "Shot. Normal Ammo, 20 left"` — but `char=0` with Irvine, whose savemap index is **2**; and **one line above it the mod's own diagnostic had already printed the answer**: `[LIMIT-DIAG] BASELINE turn=Selphie slot=2 charIdx=5`. `0x01D768EB` (and `0x01D768D9` for the Slot) is the **BATTLE PARTY SLOT, 0–2**, and `0x004C7CD0` takes that slot. **The formula was never wrong** — Selphie sat in slot 2 and the engine stored `0x01CFF3D2`, which is `0x01CFF032 + 2×464` exactly — what was fed into it was. **So the Slot window was REFUSED EVERY SINGLE TIME IT OPENED**, because v0.36.0 tested the slot byte against Selphie's character index and `slot == 5` can never be true: her limit was completely silent, and there is not one `[LIMIT-SLOT]` line in the whole run to show for it. **A FIELD WHOSE MEANING YOU HAVE ASSUMED IS NOT A FIELD YOU HAVE IDENTIFIED** — and the value that exposed it was in the mod's own log the whole time. The Slot now needs **three** creator-written fields to agree: the stored pointer equals `0x01CFF032 + slot×464` for the slot byte beside it, and `0x01D768E0` holds command 16 — which matters, because that byte is shared with another menu (eleven writers in `0x004AExxx`), so on its own it proves nothing. **(2) THE COMMAND MENU TALKED OVER THE LIMIT MENU.** Ten milliseconds after the ammo line: `[SUBMENU] Exit detected via submenu mode 0x01->0xFE` / `[SUBMENU] Exit announce: Attack`, spoken with `interrupt=true` — **"Attack" is the only thing Aaron actually heard.** Opening a limit submenu looks exactly like LEAVING one to the generic handler (the engine drops `submenuMode` to `0xFE` as the limit window takes over) — the v0.14.37 Item bug's shape, one screen over. **v0.14.14 already had a guard for the entry path and it did not fire**, because it tested `s_inLimitSubmenu`, a flag `PollLimitDiag` sets **after** `PollTurnAndCommands` has already run: **A FLAG WRITTEN LATER IN THE SAME FRAME CANNOT GATE SOMETHING THAT RUNS EARLIER IN IT.** Both paths now call `LimitMenuIsOpenNow()`, which asks the engine and is true the moment the window exists whatever the poll order; the limit `.inl` files moved ahead of `battle_tts_menu_poll.inl` so it can. **NOT A DEFECT:** `rows=1` and "Normal Ammo, 20 left" were both correct — the save holds exactly one ammo type. The v0.36.0 BAT note claiming "only Shotgun Ammo" was **my** error: I indexed `FF8_ITEM_NAMES` directly while the real `GetItemName()` treats index 0 as empty, so I named every item one place off; **the engine's own resolver, which the reader uses, was right.** **Probe verified by RE-INTRODUCING the bug** — restore the `slot == 5` test and `battle_limit_compile` reports three failures instead of passing; it now plants the BAT's actual party (Irvine 0, Squall 1, Selphie 2), asserts the slot→character mapping, refuses a slot byte that disagrees with the pointer, refuses a window opened by another command, and checks `LimitMenuIsOpenNow()` is true **before anything has polled**. **All sixteen gates.** **BAT: Aura, Limit — Selphie's Slot should read "Slot. *spell*, N times. Cast", the two options on Up/Down, and the whole line again after a Do over; Irvine's ammo with nothing saying "Attack" over it. Grep `[LIMIT]` and `[LIMIT-SLOT]` — the open lines carry both slot and character now.**

**Prior (v0.36.0 — #94: THE FOUR MENU LIMIT BREAKS, READ OUT OF THE COMMAND DISPATCH).** Aaron: *"add support for character limit breaks... Selphie, Quistis, Irvine, and Rinoa."* **THERE ARE ONLY THREE SHAPES, NOT SIX.** A battle command's kernel record carries a **menu-kind byte at +5**; `0x004BC7EA` masks it with `0x1F` and jumps the 9-entry table at `0x004BCA58`. **`0xA0` means NO SUBMENU — and Renzokuken (Squall) and Duel (Zell) both carry it**, which is exactly why "just let me pick it from the command menu" was the entire job for those two: there is nothing else there. The other four are **ONE list menu** (`0x004C7D00` → `0x004FF0C0`) differing only in which resolver pair it installs — **Shot (14) → kind 3 = IRVINE's ammunition · Blue Magic (15) → kind 1 = QUISTIS · Combine (19) → kind 2 = RINOA** — plus **Slot (16) → `0x004C7920`, SELPHIE's own UI**. Rows at `0x004C7CD0(charIdx)` = `charIdx*464 + 0x01CFF032`, stride 5 (`+0` id, `+1` stock, `+4` flags); cursor `0x01D768EC + [0x01D768F6]`, count `0x01D768F4`, and `0x004FE2A5` subtracts what is already queued this turn from `0x01D76904[row]`. Decoded from the shipped `kernel.bin` the kinds are exactly Quistis's sixteen Blue Magic, Rinoa's two options and Irvine's ammunition. **RINOA'S FIRST ROW HAS NO KERNEL NAME AT ALL** — `0x0047E4F0` special-cases index 0 and returns the savemap pointer `0x01CFDC88`, so it reads as the dog's name with "Fight together with *name*" as its description; **the mod gets that for free because it CALLS THE ENGINE'S OWN RESOLVERS rather than indexing kernel sections itself** (the #78 rule). **SELPHIE:** phase 0 rolls via `0x0048CBB0(&0x01D768DC, &0x01D768DD)` — the **only** call site, so the spell changes on a deliberate re-roll and never on an animation frame — phase 2 is the input state, the cursor at `0x01D768D8` walks 0↔1, and the two labels come from `0x0047EC70(listBase[(cur+1)*5] + 2)` = section 30 entries **66 "Cast"** and **67 "Do over"** (68/69 are their explanations). **THE SCRATCH BLOCK AT `0x01D768D0` IS A UNION** — `0x01D768D4` is the executing party slot in a Draw, a signed cursor in the Slot and a function pointer here — so nothing is read until **two creator-written fields agree**, and `[0x01D768D0] == 0x004C7CD0` is not merely plausible but **UNIQUE**: the only four dword references to that value in the whole executable are the four limit cases of the dispatch. **WHY THIS STALLED FOR TWENTY-TWO BUILDS: v0.14.13 announced "Blue Magic" and stopped because Quistis had EXACTLY ONE Blue Magic spell and ONE ROW CANNOT REVEAL A CURSOR.** `tests/battle_limit_compile.cpp` plants sixteen and walks them — and **maps EXECUTABLE PAGES at the engine's resolver addresses**, writing real machine code into them, so the reader has to genuinely call `0x004C7CD0` to pass; the base stub records that it was called, so a later "simplification" that re-derives `charIdx*464` fails instead of quietly passing. Spoken: command name + the row under the cursor in ONE utterance (v0.33.3 — and battle has an ATB clock), the row on each move, `/` for its description, a count only for ammunition, "not available" on a row that cannot be picked, and **no invented names** (the Card Mod rule). Full findings: `docs/LIMIT_BREAKS.md`. **All sixteen gates.** **BAT — YOUR SAVE SAYS WHERE TO START: `slot2_save30.ff8` has the party as IRVINE, SQUALL, RINOA**, so those two limits need no swap; Quistis and Selphie do. **And you hold ONLY Shotgun Ammo (20), no Normal Ammo** — if Irvine's list turns out to show only what you own that is one row, the exact trap that stalled v0.14.13, and a few hundred gil of Normal Ammo removes it. Cast Aura, take Limit, walk each list end to end, press `/` on a row. Grep `[LIMIT]` and `[LIMIT-SLOT]`.**

**Prior (v0.35.0 — #93: THE PREPROCESSOR HAS A NAME — `sub_4B8B30` — AND ITS TABLE IS `namedic.bin`).** Aaron: *"Go ahead and chase that pre-processor. Go back to the game exe and game files to try and pin it down statically."* **Pinned statically; no BAT was needed to find it.** The route in was the shop's own draw function: `0x004ED278` hands the description pointer to the window drawer via `[0x01D76AA0]`, whose content callback `0x004ED530` calls **`0x004B8B30` to EXPAND the text into a 0x80-byte buffer** and only then `0x004BDE30` to draw it — **which is exactly why no `cmp al, 0x0E` exists anywhere near the drawer: the drawer never sees one.** The expander's tail case at `0x004B8CB8` computes `entry = (code - 0x0E)*224 + (param - 0x20)` and reads `[0x01D2B80C]`, set at `0x004A1980` from the load buffer that `0x0047D46C` fills from the filename at `[0x00B81024]` = **`namedic.bin`** — `main.fs` entry 13, 408 bytes, LZS. Decompressed it is precisely what the code reads: `u16 count` (32), 32 × `u16 offset`, packed FF8 strings — nineteen place names, then **Restores · status · learns · ability · Magic · Refine · Junctions · Raises · command · Magazine · Ultimecia Castle · Garden · Deling**. So the BAT's ten bytes `F0 20 0E 35 20 0E 37 20 0E 36` are **"GF learns Magic ability"**, and v0.34.8's dangerous *" 1000 HP to GF"* — the fragment that sounded like a whole sentence — is **"Restores 1000 HP to GF"**. **Decoding the shipped `kernel.bin` with this table turns all 80 of its substituted strings into whole English, with nothing left dropped.** **THE OTHER HALF WAS HIDING IN PLAIN SIGHT:** the glyph drawer maps `glyph = byte - 0x20`, so the raw encoding IS the sysfnt grid shifted — the two hand-built tables in `ff8_text_decode.cpp` were never independent, and they agree on every one of ~90 shared bytes except seven slots where the Deling grid is blank. `s_charTable` is therefore **derived** from the glyph table plus a seven-entry override list, and the **57 bytes it had no entry for at all** (accented letters, brackets, semicolon, degrees, `TM`, `<`, `>`) resolve instead of vanishing. `0x0D`, `0x08` and `0x09` also stopped leaking their parameter bytes (`0x004B8D21` consumes one for all of `0x05/0x06/0x08/0x09/0x0A/0x0B`). **`ShopCountDropped()` IS DELETED:** it hard-coded a copy of "what the decoder throws away", which stopped being true the moment the decoder stopped throwing it away — `Decode()` reports its own losses now through `droppedOut`, so reader and decoder cannot disagree, and `"Partial description:"` still fires (the probe proves it by taking the word table away) but on real text never has to. `field_dialog_expand.inl` loses its private copy of the same table read and calls `FF8TextDecode::ResolveWord()`. **`tests/text_decode_compile.cpp` IS NEW AND OVERDUE:** the decoder is the one piece every screen depends on and no probe had ever run — ten of eleven menu probes STUB it, which is how v0.34.9 shipped a decoder that dropped a word out of every item description in the game with all fourteen gates green. The new probe links the **real** decoder and drives it with the **real** 408 bytes of `namedic.bin`; `menu_shop_compile` now links it too, instead of a stub that "mirrors the real one where it matters" — it did not, it dropped every byte ≥ 0x80, so it agreed with the bug. Full findings: `docs/TEXT_ENCODING.md`. **All fifteen gates.** **BAT PASSED (2026-08-19, item shop): zero `[SHOP-TEXT]` lossy lines, zero "Partial description:", and EVERY PREDICTION HELD** — `"GF"` → **"GF learns Magic ability"**, `" 1000 HP to GF"` → **"Restores 1000 HP to GF"**, `" HP to all GF"` → **"Restores HP to all GF"**, `" for dog lovers"` → **"Magazine for dog lovers"**, `"Makes GF forget an"` → **"Makes GF forget an ability"**, plus every scroll on the shelf whole. **Those ten bytes were decoded on paper, out of the exe and the archive, before the game ever saw the build, and the game said the same sentence back.** **AND THIS WAS NEVER ONLY A SHOP FIX:** `FF8TextDecode::Decode` has **57 call sites across 24 files** — every menu, the battle readers, the field scan, save, junction, magic, cards, status. Anywhere a `0x0E` or a byte in `0x79`–`0xCB` appeared in game text it was being dropped without a word. **NEXT (all named in `docs/TEXT_ENCODING.md` §6, none blocking): `0x04` outside the field path still loses its number and keeps the historical `". "` (counted, so at least audible as a gap); `0x10`–`0x1F` name codes are counted-as-lost rather than resolved through `sub_49A860` (no real text observed using them); the `>= 0xE8` pair table is still hard-coded rather than read from the engine's own copy. Unchanged from before: Call Shop untested (Aaron lacks the ability), 16 of 20 shared yes/no call sites unwired, Zell's Duel button inputs, dimmed ability rows.**

**Prior (v0.34.9 — #92: THE HEX NAMED THE CODE — `0x0E xx` IS A SUBSTITUTED WORD).** One `[SHOP-TEXT]` line settled it: **`F0 20 0E 35 20 0E 37 20 0E 36` → "GF"**. `F0` = the two-char code "GF", `20` = space, and **`0E xx` is a word the game substitutes** — which `DecodeByte` treats as an ICON and silently eats both bytes of. That is every gap in the BAT: the missing "Restores" in *"Restores 1000 HP to GF"*, the missing "ability" in *"Makes GF forget an ability"*. Bytes 0x80..0xE7 go the same way. **NEXT PIECE OF WORK: the 0x0E substitution table** — not findable by byte search (the menu text path dispatches control codes through a JUMP TABLE; there is no `cmp al, 0x0E` in `0x004Bxxxx`). **It will fix every screen that reads kernel text, not just shops.** **WHAT SHIPS NOW: knowing instead of estimating.** v0.34.8 flagged lossiness by LENGTH RATIO — it caught "GF" out of ten bytes and **passed the dangerous ones**, because *" 1000 HP to GF"* sounds like a whole sentence. `ShopCountDropped()` counts the bytes actually discarded (each `0x0E` pair, each 0x80..0xE7), so one missing word in a long sentence is flagged as surely as nine out of ten, and a fully-understood string is not flagged at all. Probe fixtures are the Magic Scroll's REAL bytes plus the sentence that loses only its first word. Rest of the BAT clean: sell list on its own cursor + price table, both stock pages, quantity 1..72, holdings updating after purchase, no pool dumps. **All fourteen gates.** **NEXT: hunt the 0x0E table via the text-draw jump table (0x004BDE30 handles 0x02/0x05 and terminates on anything ≤0x18, so 0x0E is expanded BEFORE it — find the preprocessor).**

**Prior (v0.34.8 — #92: THE SELL SIDE IS CLEAN; THE DESCRIPTIONS ARE FRAGMENTS AND NOW SAY SO).** **BAT clean on everything the shop does:** the SELL list reads off its OWN cursor and OWN price table (*"Potion, 50 gil, have 34"* vs a buy price of 100; 25 pages vs 2) — the likeliest thing to have broken, and it did not. Buy/Sell/Exit, both stock pages, quantity counted 1..72 with running total and gil left, holding updated to "have 73" after the purchase, zero pool dumps (v0.34.7 gating works). **ONE REAL DEFECT: `/` GIVES FRAGMENTS.** *" HP to all GF"* (Pet House), *"GF"* (Magic Scroll), *" for dog lovers"* (Pet Pals Vol. 4) — while Phoenix Down's *"Removes KO"* is whole. **NOT truncations — they are the pieces `FF8TextDecode::Decode` recognised, with every unrecognised byte SILENTLY DROPPED** (0x80–0xE7 skipped by design; `DecodeByte`'s own comment says so). **A player hearing "GF" cannot tell he got two characters of a sentence.** The shop's labels and messages decode perfectly through the same call, so the encoding is right for THOSE; descriptions come from another bank — `0x0047EA90` → `0x01CF3E48 + [0x01CF3EE4] + off`, **two tables split at item id 0x21**, which is exactly where good and bad split (Phoenix Down 7 ✓; Pet House 34, Magic Scroll 55, Pet Pals 192 ✗). **NOT GUESSED A FOURTH TIME: `[SHOP-TEXT]` logs the RAW BYTES beside any suspiciously short decode**, and meanwhile the fragment is spoken as **"Partial description: ..."** so it cannot pass for the whole sentence (the Card Mod principle). Probe's Decode stub now mirrors the real one where it matters (≥0x80 dropped), so the lossy case reproduces without the game. **All fourteen gates.** **BAT: in a shop press `/` on a few items and send the `[SHOP-TEXT]` lines — those hex bytes are what fix the decoder for good.**

**Prior (v0.34.7 — #92: THE ITEM SHOP IS DONE; THE DIAGNOSTIC IS GATED).** **BAT clean end to end:** *"Shop, 71400 gil. Buy"* the moment the action bar appears, then Sell/Buy; the game's own messages ("Welcome" / "What do you want to buy?" / "Come back soon!"); rows with name, price and holding across both pages ("G-Hi-Potion, 600 gil, have 8"); the quantity screen with running total and gil left (*"1 of 71 Pet Pals Vol. 3, 1000 gil, 70400 left"*); and the holding updating to "have 1" after the purchase. Zero errors. **THE POOL DIAGNOSTIC IS NOW `SHOP_POOL_DIAG 0` — GATED, NOT DELETED** (ABIL_DIAG / REFINE_FLOW_DIAG convention). It fired 121 times in that log, eighty of them on screens that were NOT shops, and the reason is worth keeping: **GAME MODE 10 IS NOT SHOP-ONLY** — the same log shows the party-SWITCH screen (`upd=004CBA50`, dispatch 9/10) running in it — **and the shop-number global at `0x01D75450` goes STALE between shops**, so the gate read "type 0 = ITEM" on a screen with no shop in it. **RULE: the module's own `+0x45` is the authoritative shop type; the global is only trustworthy while a shop is being OPENED, which is exactly when the creator reads it.** The diagnostic earned its keep first — its dump identified the clobbered `+0x08` AND the unwritten `+0x47` in one line. **Both `SHOP_POOL_DIAG` settings compile; probe passes under each. All fourteen gates.** **STILL UNEXERCISED BY ANY BAT (all three covered by the probe): the SELL list, `/` on a shop row, and the "not enough gil" message.** **BAT: in a shop, switch to SELL and walk the list (rows AND prices must differ from Buy), sell something, press `/` on a row for its description, and try to buy something you cannot afford.**

**Prior (v0.34.6 — #92: THE DRAW FN WAS INTACT ALL ALONG).** **The item shop reads.** The pool dump names the cause in one line: `slot 1 @01D76C40 inUse=1 state=0x03 upd=605D8130 draw=004ED1B0 +2C=01CFE79C +45=1 +46=0 +47=0`. **`+0x08` is overwritten with a heap pointer** — the same thing SUBMENU_AUDIT §9 records for the Item MENU module (`0x605D8200`, strikingly close) — **and `+0x0C`, the DRAW fn (`0x004ED1B0`), is untouched.** **Four builds hunted a value that is not there while the field next to it was right the whole time.** Identification now matches EITHER `+0x08` or `+0x0C`. **AND THAT EXPLAINS THE LAST SYMPTOM EXACTLY** — *"when the item shop first appears the action bar does not read out; once I pick an option it works"*: the v0.34.5 field fallback required `+0x47 ∈ {2,25}`, but **`+0x47` is written by the top-menu CONFIRM (`0x004EC2F3`/`0x004EC306`), not by the creator**, so it is 0 on the action bar and the fallback could not fire until a choice was made (every dump shows `+0x47=0` at state 3). **THIRD identification in this file keyed on a field the engine had not written yet** (`+0x46` v0.34.0, `+0x47` v0.34.5) — **the rule that survives: only test fields the CREATOR sets.** Also fixed: the "Shop, N gil" entry line was built into a local during states 0..2 where there is no row to attach it to, so it was thrown away every time; it now survives to the first thing spoken ("Shop, 4900 gil. Buy"). Rest of the log correct — buy rows read name/price/holding, top row reads the game's own labels, shop identified as shop=1 type=1. **Verified: probe builds the module exactly as the dump shows it (+0x08 clobbered, +0x47 zero) and asserts it is found and read at state 3 before any choice. All fourteen gates.** **BAT: a Balamb Shop — the action bar must read "Shop, N gil. Buy" the moment it appears, then Sell/Exit; buy something; switch to Sell (row AND price change); sell something; overspend for the message; `/` on a row.**

**Prior (v0.34.5 — #92: THE EXE MODEL RE-VERIFIED END TO END, AND A MODULE-FREE WAY TO SAY WHICH SHOP IS OPEN).** Aaron asked for the exe to be checked before another BAT. Done — **and it is airtight, which is the finding**: the problem has never been the model. **ONE POOL ONLY:** just `0x004BE540` and `0x004BE5B0` reference `0x01D76BC8`; both use TEN `0x78` slots, both store arg0 at `+0x08` and set `+0x12`, and the free routine `0x004BE610` clears both. **The two list heads (`0x01D76B48`, `0x01D76ACC`) thread the SAME array**, so a ten-slot scan cannot miss a module that exists and there is no second pool to hide in. **DISPATCH CONFIRMED:** `0x004BDB30` reads `[idx*8 + 0xB87ED8]` = {creator, music kind} — **11 = item shop (`0x004EDA40`), 12 = Junk Shop (`0x004EA4D0`)**. My earlier read was off by one entry: I guessed the base at `0xB87F00`, and **nothing in .text references that address**. (`shop.ovl` is a dead PSX overlay name — `menushop.ovl` and siblings sit unreferenced in the same block. Ruled out.) **WHAT WAS MISSING WAS EVIDENCE, NOT ANALYSIS. `0x004B2D70` is two instructions:** `[0x01D75450] & 0xFF` = shop number; type = `byte [0x00B88918 + n]`, a 52-entry table (0..20 → themselves, 21..31 → 0, **32..42 → 21 = Junk**; its length is exact because index 52 begins the string "shop.bin"). The mod now reads that **with no module, no pool, no list walk** — so the log distinguishes *"an ITEM shop is open and I could not read it"* from *"no shop was open"*, which none of the three previous BATs could. **AND THAT REVIEW MATTERS: the only CONFIRMED item-shop visit (18:09, "Balamb Shop" screenshot) was on v0.34.1, which still used the MRU-LIST WALK. The pool scan arrived in v0.34.2 and has never demonstrably been inside a Balamb Shop** — the 22 s mode-10 window in the v0.34.3 log has no screenshot and may not have been one. Pool dump now fires only on the exact failing case and leads with shop number + type. **Verified: all fourteen gates.** **BAT: go to a BALAMB SHOP (item shop) specifically and stay in it ~15 s. Then grep `[SHOP]`: "entered item shop: shop=N type=T" means it works; "found by FIELDS" means `+0x08` was clobbered; `[SHOP-POOL] #n shop=N type=T (ITEM)` lines mean it is genuinely unfindable and the dump now proves it.**

**Prior (v0.34.4 — #92: THE DIAGNOSTIC FIRED A FRAME TOO EARLY, AND A SECOND WAY TO RECOGNISE THE SHOP).** **The JUNK SHOP is complete and correct:** *"Needs 6 M-Stone Piece, have 9; 2 Screw, have 0 (short)"* — both columns, as asked. **THE ITEM-SHOP POOL DUMP PROVED NOTHING AND I SHOULD HAVE SEEN WHY BEFORE SHIPPING IT:** it fired ONCE, on the first poll after the mode change, and the modules do not exist yet then. **The same log proves it — dump at 18:43:07 shows an empty pool; the junk module is found at 18:43:08.** A false negative in both directions. It now samples the STEADY state: every 2 s while in shop mode with no module found, up to 8 dumps a visit, logging **both list heads** (`0x01D76B48` and `0x01D76ACC`) and each slot's `+0x2C/+0x45/+0x46/+0x47`. **AND A SECOND IDENTIFICATION:** identity-by-update-fn **provably does not hold in this engine** — SUBMENU_AUDIT.md §9 records the Item MENU module carrying `upd=0x605D8200` in that very field, so betting the shop on `+0x08` alone was the same assumption in a new place. `ShopFindByFields()` demands THREE creator-set things: `+0x2C == 0x01CFE79C` (inventory base, `0x004EDAE8`), `+0x47 == 2 or 25` (page count — 16 stock rows or 198 inventory rows over 8/page), and a state in range; a non-shop satisfying all three would be a coincidence in three independent fields. When it fires the log says so and prints the real `+0x08`. **Verified: probe clobbers `+0x08` and asserts the shop is still found, and that a wrong page count or wrong inventory pointer is refused. All fourteen gates.** **BAT: the ITEM SHOP. Three outcomes worth reporting — (a) it just talks; (b) `[SHOP] item shop found by FIELDS` appears, which tells us `+0x08` is clobbered; (c) still silent, in which case send the `[SHOP-POOL]` lines — there will be up to 8 dumps now, taken while the shop is actually on screen.**

**Prior (v0.34.3 — #92: A STUB IS A SECOND IMPLEMENTATION NOTHING CHECKS).** v0.34.2 failed to build — `menu_tts_shop.inl(551): error C2660: MenuDialogCompose does not take 2 arguments`. I hand-wrote a probe stub `std::string MenuDialogCompose(const MenuDialogRaw&, int)` against the real `bool MenuDialogCompose(int cursor, char* out, size_t n)`, and **the probe then passed every assertion against a function that does not exist.** Call site corrected; the probe now **includes the REAL `menu_dialog.inl`** and fills the window's three globals the way `0x004C2A20` does, so the confirmation test exercises the actual reader (padding collapse and all) instead of a fiction that agreed with it. **NEW GATE: `tests/lint_stub.py`** — if a probe defines a free function whose name is also defined in `src/`, and the probe does not include the file that really defines it, the **parameter count must match**. Thin stubs that mirror a signature pass; invented ones fail. Verified by re-introducing the exact v0.34.2 stub, which it rejects. **SECOND BUILD A MISMATCHED STUB HAS COST** — the first was the `FF8TextDecode::Decode` stub taking glyph indices where the real one takes stream bytes, and *that* one failed nothing: it tested the wrong decoder and reported OK. **Gate list is now lint_seh + lint_xtu + lint_stub + 11 menu_* probes + menu_sim + button_map_rescue_test.** No behaviour change; v0.34.2's four fixes intact. **BAT: as for v0.34.2 — the ITEM SHOP first (if silent, send the ten `[SHOP-POOL]` lines), then a JUNK SHOP for `/` with both material columns, the strength/hit change on a row, and the Yes/No remodel dialog.**

**Prior (v0.34.2 — #92: BOTH COLUMNS, THE CONFIRMATION, THE STATS, AND A POOL SCAN FOR THE ITEM SHOP).** Weapon names read correctly now (Revolver / Metal Knuckle / Chain Whip / Shear Trigger / Maverick / Valkyrie / Pinwheel / Flail). Aaron's screenshots showed three things the mod was not saying and one it still cannot reach. **(1) BOTH MATERIAL COLUMNS** — the panel draws `M-Stone Piece 2 100` = **required AND held**, and v0.34.1 spoke only the requirement, the number that decides nothing on its own. `/` now reads *"Needs 2 M-Stone Piece, have 100; 1 Bomb Fragment, have 0 (short)"*, counted from the INVENTORY rather than `0x01D8D058` — that table is filled only by the ITEM shop's creator, so in a Junk Shop it is whatever the last item shop left. **(2) THE CONFIRMATION DIALOG** *"Remodel Rinoa's weapon to 'Valkyrie' OK?"* — state `0x0B` (`0x004EADA6`), text built at `0x004EACDD` from template `0x3B`, opened through **the same `0x004C2B10` every other confirmation uses**, so `menu_dialog.inl` already read it and just had no caller here. Cursor `+0x42` (0=Yes, 1=No) — said, not assumed. **4th of that window's 20 call sites now wired.** **(3) THE STAT COMPARISON** the bottom panel draws (`Str 28 ▲ 31`, `Hit 99% ▲ 101%`): Str `[0x01D8CB84 + idx*4]`, Hit `byte [0x01CF7407 + idx*12]` (`0x004EB843`) — without it there is no way to judge whether an upgrade is worth the gil. Price also now scaled by `+0x30` (1000, **750 with Haggle**, `0x004EAE2B`); v0.34.1 was 25% high for a player with the ability. **(4) THE ITEM SHOP IS STILL NOT FOUND** — two BATs, a screenshot of "Balamb Shop" with a full stock list, zero output. **THE MRU-LIST WALK IS GONE:** it stopped at the first entry outside the pool, right for the tail sentinel and wrong for anything relinked — `0x004BE5B0` is a SECOND allocator threading modules onto `0x01D76ACC`, invisible from `0x01D76B48`. **The pool is ten fixed slots and reading all ten cannot miss a module that exists.** If it still fails, `[SHOP-POOL]` dumps all ten slots (in-use, state, upd, draw) once per entry into shop mode — **reasoning from the disassembly has been wrong about this twice, so the next BAT gets evidence instead of a third guess.** **Verified: all thirteen gates.** **BAT: the ITEM SHOP first — if it is still silent, grep `[SHOP-POOL]` and send me those ten lines, that settles it. Then a JUNK SHOP: `/` must give both material columns, a row must read the strength/hit change, and confirming a remodel must announce the Yes/No dialog.**

**Prior (v0.34.1 — #92: THE ITEM SHOP DECLINED ITSELF, AND THE WEAPON NAME IS NOT WHERE THE MODULE POINTS).** **The JUNK SHOP worked first try:** messages ("Welcome!  Who needs to remodel?" / "Come back soon"), the character picker resolving mask `0x002B` → Squall/Zell/Quistis/Selphie — **bits 0,1,3,5, so a PACKED list would have said "Irvine" for Quistis**, which is exactly what the set-bit picker exists to prevent — plus prices, materials, "already equipped" and "not available yet", all correct. Two defects. **(1) THE ITEM SHOP PRODUCED NOTHING** (no `[SHOP]` line at all, though the log shows shop mode entered a second time). `+0x46` (buy/sell) is written by the top-menu CONFIRM at `0x004EC2E1` and **by nothing else — the creator never touches it** — so through states 0..3, the whole opening of the shop, it holds whatever the LAST shop left there, and v0.34.0 required it to be 0 or 1 before it would accept the view. **A byte the engine has not written yet is not a reason to call the module unreadable.** Clamped now, and read only on the screens the engine writes it for. **(2) EVERY WEAPON READ "Unknown weapon".** The module puts `mwepon.msg base + record[0]` in `+0x20` (`0x004EAB3E`) — **but mwepon.msg is 68 bytes of nothing but spaces and terminators in this release** (34 empty strings). The DRAW fn does not use it either: `0x004EB590` calls `0x0047EBA0(recIdx)` = `0x01CF3E48 + [0x01CF3ED8] + u16[0x01CF7400 + idx*12]`, and that is what the mod reproduces — still the game's own text, just not the empty file the module pointed at. (Record index == weapon index; the engine's own "already equipped" test compares them directly at `0x004EAB94`.) Probe now enters with a garbage `+0x46` and asserts the weapon is named. **Verified: all thirteen gates.** **BAT: the ITEM SHOP specifically — top row, buy list, buy something, switch to Sell (row AND price must change), sell something, overspend for the message, `/` on a row. Then one junk-shop row to confirm the weapon NAMES itself now. Grep `[SHOP]`.**

**Prior (v0.34.0 — #92: ITEM SHOPS AND JUNK SHOPS).** Aaron: *"let's go ahead and implement support for junk shops and item shops."* Both were **entirely silent** — every purchase, sale and weapon remodel in the game was done blind. **THERE ARE TWO MODULES, NOT ONE:** `0x004EDA40` asks `0x004B2D70` which shop this is, looks it up in `0x00B88918` (0..20 → themselves, 21..31 → 0, **32..39 → 21**), and type `0x15` calls `0x004EA4D0` = **the JUNK SHOP, its own module** (update `0x004EA890`), sharing no state machine, layout or cursor with the item shop (creator `0x004EBBA0`, update `0x004EBE40`). **ITEM SHOP:** stock `0x01D8D038` (16 × {id, avail}, **compacted** so every listed row is buyable), owned-by-id `0x01D8D058`, and the engine PRE-COMPUTES both prices — buy `0x01D8CD18` (base×10, ×7.5 with Haggle), sell `0x01D8D120` ((base×factor)/2, ×3/4 with Sell-High) — so the mod READS them rather than re-deriving a formula that can drift. **BUY AND SELL KEEP SEPARATE CURSORS** (`+0x3C`/`+0x3E`, indexed by mode at `0x004EBF9D`) — reading one in the other names a row from the wrong list, **the fifth screen in this project with that exact shape**, so the probe switches modes with the cursors at different values and asserts the sell row + sell price. **JUNK SHOP:** mwepon record = {u16 msg offset, `+3` price/10, `+4..+B` four {item, count} materials}; `0x004EA770` builds the rows at `0x01D8CC08` — low 6 bits = record, **`0x80` = buildable NOW** (engine has already checked gil AND all four materials), `0x40` = seen. Its character picker indexes **SET BITS of a mask** (`0x004ABC40`) — the refine screen's shape — so it reuses `AbilCharAtPickerRow()` instead of a second copy of a thing already got wrong once. **NOTHING HERE IS A TRANSLATION:** every label, message, item description and weapon name is the game's own text via `0x004BD630` (`[0x00B86D30] + 0x2E000`, group 3); the top row is strings 52/53/54, terminated by `0xFFFF` at index 3 of `0x00B88910`, so the COUNT and ORDER come from the exe. `/` reads the item description, or the weapon's materials. **`PollShops()` runs OUTSIDE both mode blocks — a shop opens from the FIELD**, so a mode-6 gate would have left it silent where it is used; `/` is bound again there for the same reason. Gil rides on the FIRST utterance ("Shop, 1000 gil. Buy") — a separate entry line is interrupted by the row already under the cursor (the v0.33.3 lesson). **`tests/menu_shop_compile.cpp` DRIVES `PollShops()`** through mapped real addresses and asserts what was SPOKEN across both shops. **Verified: all thirteen gates (`lint_seh` 97, `lint_xtu` 179).** New: `src/menu_shop_model.inl`, `src/menu_tts_shop.inl`, `tests/menu_shop_compile.cpp`, `docs/SHOP_FINDINGS.md`. **BAT: an ITEM SHOP — top row (Buy/Sell/Exit), scroll the buy list, buy something, switch to Sell and confirm the row and price change, sell something, try to overspend for the message, press `/` on a row. Then a JUNK SHOP — the character picker, the weapon list, `/` for the materials, and one actual remodel. Grep `[SHOP]`.**

**Prior (v0.33.4 — #91: ONE POLL LATER IS STILL OVER THE TOP OF IT).** **The outcome line landed on all three refines with every number right:** *"Refined 3 Tent into 30 Curaga, 30 total"* (5 Tents → 2), *"Refined 1 Dead Spirit into 2 Death Stone, 2 total"*, *"Refined 35 Cure into 7 Cura, 78 total"* (71 held + 7) — item output, magic output and magic-from-magic all measured correctly. **BUT ON SUB-MODE 0 IT IS STILL SPOKEN OVER.** v0.33.3 returned from the poll the outcome happened in, which is only half of it: a sub-mode 0 refine lands back on the **RECIPIENT PICKER**, whose announcer the popup had re-armed on its way past, so one poll later and in the same second the log shows `"Refined 3 Tent into 30 Curaga, 30 total"` → `Refine recipient "Squall"` → `Refine recip stock stock=30`. Both interrupt, so the player hears a character's NAME where he should have heard what happened to his items — and the picker's own follow-up ("has 30 Curagas") is already inside the outcome line as "30 total". **Returning early only protects the poll you are in.** The outcome now seeds the picker dedupe + its stock follow-up to whatever screen the engine drops us on — **seeded, NOT disabled: moving the picker still announces**, and the probe asserts that too. The probe also now asserts **the poll AFTER the outcome is silent**, which is exactly the assertion v0.33.3 lacked and why a return-early fix looked complete. **Verified: all twelve gates.** **NOT YET BAT'D: the "Cancelled" path — all three refines were confirmed.** **BAT: complete one sub-mode 0 refine (T/I/L/Supt Mag-RF) and confirm the "Refined ..." line is NOT followed by a character name; then deliberately CANCEL out of a quantity popup and confirm you hear "Cancelled".**

**Prior (v0.33.3 — #91: v0.33.2 SHIPPED THE READER AND NOT THE LINE THAT ARMS IT).** The BAT log has **not one `Refine outcome` line**, and the GCW proves a refine completed (Death Stone appears in the item list at 17:04:15, one Dead Spirit lighter). **MY ERROR, PLAINLY:** the edit adding `PollRefineOutcome()` and the edit adding the block that ARMS it were one script; the script aborted on its second assertion before writing, and I re-applied only the half I had watched fail. `s_abilQtyArmed` was declared, reset and read — and never once set true. Everything compiled, every probe passed, the feature did nothing. **AND THE PROBE COULD NOT HAVE CAUGHT IT: it asserted `RefineOutcomeOf()`, a PURE function, while nothing called it.** **Testing a pure function is not testing that anything calls it** — the magazine-probe lesson (`FindItemModule()` stubbed to null) in new clothes. `menu_ability_compile` now **DRIVES `PollAbilityItemList()`** through real module memory and asserts what was SPOKEN: popup line → close → cancel → fires once. Its `GetTickCount()` stub had to advance, too; pinned at 0 the poll gate never opens and the test is vacuous. That immediately found a SECOND defect the pure test never could: **the outcome fell through to the source-row branch in the same poll, and both utterances interrupt** — "Refined 1 Dead Spirit into 2 Death Stone" would have been cut off by "Dead Spirit, 1" and the player would hear only the row he was already on. The poll now returns when the outcome speaks, and the probe asserts the utterance COUNT.
