Rest of the BAT clean: totals right on every popup (72/73/74/75 Cura against 71 held, 39 Fira against 38), no pre-write zero, zero errors. **Verified: all twelve gates.** **BAT: complete a refine (Tool-RF on a spare item, and one Mag-RF) — "Refined N X into M Y, T total" must land AND NOT be cut off by a row name; then cancel one and hear "Cancelled". Grep `Refine outcome` — if it is empty again, the line is still not being reached.**

**Prior (v0.33.2 — #91: THE REFINE ITSELF SAID NOTHING).** v0.33.1 BAT **clean on both counts** — no pre-write "Number to refine 0" line anywhere, and every popup carried the resulting stock: *"1 of 16, makes 1 Cura for 72 total, 79 Cure left"* (71 held + 1, exactly the screen), *"1 of 2, makes 2 Death Stone for 2 total"* (item output), *"5 of 5, makes 50 Curaga for 50 total, 0 Tent left"* (a result held from none). Sub-mode 2's picker logs as "Refine source character". **WHAT IT SHOWED BY OMISSION: Aaron backed out of EVERY refine, and had he confirmed one the mod would have said NOTHING.** State `0x2A` performs the refine in ONE FRAME and jumps straight out (`0x004D7DEA`), so an 80 ms poller cannot count on ever seeing it — **and cancel and confirm both clear `+0x51` and both land back on a list with the cursor unmoved**, so neither path re-announces the row. **The one action on this screen that changes the save was the one action with no feedback at all.** Told apart now by MEASUREMENT, not inference: a confirm consumes exactly `sourcePer * count` of the source, a cancel consumes nothing. The popup stages what it would do; the close reads the source back and says **"Refined 5 Tent into 50 Curaga, 50 total"** or **"Cancelled"**. **Any pair that does not add up says NOTHING** — a partial drop, a count going UP, an unreadable before/after, a zero consumed — because announcing a refine that did not happen is the same class of error as everything else in this thread, and the probe asserts every silent case. **Verified: all twelve gates clean.** **BAT: actually COMPLETE a refine — Tool-RF on a spare item, and one Mag-RF — and confirm the "Refined N X into M Y, T total" line lands and its numbers match. Then cancel one deliberately and confirm it says "Cancelled". Grep `Refine outcome`; an `unknown` line there means the measurement disagreed and wants looking at.**

**Prior (v0.33.1 — #91: THE POPUP'S THIRD ROW IS THE RESULTING STOCK).** v0.33.0 BAT: **both defects fixed and confirmed in the log.** Mid Mag-RF reads its character picker (Squall..Selphie) and then that character's MAGIC — "Cure, 84" / "Esuna, 4" / "Protect, 94" / "Fire, 69" — and the quantity popup speaks on every arrow with the recipe's real per-unit math (5 Cure → 1 Cura; 1 Dead Spirit → 2 Death Stone). The screenshots then showed two things. **(1) THE THIRD ROW IS THE RESULTING STOCK, NOT THE AMOUNT MADE.** The screen reads `Cure 4 / Number to refine 16 / **Cura 87**` while sixteen are being made — 87 because Squall already held 71; likewise "Fira 51" for thirteen made and "Death Stone 4" for four made from none. Speaking only the produced count is accurate and **hides the 100 cap, which is exactly where refine materials get thrown away**. Both grant paths clamp at `0x64` (magic `0x004C2CCC`, items `0x0047ED54`), so the line is now *"16 of 16, makes 16 Cura for 87 total, 4 Cure left"*, with the stock found by id across the character's 32 slots (`0x004C2C8D`) or the 198 inventory slots (`0x0047ED18`). **(2) THE PRE-WRITE FRAME.** State `0x28` is where the engine WRITES `+0x4C` and `+0x4F`, and polling inside it caught the zeroes: *"Number to refine 0, makes 0 Death Stone, 2 Dead Spirit left"* one beat before the real *"1 of 2"*. `0x28` still counts as the popup (so no source row is announced over it) but nothing is spoken until `RefineQtyReady()`. **Also: the sub-mode 2 picker no longer logs as "Refine recipient" — it is the SOURCE character, and calling it the recipient is how this screen got misread in the first place.** **Verified: all twelve gates clean; the stock lookup asserted through the real addresses with the screenshot's own numbers as the fixture.** **BAT: Mid Mag-RF → any spell → the popup must say "... for N total" and the N must match the screen's third row; then Tool-RF, where the FIRST line must be "Number to refine 1 of 2", never a 0.**

**Prior (v0.33.0 — #91: THE REFINE SCREEN HAS FIVE FLOWS, NOT ONE).** Aaron BAT'd v0.32.1 and sent two screenshots. **Mid Mag-RF showed a list of CHARACTERS and the mod read out the ITEM INVENTORY** — "Potion, 34", "Phoenix Down, 3", "Elixir, 6" over a screen whose only panel is Squall/Zell/Irvine/Quistis/Rinoa/Selphie with their HP; the ability's own help line three lines earlier in the same log said *"Refine Mid-Level Magic from other Magic."* **And Tool-RF's quantity popup said NOTHING**, though every word of it sits in the GCW. **ONE ROOT CAUSE: the flow was modelled as sub-mode 0's flow.** Module `+0x45` (the ability descriptor's byte +5, copied at `0x004D71DF`) is a jump-table index with FIVE values, and the engine's own tables say what each is — source base `0x004D8DA8`, post-source destination `0x004D8C04`, grant/consume `0x004D8B7C`: **0 = item→magic (recipient picker) · 1 = item→item · 2 = A CHARACTER'S MAGIC→magic (`0x01CFE0F8 + charId*152`, THIRTY-TWO slots, the `0x20` loop at `0x004D7BEE`) · 3 = item→item · 4 = the CARD LIST (`0x01D8B064`)→item.** The old gate was `+0x53 == 0`, which the engine clears at state `0x1A` — **the first state of the recipient picker, and only sub-mode 0 enters it.** Tool-RF goes `0x14 → 0x28` straight to the popup, Mid Mag-RF goes `0x14 → 0x1D` straight to a magic grid, so on both the byte stayed `0xFF` and the item-list branch ran over a screen that was not an item list. **The flow now runs off the state machine and the engine's own popup flag `+0x51`**, with every read taken off `AbilRefineBase()` at an offset that came from an instruction. Sub-mode 2's two screens are handled as what they are: a character picker on `+0x49` that runs BEFORE the source list exists, then that character's 32 magic slots on `+0x4A`. **The quantity popup reads the RECIPE ENTRY** the engine multiplies at state `0x2A` (+2 result per unit, +5 source id, +6 source per unit, +7 result id), so the three numbers spoken are the three the screen draws — and correct for a recipe that eats more than one per unit. **AND CARD MOD IS NAMED:** the creator at `0x004D7344` builds the list itself as `{cardId + 1, count}`, so v0.32.0's "Card 1" suppression is gone. **DELETED: the seven `pMenuStateA` offsets that drove the old flow.** All real bytes (`pMenuStateA + 0x296` IS the slot-3 module base) — but `+0x2E7` was read as "1 = quantity screen" when it is the NUMBER TO REFINE, which merely starts at 1, and `+0x2E9` as "past the item list" when it is sub-mode 0's marker. **Fifth screen in this menu where a byte correlated for a reason nobody wrote down.** New: `src/menu_refine_model.inl` (pure, testable). **Verified: `menu_ability_compile` OK (0 bad) — five sub-modes, the phase decision across every state 0x00..0x2C, the quantity arithmetic, and all three source arrays through their REAL addresses; the probe also had `SAVEMAP_BASE = 0` and a three-name spell stub until v0.32.1, so it now uses the real savemap and the real tables. All other probes, `lint_seh` (96), `lint_xtu` (177) OK.** **BAT: (1) Mid Mag-RF — the first screen must read CHARACTERS, then that character's SPELLS, not items. (2) Tool-RF all the way to the quantity popup — it must speak "Number to refine 1 of N, makes 30 Shell Stone, K Force Armlet left" and track every arrow. (3) a Mag-RF for regression. Grep `Refine source` and `Refine quantity` — both lines now carry the sub-mode.**

**Prior (v0.32.1 — #91: THE "/" PREVIEW RAN PAST ITS END ON ITEM-OUTPUT REFINES).** The v0.32.0 BAT proved the flow itself — item names, ability ids (100 = L Mag-RF, 108 = Tool-RF), recipient, quantity, the Refinable tag all correct — and then produced exactly the failure v0.32.0's own BAT instruction had named as the risk: `Refine info (/): "1 will refine into 30 Shell StonesCoral FragmentBetrayal SwordDead SpiritHeroForce Armlet"` and `"100 will refine into 1 Dark MatterZombie PowderPet Pals Vol.2Girl Next DoorSoft..."`. **`ParseRefinePreview` bounded the sentence with the PARTY NAMES**, which held only because a magic-output refine draws the recipient panel next. **AN ITEM-OUTPUT REFINE HAS NO RECIPIENT** — no party name is ever drawn, no marker was ever found, `e` stayed at `dec.size()`, and the whole drawn source list was spoken as part of the result. **THE CUT IS NOW THE END OF THE RESULT'S OWN NAME:** a refine result is always a named thing (an item on the item-output recipes, a spell on the magic ones), so `RefineResultEnd()` takes the LONGEST match across the item table and the spell table at the position just past `"into <count> "`, plural `s` included — independent of what the screen draws next. The party-panel bound stays as fallback, joined by a new source-row bound (the current page of eleven from `(cursor/11)*11`, read from the savemap inventory) for a result neither table knows. **AND THE PROBE COULD NOT HAVE CAUGHT THIS:** `tests/menu_ability_compile.cpp` had `SAVEMAP_BASE = 0`, `ITEM_INVENTORY_OFFSET = 0` and a three-name spell stub, so the inventory read mapped page zero and the spell match had nothing to match — **the same shape as the magazine probe that stubbed `FindItemModule()` to null.** It now uses the real savemap address and the real tables, with the two failing BAT strings as verbatim fixtures plus an unknown-result case that keeps the fallback live. **Verified: `menu_ability_compile` OK (0 bad); all ten `menu_*` probes, `menu_sim`, `button_map_rescue_test`, `lint_seh` (96), `lint_xtu` (176) OK.** **BAT: press `/` on Tool-RF (and Ammo-RF if reachable) — the preview must stop at the result and NOT recite the source list — then the same on a Mag-RF to confirm no regression, and `/` on the quantity popup, which draws fewer rows.**

**Prior (v0.32.0 — #91: THE ABILITY SUBMENU, READ OUT OF THE EXE).** Aaron cannot BAT 24 abilities he does not own, so the screen was read instead. **THE TWENTY-FOUR MENU ABILITIES DO NOT ALL GO TO ONE SCREEN.** `0x004E7990` looks the picked ability up in the descriptor table at `0x01CF7F28 + id*8` and branches on the **type byte at +5**: `0xFF` not selectable · **`0x81` -> a MODAL MESSAGE** ("can't use that here", dispatch 12, fn `0x004EA890`) — **silent** · **`0x80` -> the SHOP** (Call Shop, Junk Shop; `[0x01D8CB6D]` decides open vs refuse-with-a-beep) — **no reader at all** · else -> `[esi+0x3D]=id`, `[esi+0x3C]=0x13`, state `0x17`, push dispatch 19 = **THE REFINE SCREEN, WHICH IS A MODULE OF ITS OWN** (creator `0x004D7180`, update `0x004D7410`, draw `0x004D90E0`). **This file has been driving that module through pool slot 3 for four builds and nowhere said so.** Its fields now have provenance: `+0x45` sub-mode (jump table `0x004D8B40`), `+0x48` character id (`0x004D7589` via `0x004AD030`/`0x004ABC40`), **`+0x49` source-list cursor — ABSOLUTE, drawn row = cursor%11 (`0x004D75A7`)**, `+0x4A` packed character-picker cursor — exactly the bytes read as pMenuStateA+0x2DE/+0x2DF/+0x2E0. **THE OFFSETS WERE RIGHT AND THE REASON WAS NEVER WRITTEN DOWN — which is precisely how the GF "two cursors" stayed wrong for eight builds: a correct-looking number with no derivation behind it.** Module now found **walk first, state-checked slot-3 alias second**. **AND CARD MOD (115) REFINES FROM CARDS, NOT ITEMS:** every other menu ability sources the item inventory and this file names rows with `GetItemName()`, so on Card Mod that is a confident wrong name on EVERY row. **The name is SUPPRESSED rather than faked** ("Card 1"...), because the card list's order and cursor mapping cannot be checked against any save Aaron has. **WHAT HE CAN TEST, read out of `slot2_save30.ff8` (28h01m, 164 saves): FIVE of twenty-four — Quezacotl T Mag-RF, Shiva I Mag-RF, Siren L Mag-RF + Tool-RF, Leviathan Supt Mag-RF. Four are magic-refines; TOOL-RF IS THE ONLY ITEM-OUTPUT RECIPE and the preview parser was written against "N will refine into M <Magic>" — that is the one to watch.** Full findings incl. the gaps left open: `docs/ABILITY_MENU_FINDINGS.md`. **Verified: `menu_ability_compile` OK (0 bad) — refine module found by walk in all ten slots; the in-use state-checked slot-3 alias answering when the walk cannot; a state past `0x2C` and a free slot refused; cyclic/misaligned lists terminating; all 23 item-sourced abilities accepted with Card Mod excluded and an out-of-range id refused. All other probes, `lint_seh` (95), `lint_xtu` (176) OK; every file inside the 80 KB guard.** **BAT: Ability -> each of the five; walk the source list and let the cursor SETTLE so the Refinable tag fires; then take TOOL-RF all the way through a refine (character picker + quantity). Grep `Refine item` — it carries the ability id and the resolved base now.**

**Prior (v0.31.1 — #90: THE "ITEMS FOR REMODELING" PANEL).** v0.31.0 read the heading (text block 2 of the record) and then stopped — **the v0.28.1 empty-page failure again: a heading followed by silence is indistinguishable from the mod having broken**, and on a Weapons Monthly that list is the practical point of the page. Not in the mmag record; not usably in the GCW buffer (which holds the item names mixed with page-slide leftovers). **It is keyed off the record's WEAPON ID at +0x18** (records 0..27 carry a permutation of 0..27, 0xFF elsewhere) into **`[0x01D2BB58]` -> mwepon.bin, 33 records of 12 bytes**, loaded by the Item creator at `0x004F8023` right beside mmag.bin, with **four {u8 itemId, u8 count} pairs at +0x04 ending at the first zero count**. **VERIFIED AGAINST AARON'S OWN SCREENSHOT: the Maverick is mmag record 9, weapon id 8, and mwepon record 8 reads {155 x1, 127 x1} = Dragon Fin 1, Spider Web 1 — the two lines on that page, in that order.** Read as "Dragon Fin, 1. Spider Web, 1." per the v0.28.1 column rule, because that is what the panel is: a label column and a number column. Read LIVE from the loaded table, so the page states what the Junk Shop actually wants rather than what a baked copy said. A page whose weapon id is 0xFF gets no list rather than borrowing the previous weapon's; a one-item weapon reads one rather than running to the end of its four slots. **Verified: `menu_tutorial_compile` OK (0 bad) — the fixture plants a real mwepon-shaped table and requires both Maverick items with counts IN THE PANEL'S ORDER, a one-item weapon to read exactly one with no "Unknown item" spill, and a page with no weapon id to get no list. All other probes, `lint_seh` (95), `lint_xtu` (176) OK; every file inside the 80 KB guard.** **BAT: any Weapons Monthly page — after "Items for remodeling" you should now hear the items and their counts. Grep `item magazine`.**

**Prior (v0.31.0 — #90: WHAT THE WEAPONS MONTHLY PICTURES SHOW). BAT PASSED — all 28 plates read, keyed correctly (rec 8 Shear Trigger / 9 Maverick / 10 Pinwheel / 11 Valiant / 12 Cutting Trigger / 13 Valkyrie / 14 Ulysses / 15 Slaying Tail, each matching the prose read immediately before it), and the pages themselves now read end to end.** **AND THE POOL-WALK MYSTERY IS SETTLED BY THE ONE-SHOT DUMP:** `slot 2 @01D76CB8 inUse=1 state=81 upd=0x605D8200` — the list is intact (head -> slot2 -> slot1 -> slot0 -> 01D76AC8 tail sentinel), slots 0 and 1 carry sane update fns, and **slot 2 IS the Item module (its state word reads 81 = the magazine opening state) while the dword where its update function should sit is not a code address at all.** So `FindItemModule()` cannot match it and no hardening of the walk ever could. **For the Item screen, identity-by-update-fn is not a stricter test than the slot alias — it is a test that cannot pass**, and the state-verified alias is the CORRECT identification there rather than a fallback. What overwrites +0x08 is not established (the Item update fn contains no write to `[reg+0x08]`); it is recorded in `docs/SUBMENU_AUDIT.md` section 9 rather than guessed at. **THE GENERAL LESSON: "find the module by its update function" was adopted in v0.22.x as the safe alternative to fixed pool slots, and it is — but it is NOT UNIVERSAL. An identification is only as good as the field it reads, and the right shape is TWO identifications that must both agree with something the caller already knows.**  Aaron asked for the plates to be pulled, described, and put on `/`; and then: *"Make sure to use the text on the page to help you create accurate descriptions. e.g. Quistis' Slaying Tail might look like a tail, but it is a whip, and that is clear if you read the text."* **ALL 28 PLATES EXTRACTED, VIEWED BESIDE THEIR OWN PAGE PROSE, AND DESCRIBED** (`src/menu_magazine_art.inl`). **The chain, all checkable:** `menu.fs/.fi` — **the .fi triple is {size, offset, compression}, NOT {offset, size, ...}**; read the other way it yields plausible multi-megabyte files that decode into nothing (that cost me a detour). `mmag.bin` = 69 x 68-byte records, 0..27 = Weapons Monthly four to an issue, `+0x17` sheet / `+0x18` weapon id / `+0x34` text blocks. `magNN.TEX` = 256x256 8bpp, one RGBA palette at 0xEC (**RGBA not BGRA — as BGRA every weapon comes out cyan**), pixels at 0x4EC, sheet = rec/4, cell = rec%4 clockwise from top-left. `mngrp` section **0x57** = 111 strings, the page prose, decoded with the mod's own glyph table. **THE MAPPING IS VERIFIED, NOT ASSUMED: record 9 decodes to "With the Maverick... black leather... metal plates on the knuckles", and sheet 2 cell 1 is near-black gloves with pale knuckle studs — which is ALSO the page in Aaron's own v0.30.1 screenshot.** Every page cross-checked the same way (the saw-toothed ring is on the page that says "similar to a circular saw"; the Flame Saber's red edge is on the page that says it "got its name from its red blade"). **AND THE THREE A SHAPE ALONE WOULD MISREAD — Strange Vision, Slaying Tail, Red Scorpion — ARE ALL NAMED AS WHIPS, on their own prose's authority, which is exactly the point Aaron made.** Colour is described only where sprite and prose agree; the art is dithered 8-bit and precise shades are not claimed. `/` is claimed BEFORE the other `/` readers (a magazine covers the screen; nothing under it has a help bar) and says "No picture description for this page" on the other magazines rather than borrowing one. **Verified: `menu_tutorial_compile` OK (0 bad) — the fixture now uses the REAL record numbers (April = 8..11, Maverick = 9), so the art test checks the KEYING not just the plumbing: record 9 must say "Maverick" and describe gloves; all 28 entries must exist, be substantial, and be DISTINCT; the three whips must be called whips; a plateless page must say so; off the magazine the key must fall through. All other probes, `lint_seh` (95), `lint_xtu` (176) OK; every file inside the 80 KB guard.** **BAT: open a Weapons Monthly, any page, press `/`. Then try `/` on a Pet Pals — it should say there is no picture description. Grep `magazine art`.**

**Prior (v0.30.2 — #89: I FIXED THE IDENTIFICATION ONE FUNCTION OVER AND LEFT THE MAGAZINE ON THE BROKEN ONE).** **THE GF TARGET LIST WORKS** — `Use target entered [slot2 (walk found nothing)]: GF slot 0 avail=1 mask=0x07FF0000 -> "Use on Quezacotl"`, all eleven GFs by name, plus `slot 11 avail=0 -> "Doomtrain, not available"`. **AND THAT SAME LINE IS THE PROOF THE MAGAZINE COULD NEVER HAVE WORKED:** `walk found nothing` means `FindItemModule()` returns null, and **`PollItemMagazine` asked the RAW WALK on its first line and returned before reaching any log statement** — which is why there was no `item magazine` line at all, not even the failure one. I wrote the verified identification for the target reader in the same build and did not apply it here. Both now go through `ItemModuleBaseInStates(lo, hi, &how)`: the walk when it finds a module whose state is in range, else the `pMenuStateA+0x21E` alias **with the same state check**, so the alias stays a TEST and not the fixed-slot assumption this audit exists to remove. **AND THE DECODE HAD NEVER BEEN EXECUTED BY ANYTHING — two builds shipped a magazine reader that no probe ran**, because `menu_tutorial_compile` stubbed the Item module to null: every line of it was dead in the test as well as in the game. **THAT is the lesson: a probe that stubs the thing under test to null is not a test, it is a compile.** It now plants the real layout (range `[0x01D2BB2C]+id*4` +2/+3, record `[0x01D2BB6C]+page*68`, block list at record +0x34, strings at `[0x00B86D30]+0x1F000`) **in the game's own text encoding** and requires the headline, the body prose AND the page counter to come out; the 0x51..0x54 openers to stay silent; a slide to re-speak exactly once; the last page to say so; and an out-of-range page to be refused rather than read out of a neighbouring magazine's records. **WHY THE POOL WALK FAILS IS STILL OPEN** — a one-shot pool dump now fires the first time the alias has to answer (slot by slot: inUse, state, update fn, next, prev), so the next log settles it instead of another guess. **Nothing depends on the answer: the alias is verified, so both readers are correct either way.** Still outstanding and named: the "Items for remodeling" panel. **Verified: `menu_tutorial_compile` OK (0 bad) with the new magazine section; `menu_item_compile` OK (0 bad) with the identification tests; all other probes, `lint_seh` (95), `lint_xtu` (175) OK; every file inside the 80 KB guard.** **BAT: read a Weapons Monthly and turn its pages. Grep `item magazine` (the open line carries the identification, the id, the page range and both data pointers) and `pool dump`, which appears once.**

**Prior (v0.30.1 — #89: THE BAT REFUTED BOTH HALVES OF v0.30.0, AND SAID SO IN ITS OWN LOG).** **(1) THE TARGET READ FAILED ON EVERY ENTRY** — "engine read failed" three times, while the SCREENSHOT taken at that moment shows eleven GFs drawn in two columns with the cursor on Alexander, **slot 10, comfortably inside the bounds check. The module was there and the pool walk did not find it.** The fix is NOT to trust a slot instead: `ItemTargetBase` accepts EITHER the walk or the `pMenuStateA+0x21E` slot-2 alias **and demands the same evidence from both — the module's own state word must equal the state the caller is already in.** That alias is the base the Item reader has used for its state byte all along (+0x22E is slot2+0x10), so checking +0x10 against the live state turns it from an assumption into a TEST, and the log names which one answered. The failure line now says WHY instead of covering three causes with one sentence — which is what made this cost a whole BAT to diagnose. **(2) THE ITEM MENU'S MAGAZINES ARE NOT A SEPARATE MODULE, AND THE LOG SAID SO BEFORE I SHIPPED:** `Item focus: 5 -> 81 -> 82 -> 83 -> 84 -> 85`, then `85 -> 86 -> 87 -> 85` and `85 -> 88 -> 89 -> 85` — **states 0x51..0x59 of the ITEM state machine, page slides included.** v0.30.0 hoisted the Tutorial viewer's gate on the assumption they shared its module. They do not, so that change could never have helped, and **I had the evidence to know it and did not look.** The DATA is the same shape elsewhere: `[0x01D2BB2C]+magId*4` = page range (+2 first, +3 last; magId at module +0x65), `[esi+0x52]` = page, `[0x01D2BB6C]+page*68` = record, record +0x34 = the same 4 x {u16 x, u8 y, u8 strIndex} block list, strings at `[0x00B86D30]+0x1F000` (0x004FD746) — byte for byte what v0.27.0 already decodes, so `MagCopyRawFrom` serves both. **WHY NOT SCRAPE THE SCREEN: the GCW buffer on that page held only "Steel PipeScrewDragon FinSpider Web to scroll/ to go to Item screen" — the remodel item names and the footer hint. THE HEADLINE AND THE PROSE ARE NOT IN IT. A scrape would have produced a confident fragment of the page and called it the page.** **STILL OUTSTANDING AND NAMED RATHER THAN IMPLIED: the "Items for remodeling" panel** (names + counts down the right) comes from the record's own fields, not the text blocks, and is not read yet — on a Weapons Monthly that panel is the point of the page. **Verified: `menu_item_compile` OK (0 bad) — now including THE IDENTIFICATION ITSELF: the walk answers when its state agrees; with no walk result the verified alias answers and names the same character; **a slot-2 state that DISAGREES is refused**, so the fallback cannot rot into the fixed-slot guess this audit exists to remove; and a stale walk hit falls through to a good alias rather than winning. Plus the v0.30.0 set (gapped mask {0,2,5}, both mask halves, unavailable rows, out-of-range refusals). All other probes + `lint_seh` (95) + `lint_xtu` (175) OK; every file inside the 80 KB guard.** **BAT: Item -> Use -> a G-Returner or G-Hi-Potion and walk the GF panel; then a Potion and walk the character column; then read a Weapons Monthly and turn its pages (headline and prose must speak, the remodeling list will not yet). Grep `Use target` — the line carries `[walk]` or `[slot2 ...]` now — and `item magazine`.**

**Prior (v0.30.0 — #89: THE ITEM SUBMENU — THE TARGET LIST, AND THE MAGAZINES).** Aaron: *"The list of characters / party members / GFs doesn't always seem to be accurate. Most of the time it is, but sometimes not."* **0x004F8600..0x004F86BF BUILDS THE TARGET LIST AS A 32-BIT MASK:** low half from `0x004AD030` (bit i = character i, savemap +0x94 odd at 0x01CFE17C stride 0x98 — **and narrowed to the three formation ids at 0x01CFE74C when [0x01CFE97A]&1**), high half from `0x004AD090` (bit 16+j = GF j, savemap +0x11 odd at 0x01CFDCB9 stride 0x44), `[esi+0x64]` says which half is on screen, `[esi+0x38]` holds the mask. **AND THE CURSOR AT +0x58 IS THE BIT INDEX, NOT A POSITION IN A PACKED LIST** — the draw code positions characters at `y = cur*13 + 0x42` in one column (0x004F8886) and GFs at `row = cur/2, col = cur&1` (0x004F889C), i.e. **from the SLOT. The screen leaves GAPS; it does not compact.** The mod collected an 0xFF-terminated roster from pMenuStateA+0x1DB, SORTED it, and indexed that with the cursor. **That agrees only while the set bits run 0,1,2,... with nothing missing — Aaron's party today, Squall 0 through Selphie 5 — which is exactly why it was right most of the time and why no test ever caught it.** Put a gap in the mask ({0,2,5} from a three-member formation, or Seifer/Edea over an empty slot) and **every name below the gap shifts by one**; and **GFs were never handled at all — a GF target list was read out as party members.** Now: walk the pool for update fn 0x004F81F0, read mask/cursor/kind, name by SLOT, name GFs from the savemap (so a renamed GF reads as the player named it), and say "not available" on a clear bit rather than skipping past it. **`GetPartyCharAtVisualPos` is DELETED, not kept as a fallback: a fallback that is wrong in exactly the cases the fix exists for is not a safety net, it is a second answer nobody asked for.** **THE MAGAZINES NOW READ FROM THE ITEM MENU.** The viewer was already there (v0.27.0) and its own comment said records 0..42 are the field magazines "which share this viewer but are not reached from here" — **they share it because it is ONE MODULE** (all three creators call `0x004BE540(0x004C9060, 0x004C9330)`; `0x004C99B0` is a generic setter over the table at [0x01D2BB3C]). **What kept them silent was the GATE, not the reader:** the poll only ran when the active-submenu byte held a dispatch id the TUTORIAL pushes. **A module found by its update fn is already positive identification** — the id gate only added a way to be wrong about where the viewer was opened from. Topic names stay limited to the three the mod can actually name; a field magazine's stored text carries its own heading. **SUBMON SPLIT to `src/menu_submon.inl`**, partly so the Item file can be probed and partly to write down what it cost: **it finds bytes that CORRELATE and cannot tell you what they MEAN**, and #88/#89 were largely spent undoing that difference (GF's "two cursors" were `cursor[page]`; this target cursor was a bit index; Switch's availability flag is still a hypothesis its own comment admits to). **An offset SUBMON suggests is a lead; the disassembly is the evidence.** **Verified: NEW `menu_item_compile` OK (0 bad) — module walk in all ten slots + three bad-list hazards; the contiguous-party case that always worked; **a GAPPED mask {0,2,5} where slot 2 must still be Irvine and slot 1 must be "Zell, not available"**; a mask starting at slot 6; both halves of the mask (a character bit must not make a GF available, or vice versa); "Use on" only on arrival; refusals on slot 16, a negative cursor, an empty pool. `menu_sim` / `menu_gf_compile` / `menu_save_compile` / `menu_ability_compile` / `menu_magic_compile` / `menu_junction_compile` / `menu_card_compile` / `menu_config_compile` / `menu_tutorial_compile` / `button_map_rescue_test` OK; `lint_seh` OK (95); `lint_xtu` OK (175); all files inside the 80 KB guard.** **BAT: Item -> Use -> a Potion and walk the WHOLE target column — every row must name the character at that position, and any row you cannot pick must SAY SO rather than going quiet or naming the next one down. Then a GF-targeted item (Pet Pals, or a GF-healing item): that list must read as GFs, by name. Then READ a Weapons Monthly or a Pet Pals from the Item menu and turn its pages. Grep `Use target` — the line carries the slot, the kind and the raw mask now.**

**Prior (v0.29.2 — #88: THE GF LEARN LIST, READ FROM THE ENGINE).** Aaron on v0.29.1: *"It did not seem to consistently announce abilities as I moved through the list on later GFs like Leviathan, Cerberus, and Pandemona."* **THE ABILITY-SCREEN DEFECT, ONE SCREEN OVER, AND 0x004D35ED SPELLS OUT EVERY PART OF IT:** `mov al,[esi+0x36]` (**PAGE**) · `movsx ecx,[eax+esi+0x39]` (**cursor row ON THAT PAGE — an ARRAY**) · `lea edx,[eax+eax*4]` / `lea eax,[eax+edx*2]` (**page*11**) · `add ecx,eax` (**ABSOLUTE**) · `cmp ecx,[0x01D7DAA0]` / `jge` · `mov al,[ecx*8+0x01D7D9F0]`. So **+0x36 = page (0/1, `sete al` at 0x004D2C1F), +0x39[page] = row, +0x3B[page] = that page's row count (0x004D2C2F clamps to it), 11 rows a page (`idiv 0xB` 0x004D33DC), 0x01D7D9F0 = THE WHOLE LIST stride 8 byte 0 = id, count 0x01D7DAA0 filled in one call at 0x004D2F13.** **THE MOD INSTEAD PARSED ABILITY NAMES OUT OF THE GCW DRAW BUFFER — which holds only the page on screen — AND INDEXED THAT WITH WHICHEVER OF pMenuStateA+0x257/+0x258 HAD JUST CHANGED. Those two bytes are module +0x39 and +0x3A: cursor[0] and cursor[1]. The right bytes, guessed, with no page byte to say which is live and no way to reach page 2 at all.** **AND THE FAILURE MODE WAS SILENCE:** when the parse under-read (Leviathan, three rows short, the v0.29.1 cause), the cursor ran past the parsed count into an "empty slot" branch that only speaks when the help text is blank — it was not blank, it was the run-on — **so nothing was said and nothing was logged.** Now: walk the pool for update fn 0x004D2A00, read page/row/count/id the way the engine does, treat past-the-count as an empty slot on **the engine's own bound (0x004D3602)** rather than on whether help happened to be blank, dedupe on **(GF, page, row)** so Q/R re-announces without a cursor move, and **LOG when the read fails instead of going quiet** — a reader that stops talking without leaving a trace is what made this cost a BAT and three screenshots. The GCW parse still proves the list is on screen and slices the help text; **it no longer decides which ability the cursor is on.** **CHECKED AND DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED: the Ability screen COMPOSES its own absolute index and stores it (`mov dl,0xB / imul dl / add al,bl / mov [esi+0x3a],al` at 0x004E7975), so reading +0x258 there was right. The two screens keep their cursors differently — which is exactly why one guess worked and the other did not, and why "it works on the GF I tested" was never evidence.** **Verified: `menu_gf_compile` OK (0 bad) — maps the pool and the ability list at the game's own addresses and exercises page*11+cursor[page] across BOTH pages of a 16-ability GF, proves the two cursors do not leak into each other, pins the empty-slot boundary at abs==count, keeps the single-page case, and requires a REFUSAL on every out-of-range field (page 2, row 12, count 25, no module); plus the module walk in all ten slots and the three bad-list hazards. `menu_sim` / `menu_save_compile` / `menu_ability_compile` / `menu_magic_compile` / `menu_junction_compile` / `menu_card_compile` / `menu_config_compile` / `menu_tutorial_compile` / `button_map_rescue_test` OK; `lint_seh` OK (95); `lint_xtu` OK (174); all files inside the 80 KB guard.** **BAT: Leviathan, Cerberus and Pandemona — walk the WHOLE Learn list on each, including onto PAGE 2 where there is one, and every row must speak. Then Q/R to switch GF WITHOUT moving the cursor; that must re-announce. Grep `GF learn page` — the line now carries page, row, absolute index and count, so a wrong row is visible in the log this time.**

**Prior (v0.29.1 — #88: WHAT THE v0.29.0 BAT SHOWED, INCLUDING ONE I BROKE).** **CONFIRMED WORKING: the Save overwrite prompt speaks** — `"Data exists. Overwrite?. No"`, once per cursor move, both labels read off the window, default correctly announced as No — **and Zell's Duel page is right** (cur=1 Punch Rush ... cur=5 Burning Rave, checked against the SCREENSHOT, not the log). Magic All transferred and announced. **(1) I BROKE THE ITEM REARRANGE IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION.** v0.29.0 armed the swap check inside the destination-cursor block, which runs **LATER IN THE SAME POLL** than `s_rearrangePrevFocus = focusState` a hundred lines above it — so the "am I arriving?" test was already false on the first frame, `s_swapSrcIdAtArm` never left 0xFFFF, and **every rearrange said "Cancelled", real swaps included. The same lie pointing the other way.** The arm moves into the focus-change block beside the battle path's, which was always correct because it was written there. **AND THE LOG COULD NOT HAVE SHOWN IT** — both outcomes printed "Rearrange swap completed", so the word the player actually heard was absent from a log I had already read. **A LOG THAT RECORDS THE TRANSITION AND NOT THE DECISION CANNOT FALSIFY THE DECISION.** Both paths now log the word they spoke. **(2) THE GF LEARN HELP SWALLOWED THE TOP OF THE LIST** — "/" on Leviathan read *"Raises Spr by 20%Mag-JSpr-JElem-Defx2"*. One cause, two symptoms: **THE GAME DROPS "-J" IN FRONT OF A MULTIPLIER** and its own draw buffer says so verbatim (`...SaveRaises Spr by 20%Mag-JSpr-JElem-Defx2MagicGFDraw...`), so id 14 renders "Elem-Defx2"; the right-to-left parse could not match it, stopped three rows short, and the help slice — everything between "Save" and the first row parsed — swallowed the rows it missed. Fixed in `NormalizeAbilityToGcw` (ids 14/15/16/17; **x2 witnessed, x4 and the ST- pair follow by construction and are NOT separately witnessed**), **plus `GFHelpSliceIsClean` for the CLASS rather than the case**: any slice holding an ability name glued to its neighbour — the parse's own test — is dropped. Prose that legitimately ends in an ability word survives because the game puts a space there ("Boost GF"). **It fails towards silence on purpose: dropping costs a line the row announce already gave, keeping it states something the screen does not say.** `ABILITY_NAMES` split to `src/menu_ability_names.inl` so the probe uses the real table. **(3) REPORTED, NOT FIXED — ZELL'S DUEL INPUTS.** The screenshot lists every Duel move with the buttons that perform it (Punch Rush = Down then X; Burning Rave is a five-step string) and **the mod says the name and the description and never the input, which on that page is the part that matters.** The glyphs are SPRITES and are absent from the GCW buffer entirely, so this needs the exe's Duel input table and its own build. Also still open: dimmed ability rows read the same as available ones (visible in both GF screenshots), and the shared confirmation window is read on 3 of its 20 call sites (`docs/SUBMENU_AUDIT.md`). **Verified: NEW `menu_gf_compile` OK (0 bad) — it replays BOTH real Learn screens out of the BAT log PASTED VERBATIM and requires eleven rows and a clean help line on each, plus the four multiplier substitutions, the bare "-J" forms untouched, and the guard accepting "Boost GF" while rejecting a glued list head; `menu_sim` / `menu_save_compile` / `menu_ability_compile` / `menu_magic_compile` / `menu_junction_compile` / `menu_card_compile` / `menu_config_compile` / `menu_tutorial_compile` / `button_map_rescue_test` OK; `lint_seh` OK (95); `lint_xtu` OK (174); all files inside the 80 KB guard.** **BAT: an item rearrange — one you COMPLETE and one you CANCEL, and they must differ. Then "/" on Leviathan's Learn list (expect "Raises Spr by 20%" and nothing after it). Grep `Rearrange armed` and `Rearrange 99->97`; the second line now names the word it spoke.**

**Prior (v0.29.0 — #88: THE SUBMENU AUDIT).** Aaron: *"go through all the main menu submenus — Junction through Save — and compare their implementation in the mod to their code in the exe... I know of two outstanding bugs in the submenus, but instead of telling you them I want to see if you can find and fix them yourself."* Every screen read against its state machine in the exe. **SIX FIXED, ALL PROVED AGAINST THE DISASSEMBLY.** **(1) THE SAVE OVERWRITE PROMPT WAS SILENT AND ITS CURSOR DEFAULTS TO NO** — `0x004E3B0E` sets phase `+0x48`=4 and cursor `+0x4F`=**1**, and state 0x37 at `0x004E4C1E` maps cursor 0 to the write and anything else to abandon, **so a blind player pressed Confirm on an occupied block, heard nothing, pressed Confirm again on the default, and was returned to the block list BELIEVING HE HAD SAVED. There is no later symptom and nothing about it is recoverable.** **(2) NEW `menu_dialog.inl` READS THE MENU'S SHARED YES/NO WINDOW** — `0x004C2B10 -> 0x004C2A20` stores body `[0x01D77300]`, options `[0x01D772F0]`/`[0x01D772E0]`, all GLOBALS, so one reader serves every screen — **and it reads the option WORDS off the window rather than assuming Yes-then-No, which this mod has already been caught by once (v0.26.2, the exam's "Really?", which lists NO first).** The Save module is found by **WALKING THE POOL** for update fn `0x004E3090`, not by the `pMenuStateA+0x1A6`/`+0x21E` slot aliases the rest of Save still uses. **(3) MAGIC'S ALL WARNING REPEATED EVERY FRAME WITH WORDING WRONG TWICE OVER** — state 107 (`0x004F1CB6`) is a steady 2-option poll, the dedup key was cleared every poll, and 106/107 are **shared with Exchange and Split**, so an Exchange announced "Cannot take all magic". **(4) `ABIL_MENU_ID_LO` 97 -> 92**; the engine's own guard `0x004C2B40` accepts `[0x5C,0x74)`, so five real abilities read as nothing. **(5) THE ABILITY LIST WAS THE ELEVEN DRAWN GCW ROWS WHILE THE CURSOR AT `+0x258` INDEXES THE WHOLE LIST** — on any GF with more than eleven abilities the mod named the WRONG ability from row twelve on, confidently; now reads the engine's flat list `0x01D8CB54` / count `0x01D8CB6C` (`0x004E770F`), GCW parse kept as fallback. **(6) ZELL'S DUEL PAGE OFF BY ONE** — cursor 1 is Punch Rush and the mod said "Booya"; the page builder pre-seeds its row counter per mode, Squall 4 (`0x004CE92C`) and **Zell 1** (`mov ebx,1` `0x004CE968` + `mov [esp+0x30],ebx` `0x004CE9AD`), corroborated by the help bias `0x004CE4BD` vs `0x004CE536`. **(7) CANCELLING AN ITEM ARRANGE ANNOUNCED "SWAPPED"** on both flows — they leave the destination screen for the SAME state either way, so the transition proves nothing; the decision now rests on the item id at the ARMED SOURCE SLOT (`menu_item_swap_model.inl`), and two slots holding the same item read as "Cancelled" because swapping them is a no-op and that statement stays true either way. **THE SYSTEMATIC FINDING: THE SHARED WINDOW IS OPENED IN 20 PLACES MENU-WIDE AND THE MOD READ ONE.** It reads three now. **The other seventeen are mapped — screen, entry state, cursor byte, all confirmed — in `docs/SUBMENU_AUDIT.md`**, and they include destructive confirmations: Junction "Off" (state 0x12) and "Keep previous setting" (0x2C, `+0x5C`), Switch's junction exchange (0x10, `+0x3F`), GF's "Don't learn anything?" (0x18, `+0x57`). **THEY ARE DELIBERATELY NOT WIRED YET — the shared reader has never run on hardware, and pointing an unproven reader at sixteen more screens is exactly how v0.26.2 shipped a green probe built around the same wrong belief as the code.** **METHOD: the Zell off-by-one and the ability paging both produced perfectly well-formed, confident, WRONG sentences. A log cannot show that. The disassembly is the only thing in this loop that is not downstream of my own assumptions.** **Verified: `menu_sim` OK (0 bad); NEW `menu_save_compile` OK (0 bad) — module walk in all ten slots, out-of-pool/misaligned/cyclic lists, the prompt spoken once and following the cursor, a REVERSED-LABEL window, phase 8, silence on every other phase and with no module, null/empty bodies; NEW `menu_ability_compile` OK (0 bad); magic/junction/card/config/tutorial probes OK; `button_map_rescue_test` OK; `lint_seh` OK (95); `lint_xtu` OK (173); all source files inside the 80 KB guard.** **BAT: save over an existing block — the prompt must speak and the option it reads must be the one Confirm will take. Then Magic -> All between two characters; a GF with more than eleven abilities; Status -> Zell's limit page; and an item arrange you CANCEL.**

**Prior (v0.28.1 — #87: TWO THINGS THE SCREENSHOTS SHOWED).** The v0.28.0 BAT log read clean end to end and I said so. Aaron: *"Be sure to check the screenshots I took as well. Make sure the info shown visually is being announced by the mod."* Two pages did not survive that. **(1) THE BATTLE REPORT IS A TABLE** — Walked/Battles/Won/Escaped in one column, 109751/41/35/6 in another — and the mod read "Walked 109751 Battles 41 Won 35 Escaped 6", four labels and four numbers with nothing saying which belongs to which. **A RUN OF SPACES INSIDE A LINE IS A COLUMN, NOT A GAP:** it now becomes a comma and the row gets a terminating stop. **THREE GUARDS make this safe where the v0.27.0 line-length heuristic was not:** the run must be REAL SOURCE SPACES (2+); the char already emitted before it must be a LETTER OR DIGIT (so sentence spacing after a full stop is left alone, and so is an indented continuation, which follows the space a line break emitted); and **the run must have a word after it on the SAME LINE** — 12 of the 2,926 line breaks in the Information corpus have trailing padding and a comma there lands at a WRAP, which is exactly what killed the earlier attempt. Magic pages gain too: "Target, Single. Effect, Fire damage." **(2) AN EMPTY PAGE HAS TO SAY SO** — "Select name" under Person is all story-gated links, so until you have met somebody the game draws a blank window and the mod announced the title then fell silent, indistinguishable from the mod having failed. Now "Nothing here yet". **METHOD, and this is the one to keep: both defects were sitting in the v0.28.0 log — `"Select name. "` and `"...your party Walked 109751 Battles 41..."` — and I had read that log and called it clean. Neither was LEGIBLE as a defect until the screenshot said what the screen actually looks like. A log tells you what the mod SAID; only the screen tells you what it SHOULD have said.** Revalidated over all three corpora (300 exam questions, 25 magazine pages, 425 Information records), no regressions. **BAT: Information → Battle Report should read "Walked, 109751. Battles, 41. Won, 35. Escaped, 6." Information → Person → a name list you have not filled should say "Nothing here yet". Magic pages under Basic Terms should read "Target, Single. Effect, Fire damage." Everything else unchanged.**

**Prior (v0.28.0 — #87: INFORMATION, THE NESTED PAGE BROWSER).** The last unspoken screen in the Tutorial section and the largest body of writing in the menus — **425 records** across mngrp sections 128-133, the game's glossary. Dispatch 21, update 0x004D5F10, 22 states, table 0x004D6A5C, **steady state 7 only** and no slide reads input. **THE LINKS ARE ENUMERATED, NOT GUESSED:** 0x004D6B20 expands the record into 0x01D7EC48 and — exactly like the exam's answer labels — does NOT copy the 0x0B markers; it diverts each into a position array at 0x01D85658 as {u16 penX, u16 penY, u16 target} and copies the LABEL through as ordinary text. Pen advances 0x10 per line break, so **penY/0x10 is the link's line**, and **checked against all 425 records: not one page puts two links on one line**, so a link's line IS its label. That is the structure the magazine pages could not offer, where no threshold separated a list item from a wrap (v0.27.0). Arrival = title + prose + "N topics" + the link under the cursor (NOT all ten — a page of links read in full is a wall); moving reads the link alone; **key 2 = the whole topic list**, the column a sighted player takes in at a glance; key 1 = every route out, including the two the game marks only as a "1/2" in the title (Left/Right between siblings, Cancel to parent or out at the root); key 0 = the page again. **The cursor is stored PER RECORD by the game** at 0x01D83E4C + record, so backing out and returning lands where you left — the mod reads that table rather than tracking its own, which is why it agrees with the cursor on screen. Nav flags: parent 0x01D84E4C, prev 0x01D8575A, next 0x01D85758, 0xFFFF = none. Validated by rendering all 425 offline: 49 link pages, 376 prose, 0 empty titles, 0 unlabelled links, 0 overflow, 0 truncated labels. **TWO LOG FIXES folded in from the last BAT:** the [TutorialTTS] line now reports the Online Help cursor (every Help line read row=1 while the help cursor moved) and magazine lines widened 200→400 chars so "Last page" is checkable in the menu log. **THAT LEAVES ONLY the nine Online Help guided demos**, which the list already announces as undescribed. **BAT: Tutorial → Information. Expect "Select term. 10 topics. Basic Terms, 1 of 10"; arrow through the topics; key 2 should list all ten; Confirm into one and it should read the page; Cancel should climb back and land on the topic you came from. Find a page whose title ends 1/2 and press Left/Right. Key 1 anywhere should name every way out.**

**Prior (v0.27.0 — #86: THE REST OF THE TUTORIAL SECTION).** Aaron, with a screenshot of the seven-row list: *"Take a look at that and all the options / submenus it contains. We need to make sure we account for and make all of these accessible."* **BATTLE OPERATION, CARD GAME RULES AND ICON EXPLANATION ARE ONE MODULE** — update 0x004C9060, 17 states, **steady state 9**, and unlike the Card album **NO slide state here samples input**. The three creators are byte-identical; only the record range the Tutorial preloads into 0x01D7D3A5/A6 tells them apart (43-50 / 51-63 / 64-67, from mtmag.bin). Page text = 3-4 absolutely positioned blocks: `rec = [0x01D2BAF8] + 68*module[+0x28]`, block idx at `rec+0x34+4i+3` (0xFF-terminated), `string = 0x01D773A4 + u16[0x01D773A6 + idx*2]`. **No pre-processing buffer** — the draw fn renders raw bytes, so the mod expands them itself. All 25 pages validated end to end. **ONLINE HELP IS NOT A MODULE:** row 1's action byte 0xFF sets state 24 inside the Tutorial module, so the list is a second panel — cursor +0x35, length +0x36, row→descriptor map at +0x39.. that the game fills from the story-progress filter (descriptor table 0x00B88360, 9 × 12 bytes). Three rows are named after a party member and use the player's own name. **SYMBOLS: the element and status legends are now named** (Fire..Holy, Death..Drain) on the same authority as the ability icons — section 89 strings 43/46/47 draw each sprite and write its name beside it. Plus the four directions, the junction marker, the Limit Break marker. 0x38/0x3B stay "a button" (Start vs Select NOT established). **A HEURISTIC TRIED AND REVERTED, and this is the bit to remember:** "short line = list item, long line = wrap" would have made the stats legend read as a list, but against the real corpus it wrote *"Same Wall uses Battle, Area wall"* and *"Wall is assumed to have, 'A' value"* — a wrapped line can be 21 chars and a list item 16, and **no threshold separates them**. **A WRONG COMMA IS A LIE ABOUT THE TEXT; A FLAT LIST IS ONLY FLAT.** The sim pins both sentences so it cannot come back. Also fixed: the possessive rule that closes up "Squall 's" was eating the space in "have 'A' value" — a possessive is followed by a LOWER-CASE letter, an opening quote is not. **STILL UNSPOKEN, and the Online Help list now says so out loud:** the Information page browser (menutips, 425 linked records — globals and state map are in docs/TUTORIAL_MENU_FINDINGS.md) and the nine guided demos. **BAT: open Battle Operation and arrow through all 8 pages with Left/Right — each should read heading, counter and body, page 8 should add "Last page". Key 1 = topic and page count, key 0 = repeat. Then Card Game Rules (13 pages) and Icon Explanation (4 pages) — page 1 of Icon Explanation should name every element symbol, page 4 every ability icon. Then Online Help: it should say how many topics are unlocked and warn that the demos are not described yet.**

**Prior (v0.26.2 — #85: THREE THINGS THE LOG SAID).** BAT: *"I think the SeeD test quiz is good to go unless there is something you see which we missed in the log."* Three, and Aaron hit none of them in that session but would have hit all three. **(1) THE ANSWER WORDS ARE NOT ALWAYS YES THEN NO.** Section 95 string 7 — the **"Really?"** confirmation — is `{SLOT:20}NO      {SLOT:21}YES`. **NO IS FIRST**, so the hard-coded Yes/No would have named the EXACT OPPOSITE of the cursor on the one screen whose whole job is to double-check the player; other screens offer END and GO BACK, which it would have called "Yes". The words now come off the screen's own answer line: 0x004D4A80 does NOT copy the 0x0B markers into the drawn buffer, so the labels arrive as ordinary words in the region below the cut, split on a RUN of 2+ spaces (keeps "GO BACK" one answer, "YES     NO" two) and title-cased — a synth handed a short all-caps "NO" is liable to spell it out. **(2) 0xB5 WAS BEING DROPPED.** Only byte above 0xAF anywhere in the exam corpus, 3 occurrences, every one where a pause belongs ("won't go any higher<B5> will you still take the test"). Glyph NOT established (it is not the 0x3C comma; shapes live in the font) but dropping it runs two clauses together in all three — now a comma, right whichever mark it draws. **(3) SUBMON GATED OFF (SUBMON_DIAG 0).** The submenu cursor-DISCOVERY monitor wrote **5,399 of 6,217 BAT-log lines, 87% of the file**, and there is nothing left in the main menu to discover. Gated not deleted, per that file's own convention — the Information pages are still unexplored and it is the tool that would find their cursor. **NOT A BUG:** the pass screen's *"Your scored 100%"* is FF8's OWN typo, section 95 string 2 verbatim — do not chase it. **METHOD:** all three came from reading the corpus and the log rather than the screen in front of us; the ones that bite are the paths the tester has not walked yet. **BAT: fail a test then answer No to the retry offer to reach the "Really?" dialog — it must say "Really?. No" with the cursor on the left and "Really?. Yes" on the right. Everything else should be unchanged, and the menu log should be ~8x smaller.**

**Prior (v0.26.1 — #85: THE EXAM'S MESSAGE WINDOWS WERE READING THE FOOTER).** BAT: *"the screen at the start of the exam and the screen with the results are not reading out as expected."* Both announced **"the Confirm button to quit"** — the hint line along the BOTTOM of the window. **module+0x20 IS THE FOOTER, NOT THE MESSAGE.** The text the game draws is the pre-processed buffer at **0x01D7DAB8**: 0x004D4A80 expands the chosen string into it with names, GF names and NUMBERS already substituted, NUL-terminates it (0x004D4CF5), and the draw fn renders from there (0x004D596C). Reading the stored string would not have sufficed either — *"Your score was 80"* is a {0A} variable and **the score exists only in that buffer**. Questions and messages now share ONE text path, the one the game draws. **CUTTING THE ANSWER LABELS NEEDED NEW ARITHMETIC:** 0x004D4A80 does NOT copy the 0x0B markers into the buffer — it diverts them to a position array at 0x01D7EB40 (8 bytes each, {u16 x, u16 y, u16 slot}) and copies "YES     NO" / "END" through as ordinary words. The pen advances exactly 0x10 per line break (0x004D4CD3), so **first choice y / 0x10 = the line the labels start on**. Also fixed: the poll deduped SPEECH but not the LOG, so an unchanging message window wrote a [TutorialTTS] line every frame — forty identical entries in one second. **METHOD LESSON, and it is the v0.25.1 stub lesson again in a new costume: the probe was built around the same wrong belief the code was** (it staged a question blob and read module+0x20 because that is what the implementation did), so it was green throughout and could never have caught this. It now drives the real buffer and both of Aaron's screenshots are fixtures in it. **BAT: sit a test — the offer screen should read "We will begin the SeeD written test. Your test level now is N. Will you take the level N+1 test?" then "Yes"; the result screen should read "Your score was NN. You failed/passed..." and stop, with no YES/NO/END trailing and no repeat. Questions should be unchanged.**

**Prior (v0.26.0 — #85: THE TUTORIAL MENU AND THE SEED WRITTEN EXAM).** Last of the three. **TWO MODULES, NOT ONE:** Tutorial is dispatch 20 (update 0x004C9CB0, 34 states, jump table 0x004CAC0C) and choosing TEST/Review does NOT change its state — state 17 PUSHES the exam (dispatch 23, update 0x004D4D30, 28 states, table 0x004D5828) and parks in state 18 until it pops, so **both sit in the pool together** and the poll must prefer the exam or it reads a stale menu over a live question. Steady states: Tutorial 4 (seven-row list) and 7 (review picker); exam 21 (answer input) + message windows 5/8/11/13/16/23 (5, 8 and 23 share one handler 0x004D4F43). Tutorial 9 and 11 read Left/Right MID-SLIDE — reading input is still not the test. The list names both gates the game enforces IN SILENCE (TEST needs 0x004C3090() >= 0, Review needs [0x01CFE98B] != 0), because a keypress answered by nothing is indistinguishable from a hang. **QUESTION DATA:** mngrp section 96+testIndex → [0x00B86D30]+0x1F000; u16 count, u16 offsets, and **the byte at each offset is the answer key** with text starting one later (0x004D5475). All ten must be right (0x004D55E4 vs 10) — which is why the running score is NEVER spoken (the screen does not show it; speaking it would be extra information, not equal access) and why the symbols could not be left unnamed. **THE SYMBOLS (Aaron's specific ask):** 29 of 300 live questions carry one. 0x05 0x4x = the six ability icons, named from the game's OWN Icon Explanation page (section 89 string 54) and confirmed by every answer key; 0x05 0x2x = a button chosen through the player's map (0x004A2DF0) so it is spoken as WHAT IT DOES, reusing menu_config_model.inl's function names; 0x05 0x38 = Start-or-Select, NOT provable, stays "a button". Naming the icons trivialises six questions and that is the right trade — unnamed, those six tests are unpassable except by luck. **TWO DEFECTS ONLY REAL DATA FOUND** (both passed a fixture, both failed the corpus): a line break is a WRAP not a sentence end (as a stop it split the game's own sentences in half), and everything after the first 0x0B is answer LABELS not the question (every stored question ends "...YES NO"). Third, smaller: each question's text opens with its own "Question N", so the position label strips it. **VALIDATED BY EXPANDING ALL 300 QUESTIONS straight out of mngrp** — 0 empty, 0 unnamed symbols, every one with exactly 2 choices. Full write-up in docs/TUTORIAL_MENU_FINDINGS.md. **NOT IN THIS BUILD:** the Information pages — sections 128–133, 425 records with parent/prev/next links and inline 0x0B links to other records ((b1<<7)+b2-0x1020, 0x004D6DA6). That is a nested browser, not a list; it needs its own model and it is v0.26.1. **BAT: Tutorial menu — arrow the seven rows; TEST should say which test is next, Review how many you can review, and both should say when they are unavailable. Keys 0/1/2 = row help / position / SeeD standing. Then sit a test: expect "Question 1 of 10" + the question + "Answer Yes", Left/Right says just Yes or No, key 0 repeats the question, key 1 says where you are. **Test levels 2, 3, 5, 8, 9 and 24 are the ones with icons in them** — those should name the icon, e.g. "the Junction Ability icon signifies Junction Ability".**

**Prior (v0.25.2 — #84: THE WAY OUT WAS ON SCREEN THE WHOLE TIME).** Aaron, still stuck after v0.25.1: *"It is still not back to the original controls. I confirmed it was set to Normal on the screen. I also tried the Alt K shortcut... Take a look at the screenshot to see if you can spot the reset option on the customize screen."* **IT WAS IN THE FOOTER: "S to end, F to default".** One press of F fixed it. The game has always offered a one-key reset and the mod never read the line out — two builds went into reproducing a recovery the screen was already offering, because the screen was never being listened to. **THE CUSTOMIZE SCREEN NOW SPEAKS FOR ITSELF:** arrival gives Aaron's warning (*"changing the game's defaults could conflict with keys used by the mod"* — and it is worse than a conflict, since every letter pressed in there is a reassignment), then the way out built from the two bits state 7 actually tests (0x0100 = restore defaults at 0x004EE007, 0x0800 = leave at 0x004EE03E; Cancel is not among them), then the page, then the row. Cursor moves speak the row alone; labels are mngrp section 1 bank 2 entry `row + 10*(page+2)`, DOUBLED because every entry in that bank is a pair, so the same key reads "Walk or Cancel" on the field page and "Cancel" in battle. The Customize branch **deliberately bypasses the whole-line dedup** — four field-page rows read "not used" and a text-keyed guard would go silent exactly where the player needs to know the cursor moved; (page,row) is the right key and the probe pins both rows speaking. **ALT+K NEVER FIRED AND THAT WAS THE REAL v0.25.1 BUG:** not one [BTNMAP] line in any log. The poll runs from the game frame loop and **HOLDING ALT PUTS A WIN32 WINDOW INTO MENU-MODAL MODE**, stalling that loop for as long as the combo is held — the modifier chosen to make the shortcut feel deliberate was the one that guaranteed it could never be observed. Now **SHIFT+F9** (matching Shift+F3/F4); bare F9 rejected because field_nav_handlekeys.inl binds it to the Garden battle SKIP, F10 and Alt rejected as Windows menu keys. Controller-row warning is now **UNCONDITIONAL** — v0.25.1 gated it on the row already reading Customize, and a warning about a one-way door is only useful on the side you can still turn back from. **STILL OPEN:** key LETTERS are font glyphs (`char = 0x004A2DF0(logical) + 0x80`), so they are pixels and appear nowhere as text in the exe or mngrp; CFG_KEY_LETTER[12] in menu_config_model.inl is all null and every caller degrades to naming Shift+F9, because a WRONG key here is worse than no key. **To fill it: one F11 screenshot of the Customize screen with the map at default gives rows 0-7 (logicals 6,4,7,5,2,3,0,1 → raw slots, identity at default) plus the footer's two letters (raw 8 = default, raw 11 = end). Raw 9 and 10 are the analog sticks and never show a letter.** **BAT: open Config — the Controller row should warn every time now. Enter Customize: expect the warning, "Press Shift F9...", "Cancel does nothing here", "Field Map Controls", then "Talk or Confirm". Arrow down through the four "not used" rows and confirm each speaks. Left/Right should rename every row. Press 2 for the way out, 0 for the whole sheet. Shift+F9 anywhere should say "Controls restored to default" and log [BTNMAP] before/after.**

**Prior (v0.25.1 — #84: ALT+K, A WAY OUT OF THE CUSTOMIZE SCREEN).** Aaron, testing v0.25.0: *"I think I inadvertently switched the controls. I tried to switch the option back to Normal but it still doesn't seem correct. For example X is no longer Confirm, Z is."* **THE MOD WALKED HIM UP TO A ONE-WAY DOOR AND DID NOT MENTION IT WAS ONE.** The Controller row's second setting opens a screen where **Cancel is not handled at all** (state 7 tests only 0x0100/0x0800/0x2000/0x8000) and where the Steam port arms a *"press the button you want"* rebinder — so a blind player feeling for the way out **remaps a control with every press**, and the documented exit is a button whose keyboard equivalent is written down nowhere in the game. v0.25.0 warned about the dead Cancel only once you were already inside. **ALT+K NOW RESTORES STOCK CONTROLS FROM ANYWHERE** — field, menu or battle, in dinput8.cpp beside the volume keys, edge-triggered. It reproduces the game's own defaults action (0x004EE007: `map[i]=i+1` for twelve, clear analog-swap 0x0080) **and also clears the Controller bit 0x0020, which is the part that actually matters**: 0x004A2D60 — the function EVERY button read passes through — returns the input word COMPLETELY UNTOUCHED unless that bit is set (`test byte [0x1CFE73C], 0x20 / jne` are its first two instructions), so clearing the bit is on its own sufficient to restore stock controls and the map rewrite is belt and braces. Unrelated flags (ATB, Cursor, Scan) are preserved and the probe asserts it. **DIAGNOSIS NOTE FOR NEXT TIME:** the settings block including the 12-byte map is saved PER SAVE SLOT (0x004CA960 copies 20 bytes to save+0x27AE0, 0x004CADC0 copies them back), so **loading any save made before the change also restores the map** — a no-build recovery worth remembering. And Aaron's install has NO `ff8input.cfg` (the string is at 0x00B86A74; the Steam rebinder would write one), which is what rules the Steam-side binding out and pins this on the in-game map. **THE CONFIG SCREEN NOW WARNS BEFORE THE DOOR, NOT ONLY AFTER:** sitting on "Controller, Customize" explains what Confirm will do, and **a non-stock map is reported even when the row reads Normal** ("Buttons have been remapped. Press Alt K to restore defaults") — that is exactly the state Aaron was left in, and it is the case a warning attached to the Customize row alone would have missed. A stock pad on Normal says nothing extra, because a warning that fires when nothing is wrong is noise; both halves are gated. **Verified: button_map_rescue OK (0 bad)** — swapped maps, the Customize bit on its own, unrelated flags preserved, edge-triggered; menu_sim / menu_config_compile / menu_card_compile / menu_junction_compile / menu_magic_compile OK; lint_seh OK (93 files); all world-map tests and harnesses green. **BAT: press Alt+K — expect "Controls restored to default" (or "already at default"), then check X is Confirm again. Then open Config: the Controller row should read "Controller, Normal" with nothing extra. Grep the log for `[BTNMAP] before` / `after` to see the actual twelve bytes.** **BUILD FIX (same version -- it never compiled, so it was never pushed):** MSVC rejected `menu_tts_config.inl(83): error C2653: 'ButtonMapRescue': is not a class or namespace name`. ButtonMapRescue lives in `src/button_map_rescue.inl`, which **dinput8.cpp** includes; the Config row calling it is part of **menu_tts.cpp**. Two TUs, one name, nothing declaring it across the gap -- and `IsDefault()` was `static`, so a declaration alone would only have moved the failure to link time. Now non-static + `namespace ButtonMapRescue { bool IsDefault(); }` forward-declared in menu_tts.cpp. **NEITHER HOST PROBE COULD HAVE CAUGHT IT:** menu_config_compile stubs ButtonMapRescue because it does not own it, button_map_rescue_test stubs the menu side for the same reason, and both were green. **A STUB IS A STATEMENT ABOUT AN INTERFACE, NOT EVIDENCE THE INTERFACE IS REACHABLE.** New gate `tests/lint_xtu.py`: namespaces defined only in `.inl` (never declared in a `.h`), mapped to the `.cpp` that textually includes them, flagged when another TU uses the name with no forward declaration; comments are stripped first so a prose mention is not a call. Verified against the broken tree (reports exactly the line MSVC rejected) and `lint_xtu: OK` on the fixed tree, 169 files.

**Prior (v0.25.0 — #84: THE CONFIG SCREEN).** Second of the three menus. **THE THING THAT MAKES THIS SCREEN DIFFERENT: EVERY VALUE ON IT IS INVISIBLE TO A SCREEN READER BY DESIGN.** The four toggles draw BOTH words side by side (Normal *and* Customize, Active *and* Wait) and signal which is active ONLY by PALETTE — bright vs dimmed, no marker, no brackets, no cursor on the value — and the five bars have NO numeric readout at all. Everywhere else the mod adds context to text that is at least present; here it is speaking the entire state of the screen. **THE TWO ROWS THE MOD HAS TAKEN OVER**, which is what Aaron asked for: *"when a volume option is set the announcement can tell the player volume is controlled by the accessibility mod and the keys... When ATB is selected it should inform the player whether Enhanced Wait Mode is on/off and the key for it."* **Sound** → *"Sound, 100 percent. Volume is controlled by the mod: F7 and F8 for music, F5 and F6 for effects"* — a blind player who drags that slider and hears nothing change would reasonably conclude the setting is broken, when in fact the mod's mixer is what they are hearing. **ATB** → *"ATB, Active. Enhanced Wait Mode is on. Press O in battle to toggle it"* — and the two are genuinely independent: the game's bit 0x0001 only freezes the battle clock **while a command or target menu is open** (0x004A9450, called once from 0x004842D1), which is far narrower than EWM, so which of the two is on is the useful fact rather than the bit. **Both notes fire EVERY time the row is selected, not once per visit** — the whole point is that the row does not do what its name says, and someone returning an hour later needs it again. EWM state is read via `Config::GetInt("ewm_enabled")`, the INI key the O-key toggle already persists, rather than adding a cross-TU accessor for a value only this screen reads. **THE BARS RUN THE OPPOSITE WAY TO THEIR STORED BYTE:** the value is 0..4 with **0 = the FULL bar**, and Right DECREASES it. Speaking the raw byte would tell the player 0 is the most and 4 the least — backwards from the bar in front of them AND from any guide. The spoken number is the **bar length 1..5** with only the two ends given an adjective, and "fastest" on the three speed rows becomes **"most"** on Camera movement, which shares the widget but not the meaning. **Identification double-confirmed the way Card's was:** 0x00B87FE0 pairs text entry 9 ("Config" / "Configuration Menu") with action byte 8, and dispatch index 8's overlay is menucfg.ovl, used by no other index. Creator 0x004EDD30, update **0x004EDE90**, draw 0x004EE750, state table 0x004EE6D8 (14 states), 9 rows at **0x00B88970** (stride 0x10), settings block **0x01CFE738** (20 bytes, saved per slot), flag word 0x01CFE73C. **STEADY STATES ARE 3 (list) AND 7 (Customize) — AND STATE 2 FALLS THROUGH INTO STATE 3's BODY**, so the FIRST INTERACTIVE FRAME REPORTS 2. Gating on 3 alone would have dropped the arrival line whenever the poll happened to land on that frame: an intermittent, which is the worst kind. Both are speakable and the dedup makes the overlap free. States 5 and 11 are the list↔Customize slides and read **no input at all** — worth noting against the Card album, whose slides DO read input, which is why "does it read input" is not the steadiness test. **LEFT/RIGHT CHANGE A VALUE WITHOUT MOVING THE CURSOR OR THE STATE**, so the dedup key is the WHOLE COMPOSED LINE. Keyed on the row number, adjusting a slider would have been completely silent — and silence is the one thing this screen must never be, because nothing on it reports a change. **CANCEL DOES NOT WORK IN THE CUSTOMIZE SUB-SCREEN:** state 7 tests only the 0x0800 edge bit and never reads the Cancel bit, so pressing Cancel does nothing and reads exactly like the game having hung. Sighted players see the on-screen legend. That warning now LEADS the Customize announcement. Keys 0/1/2 = every setting in one pass, position, the game's own help line for the row. **Verified: menu_sim OK (0 bad)** — all four toggles both ways, both overridden rows including the mod-state-versus-game-bit independence, the bar inversion, the camera row's different meaning, the nine-setting readout, and 20,000 randomised views with out-of-range rows and bar bytes above 4; **menu_config_compile OK (0 bad)** — module walk in all ten slots, the 14-state gate including the fall-through, a value change with the cursor stationary, and the Customize warning; menu_card_compile / menu_junction_compile / menu_magic_compile OK; lint_seh OK (92 files); all world-map tests and harnesses green. **NOT DONE: the Customize screen's per-row button assignments** — the page and the exit warning are spoken, the ten action rows are not (rows 0x00B88A10, page +0x29, cursor +0x2A, map 0x01CFE740). **BAT: main menu -> Config. Walk all nine rows; every one should say its label AND its current value. Change a toggle with left/right WITHOUT moving the cursor — it must speak the new value. Land on ATB and on Sound and check the extra sentences. Check a speed bar reads 5 of 5 at its longest and 1 of 5 at its shortest, and that Camera movement says most/least rather than fastest/slowest. Press 0, 1, 2. Then Confirm on Controller when it reads Customize — expect the page name and the Cancel warning; getting back out needs the controller's select button.** **NEXT: Tutorial** — including the SeeD exam quiz and the symbols some questions display.

**Prior (v0.24.2 — #83: THE FIFTH NUMBER NEEDED A NAME).** v0.24.1 BAT passed (*"That worked great!"*). One tweak: *"you hear a card's name followed by its values and then the quantity you have of that card. The trick is you hear the values immediately followed by the quantity number without context. e.g. Bit Bug 1 3 5 2 4 - that 4 at the end is actually the quantity, not one of the values."* **Exactly right, and it is a failure OF the terse format rather than a bug IN it — worth writing down because the same trap is waiting in any list that speaks bare numbers. Four bare numbers are unambiguous PRECISELY BECAUSE there are always four of them: the ear counts them off and stops. A fifth number silently joins the set, and there is nothing in the SOUND to say it is not a left-hand value.** The line now reads "Bite Bug, 1 3 3 5, quantity 4" and "Ifrit, 9 6 2 8, Fire, quantity 2" — one word, and deliberately the SAME word the Magic and Junction lists already use, so there is now one term for "how many of these you have" across the whole menu. Unchanged: a quantity of exactly 1 is still not spoken (the four-number form stays clean for the common case) and "not held" still covers the only other state. **Verified: menu_sim OK (0 bad)** — including Aaron's own example card, Bite Bug, whose real values are 1 3 3 5 (his "1 3 5 2" was illustrative, and the test uses the card's actual numbers so it is checking the mod rather than the anecdote); **menu_card_compile OK (0 bad)** — the labelled count read through the real offsets; menu_junction_compile / menu_magic_compile OK; lint_seh OK (91 files); entryaim/trigwalk/trigseg/pathdecimate/vehsig OK; catalog_story and garden_aboard 0 failures; all four harnesses compile. **NEXT: Config, then Tutorial.**

**Prior (v0.24.1 — #83: TWO PANELS THE SCREENSHOT CAUGHT).** Aaron BAT'd the Card album clean — 64 [CardTTS] lines, ZERO errors — and sent an F11 screenshot with Ifrit selected. **THE SCREENSHOT SHOWS THE FOUR NUMBERS AS 9 / 8 / 6 / 2 IN THEIR DIAMOND, WHICH IS EXACTLY WHAT THE MOD SAID ("Ifrit, 9 6 2 8")** — so the generated table is now confirmed against the RENDERED SCREEN and not only against the exe, which is the one check neither the generator's arithmetic gate nor the published-values gate could give. **It also showed two panels the mod was reading none of, which is exactly why asking for a screenshot was worth doing.** **(1) THE SUMMARY PANEL** — permanently down the right-hand side: MONSTER 9 / BOSS 0 / GF 2 / PLAYER 0 / TOTAL 11. The game computes these itself at album open into module **+0x32..+0x3A**; key 0 now speaks them **in the screen's own order** before the two numbers the screen does NOT show (different-of-110 and seen). **v0.24.0's key 0 recomputed its own totals, which is worse than useless: they would not have matched what a guide or a sighted player was looking at.** **(2) THE BOTTOM INFO LINE, on new key 3** — the bar the screenshot shows as "AREA … Squall", which changes with the cursor. For the 77 COMMON cards it is labelled **MONSTER** and names the monster that carries the card, or the PAIR that play it from level 5 up ("Carried by Snow Lion, Funguar") — a static table at [0x00B96500] = 0x00C75524, generated into menu_card_data.inl with the rest. For the 33 RARES it is labelled **AREA** and is **DYNAMIC**: it names whoever is holding the card RIGHT NOW — 0xF0 = you, 0x00 = "used up", anything else = an NPC id resolved through the map at 0x00B96878 into the loaded areames bank, **reproduced exactly as 0x004C0660 does it** (`bank = [0x01D2BB48]; if (textId >= u16[bank]) textId = 0; str = bank + u16[bank + textId*2 + 2]`) — two reads and a bounds check, no file parsing and no calling into the game from the mod's thread. **FOR A RARE CARD THAT LINE IS THE ONLY THING IN THE GAME THAT TELLS YOU WHERE TO GO AND FIND IT, and it was invisible.** On a KEY rather than in the list line because it is a sentence and the list line is meant to be four numbers you can move through. **With no areames bank loaded the reader stays SILENT** rather than speaking whatever a null pointer dereferences to, and the probe asserts that — which forced a probe fix worth remembering: **on the host `__try` is a no-op, so an unmapped read is a SEGFAULT rather than the caught exception it is in the real build**, and the probe therefore has to map the pages the mod is ALLOWED to fault on before it can test the null path. **Verified: menu_card_compile OK (0 bad)** — both fixed owner codes, the common-card source including a level-5 pair, the null-bank silence, the unseen card, and the summary panel read from the module's own counters; menu_sim OK (0 bad); menu_junction_compile / menu_magic_compile OK; lint_seh OK (91 files); entryaim/trigwalk/trigseg/pathdecimate/vehsig OK; catalog_story and garden_aboard 0 failures; all four harnesses compile. **KEPT AS-IS DELIBERATELY:** a quantity of exactly 1 is still not spoken. The screen does show "1" in its NUM column, but "not held" already carries the only distinction that matters and 110 rows is a lot of ones — say the word and it is a one-line change. **BAT: on the album press 3 on a common card (expect "Carried by <monster>"), on a level 5+ card (expect a PAIR of monsters), and on a rare/GF card you hold (expect "Area, you have it"); if you know of a rare card an NPC still holds, 3 on that one should name them. Press 0 and check the five numbers match the panel on screen.** **NEXT: Config, then Tutorial** — requirements already recorded (Config must say where the mod has overridden a setting: volume is the mod's, with its keys; ATB must report Enhanced Wait Mode and its key. Tutorial must make the SeeD exam quiz accessible INCLUDING the symbols some questions display).

**Prior (v0.24.0 — #83: THE CARD ALBUM).** Aaron: *"the only menus we haven't built out yet are the Card, Config, and Tutorial menus... We can do the three menus one at a time."* This is Card — the first screen in the main menu the mod had never spoken a single word of. **THE LINE IS FOUR BARE NUMBERS, CLOCKWISE FROM THE TOP** ("Geezard, 1 4 1 5"), per Aaron: *"just announce each number with a clear space between rather than saying 'Top 5, Right 2, Bottom 3, Left 4' as that is verbose and will get old to the player"*; the labelled form lives behind key 2, for when you have STOPPED moving and want to be sure which one was the left. **TEN IS SPOKEN AS "A"** — what the card shows, and what every Triple Triad guide, opponent and rule discussion calls it; "ten" would be a private vocabulary matching nothing the player can look up. **IDENTIFICATION WAS A TRAP AND IT NEARLY TOOK ME.** Dispatch indices 20, 22 and 30 ALL use text group 13 and all look like the card menu — **their group 13 is a different mngrp SECTION**, because their text overlay is menututo.ovl (they are Tutorial). The album is **dispatch index 7** (creator 0x004EF020, update 0x004EF6F0, draw 0x004EF750, jump table 0x004EF6BC, 13 states), confirmed from two directions: the dispatch pair's second dword at 0x00B87ED8 is **NOT a music id** as first assumed but the TEXT-OVERLAY id (index 7 -> 12 = menucrd.ovl), and the main menu's own {textEntry, actionByte} table at **0x00B87FE0** pairs text entry 6 ("Card" / "Look at cards") with action byte 7. **STATE 5 IS THE ONLY STEADY STATE, AND THE TEST THAT FOUND THE JUNCTION GRID DOES NOT WORK HERE:** states 7 and 9 are the page-slide animations and they **DO read the input word**, because they queue a further left/right flip mid-slide. "Does this state read input" is therefore NOT the test; **"can the player sit still in it"** is — 7 and 9 auto-advance to 5 when the slide finishes. Worth remembering before the Config and Tutorial machines. **THE TWO SAVEMAP ENCODINGS SHARE ONE BYTE.** For the 77 common cards 0x01CFEF38+id is `0x80 | count` and **0x00 means NEVER SEEN, not zero held**; for the 33 rares (id >= 77) the same byte is an **OWNER CODE** — 0xF0 you hold it, 0x00 "used up", anything else an NPC id — and "have you ever seen it" is a separate bit at 0x01CFEFA6 (5 bytes, 33 bits). So 0x00 means two different things depending on which side of card 77 you are on, which is exactly the kind of thing a wiki-shaped assumption gets wrong. The mod REPRODUCES 0x00534950 rather than calling it, because calling into the game from the mod's thread is a risk a read-only screen does not need to take. **THE ALBUM IS READ-ONLY — there is no confirm handler at all**, which makes it the simplest screen in the menu and also the one where getting the NUMBERS right is the entire job. **Unobtained cards are conveyed by DIMMING and never-seen ones by a BLANK ROW**, both invisible to a screen reader, both now spoken — and a never-seen card is deliberately **NOT NAMED** ("Card 7, not seen"), because the blanks are the point of a collection record and naming one would leak the album. **THE 110-CARD TABLE IS GENERATED, NEVER TYPED:** offline/gen_cards.py emits src/menu_card_data.inl from 0x00C74D00 (stride 8, [0]=Top [1]=Bottom [2]=Left [3]=Right [4]=element bitmask [5]=AI rating) and the name table at 0x00C75074. **The generator REFUSES TO EMIT** unless every card has four powers in 1..10, at most one element bit, and byte +5 equal to (T²+B²+L²+R²)/2 — the last being the game's own precomputed rating and therefore an INDEPENDENT confirmation that those four bytes really are the four powers. Nine published card values are a hard gate on top, **chosen so most have four DISTINCT numbers: the arithmetic check squares and sums all four and is blind to a transposition, so only a card whose numbers differ can catch one.** A hundred and ten hand-typed rows would have been a hundred and ten chances to be quietly wrong about a number the player cannot check against the screen.
