Keys 0/1/2 = collection (held / different / seen — the game's own summary counts duplicates and stops there), position, labelled card. **Verified: menu_sim OK (0 bad)** — nine published cards pinned, every name and power range-checked, terse and labelled lines, dimmed and blank rows, "A" for ten, the four category boundaries, and 20,000 randomised views with out-of-range cursors and counts; **menu_card_compile OK (0 bad)** — the module walk in all ten pool slots, BOTH savemap encodings including the 0x00-means-two-things case, the 13-state gate, the page-turn header, repeat suppression and the number keys; menu_junction_compile / menu_magic_compile OK; lint_seh OK (91 files); entryaim/trigwalk/trigseg/pathdecimate/vehsig OK; catalog_story and garden_aboard 0 failures; all four harnesses compile. **BAT: main menu -> Card. Expect "Level 1, Monster. Geezard, 1 4 1 5" on entry. Up/down should read one row each; left/right should turn the page and re-announce the level (10 levels, 11 rows each). Find a card you own several of (the quantity is spoken only when it is more than one), one you have seen but do not hold ("not held"), and an unseen row ("Card N, not seen"). Check an elemental card — Ifrit, Shiva, Quezacotl — names its element. Press 0 for the collection, 1 for the position, 2 for the labelled card. Grep the log for `[CardTTS] cursor=` and `album module at ... pool slot N`.** **FULL DISASSEMBLY IN docs/CARD_MENU_FINDINGS.md**, including the complete 110-card table with names, powers, elements and levels. **NEXT: Config, then Tutorial.** Aaron's requirements for those are recorded: Config must add announcements where the mod has overridden or duplicated a setting (volume is controlled by the mod — say so and give the keys; ATB must report whether Enhanced Wait Mode is on/off and its key), and Tutorial must make the SeeD Exam quiz accessible **including the symbols some questions display**.

**Prior (v0.23.3 — #82: THE CHARACTER SWITCH, AND TWO THINGS THE LOG SAID).** BAT: *"That worked well. Check the logs for any bugs or issues we may have missed. The only thing I noticed is that Junction here doesn't announce the name of the new selected character when Q and E are pressed to switch between characters."* **(1) L1/R1 SWAPS THE CHARACTER AND NOTHING SAID SO.** The switch is a change in **pMenuStateA+0x261**, the state machine's own character field, so the watcher runs in EVERY state rather than only the two that speak lines — **which is what the log settled: he made the switch at the ACTION ROW, not the grid** (17:40:25 `[SUBMON] +0x261: 2 -> 0`, with `ActionMenu: Junction` either side of it and nothing spoken between). A watcher living inside the grid/magic poll would have missed the very case that was reported. **THE COMPLICATION IS THAT CONFIRMING OUT OF CHARACTER SELECT CHANGES THE SAME BYTE** (17:39:22 `+0x261: 0 -> 2`), and there the char-select screen has just said "Irvine, Level 19, HP 522 of 1837" — repeating the name is noise. **Both cases sit ~2 seconds after a CharSelect announcement, so RECENCY CANNOT TELL THEM APART; the VALUE can** — a confirm lands on the character char-select just named, a switch never does. New `s_juncCharSelSpoke` records it and the watcher **CONSUMES** the marker, so switching away and back announces BOTH times; a recency test would have swallowed the second, and the gate asserts exactly that sequence. In states 52/59 the name goes into the HEADER instead of being a separate utterance ("Squall. HP, Life, 2370"), and the grid's arrival header now names the character as well ("Junction, Squall.") — every line on that screen differs per character and NONE of them say whose it is. **(2) THE CHARACTER SELECT ANNOUNCED ITSELF TWICE** — not audible enough for Aaron to report, but the log has two identical `CharSelect: Irvine, Level 19, HP 522 of 1837` lines in the same second, at 17:39:45 AND again at 17:40:23. The "force a re-announce when returning from deeper" reset lived at the END of PollJunctionSubmenu: the arrival frame announced with the stale cursor still matching, then the reset fired and the next frame announced again. **Moved ABOVE the announce block** so the arrival frame is the only one that speaks. Pre-existing since v0.09. **(3) THE LOG CLAIMED A SOLVED SCREEN WAS UNHANDLED** — `[JuncTTS] Unhandled focus=52` x14, `=59` x11, plus 49/54/56/57/58/61/62/63. Those are the grid, the magic list and their slide/scroll states, owned by PollJunctionStats since v0.23.0; the diagnostic predates it and now skips 49..62, so the next BAT log does not read like a hole in a screen that is finished. **(4) NOTED, NOT A BUG — THE ELIGIBILITY MASK LAGS A COLUMN CHANGE.** +0x24A is rebuilt by state 58 (entering the magic list) and by state 52's own input block at 0x004DE45E, but **NOT by the page-scroll states 53..56**, so a grid line read on the frame after a column change carries the PREVIOUS column's mask — which is why the log shows slot 9 as 003FFFFF once and 00196058 the next time. **Nothing on the grid uses it** (JuncAnnounceGrid never reads the mask) and the "None of your magic affects this row" header is only ever composed in state 59, where 58 has just rebuilt it — so every readout was correct. But a stale number in a log invites a bug hunt, so it is now printed ONLY in state 59. **WHAT THE LOG CONFIRMED WORKING:** the v0.23.2 trade on both attack rows ("Thunder, quantity 90, Ice 46 to 0 percent, Thunder 0 to 45 percent" and "Berserk, quantity 11, Berserk 0 to 11 percent, Slow 41 to 0 percent" — the row that started the arc), v0.23.1's assembled status mask ("Status attack, Slow, Slow 41 percent"), "locked" on two characters with genuinely different junction abilities, the grid rows across both pages, and **zero errors or exceptions in 1,169 lines**. (ff8_mod.log's `MH_Initialize = MH_ERROR_ALREADY_INITIALIZED` is pre-existing and benign.) **Verified: menu_junction_compile OK (0 bad)** — the switch named on the action row, folded into the header on the grid, silent on a char-select confirm, and speaking again after switching away and back; **menu_sim OK (0 bad)**; menu_magic_compile OK (0 bad); lint_seh OK (90 files); entryaim/trigwalk/trigseg/pathdecimate/vehsig OK; catalog_story and garden_aboard 0 failures; all four harnesses compile. **BAT: press Q/E from the action row — expect just the name. Press Q/E while on the junction grid — expect "<Name>. <row>" as ONE utterance. Enter the grid fresh — expect "Junction, <Name>. <row>". Go to char select, pick a character and confirm — expect the usual name/level/HP line ONCE (not twice) and NO second bare name after it. Then switch away with Q/E and back again: both should speak.** **BAT RESULT: PASSED** — *"You can only press Q and E to switch between characters on the Junction screen when the Action Bar has focus. Tested that and announcements worked as expected."* **The action-row-only constraint is now PINNED IN THE EXE, not just observed:** `+0x43` is written at exactly two instructions — 0x004DBCF2 and 0x004DBF68, the handlers for states 4 and 6 — and states 4 and 6 are dispatched from ONE place only, state 3 (0x004DABB5 and 0x004DAD11). Nothing else in the 74-state machine touches it. The swap runs **4 -> 5 -> 6 -> 7 -> 3** as a slide, resets the ability left-panel cursor +0x5E, and re-syncs the two 464-byte stat blocks via 0x0049A7B0, so the preview's baseline is already correct for the new character. The watcher stays state-agnostic regardless: reading the field costs nothing, and "only state 3 can do this" is a fact about the game's INPUT ROUTING, which is the more fragile of the two facts to lean on. The grid/magic header branch is now MARKED as the defensive path it is rather than left looking load-bearing. **⚠ THE NEAR-MISS THE CONFIRMATION TURNED UP, AND IT IS THE THING TO REMEMBER: THE SWAP ALSO CALLS 0x004BE790** (0x004DABA9 and 0x004DAD05, on the way into states 4 and 6) — **the very auto-junction routine the mod's Auto-confirm HARDWARE BREAKPOINT watches.** That detector is correct only because it gates on the Auto submenu being focused (+0x22E == 11), and during a swap the focus is 3. **Without the gate, every character switch would announce "Junctioned automatically for ..." — and only after an Auto submenu had been opened at some point in the session**, i.e. an intermittent that reproduces on nobody's first try. Recorded in the VEH handler itself and in docs/JUNCTION_MENU.md; do not widen that gate. No behaviour change, so the version stays 0.23.3.

**Prior (v0.23.2 — #82: A JUNCTION IS A TRADE, AND ONLY ONE SIDE WAS SPOKEN).** BAT: *"it is only announcing the one value when in fact two or more may change... the mod said Confuse 8% or similar, but neglected to mention the drop in the Stop status. A sighted player can see both effects. Make sure this fix applies to elements and other magic junctions as well where increasing one value decreases another."* **THE CAUSE WAS A DESIGN NOTE I WROTE IN v0.23.1 AND WAS WRONG ABOUT:** *"an attack row is a SET plus one percentage, not a per-entry table, so there is no useful delta to speak... naming the outgoing set as well would double the sentence for no gain, since the grid line already said it."* **It is not no gain.** Junctioning Confuse over Stop RAISES Confuse and DROPS Stop and BOTH ARROWS ARE ON SCREEN AT ONCE; saying only the rise describes a trade as though it were a gift. And the grid line does not cover it, because the grid line describes the row BEFORE you started choosing — the one moment the outgoing value is not news. **THE FIX GENERALISES RATHER THAN SPECIAL-CASES.** An attack row is now turned into the SAME per-entry before/after table the defence rows already were — an entry's percentage is `(mask has it) ? row percentage : 0` — and every row type goes through ONE collector (`JuncCollectDeltas`) and ONE emitter (`JuncAppendDeltas`). **There is no longer a code path that CAN report one side of a change**, which matters more than the specific bug: the old shape had two independent branches and only one of them was ever going to be exercised by the row Aaron happened to test. "Confuse, quantity 8, Stop 40 to 0 percent, Confuse 0 to 8 percent". **THE OUTGOING SIDE HAS TO COME OFF THE BASELINE BLOCK (0x01D8B3B0) AND BE ASSEMBLED FROM ITS OWN +0x1B4 AND +0x18C** — reading the live block's status word for both halves reports no drop at all, and it is the kind of slip that still passes a test whose fixture sets only one block, so menu_junction_compile plants a DIFFERENT spell in each block and asserts both clauses appear. **THE FLAT CAP OF FOUR IS GONE**, because it could truncate exactly the drop that was missing. Deltas are **GROUPED BY THEIR (from, to) PAIR**, which is the shape junction changes actually have — a whole set moving 0->N or M->0 — so ten statuses arriving together are ONE clause naming ten statuses ("Poison, Petrify, Darkness, Silence, Berserk, Sleep, Slow, Stop 0 to 20 percent") rather than ten clauses, and rather than four clauses and a shrug. The remaining caps (4 groups, 8 names) exist ONLY so a pathological state cannot overflow the caller's 512-byte buffer; grouping means a real junction never reaches them, and anything dropped is still admitted with ", and more" — **a silent truncation would read as completeness, which is the failure this whole change is about.** Order follows the TABLE, not the direction of the change, so the preview and the on-demand readout agree about where an element sits in the list. **Verified: menu_sim OK (0 bad)** — Aaron's exact case (Stop 40->0 while Confuse 0->8), the same-set-lower-percentage case (one delta, not two), a three-status spell displacing a one-status spell, the elemental-attack swap, the defence swap, the ten-status grouping with its admitted tail, and **20,000 randomised views that now randomise BOTH halves of every attack and defence table** — the worst case for the emitter is a before and an after sharing no entries at all, which the old fuzz could never generate because it left the before halves fixed. **menu_junction_compile OK (0 bad)** — the trade read through the two real blocks, plus the v0.23.1 state gate, paging replay and mask assembly. menu_magic_compile OK (0 bad); lint_seh OK (90 files); entryaim/trigwalk/trigseg/pathdecimate/vehsig OK; catalog_story and garden_aboard 0 failures; all four harnesses compile. **METHOD RULE, AND IT IS THE ONE TO KEEP FROM THIS ARC: THREE OF THE LAST FOUR JUNCTION DEFECTS WERE A SENTENCE I WROTE ASSERTING THAT SOMETHING DID NOT NEED SAYING.** "The grid line already said it" was the third. A comment that JUSTIFIES AN OMISSION is the first place to look when a BAT says something is missing — it is where a decision got made without evidence and then got documented as though it had been. **BAT: (1) with a status spell already junctioned to ST-Atk, open the row and hover a DIFFERENT status spell — you should hear both clauses, the old status falling to 0 and the new one rising. (2) Same on Elem-Atk with two different elements. (3) Same on an Elem-Def and a ST-Def row. (4) Hover the spell that is ALREADY junctioned: expect "no change". (5) A multi-status spell (Pain, Life, Dispel) should group its statuses into one clause rather than reading them one at a time.**

**Prior (v0.23.1 — #82: THE STATUS-ATTACK ROW WAS READING HALF A MASK).** BAT: *"Junctioning to ST-Atk doesn't seem to announce any value. Regardless of which spell I selected for it I heard ST-Atk would have no effect"* and *"as I moved through the page of spells to junction I heard repetitive announcement on each page."* **(1) THE STATUS-ATTACK MASK IS ASSEMBLED, NOT STORED.** v0.23.0 read it as a u16 at block +0x1B4 with the percentage as a u16 at +0x1B6 — both invented, and the Status screen could not have corroborated them because its own v0.18.2.50 investigation concluded there is NO ST-Atk field in the block at all (it derives the value from char+0x66 and the stock count). **0x004E0FA0 takes SEVEN bits from +0x1B4 and SIX MORE out of the status word at +0x18C** — Sleep(7) 0x0001, Slow(8) 0x0004, Stop(9) 0x0008, Curse(10) 0x0200, Confuse(11) 0x4000, Drain(12) 0x8000 — which is FF8's real two-word status bitfield folded into the thirteen entries the junction screen shows. **Six of the thirteen live ENTIRELY in the second word, Sleep among them**, i.e. about the most likely thing anyone junctions to ST-Atk, so the old read returned zero. **Wrong in the direction that produces SILENCE rather than a wrong number, which is exactly why it presented as an unhooked row rather than as a bad value.** The percentage is offset as well (0x004E0C7D: `sub eax, 0x64` on +0x1B6) while the elemental-attack percentage next door is ABSOLUTE (0x004E12C3 uses +0x1C5 raw) — the game's asymmetry, not a transcription slip. New `JuncAssembleStatusMask()` in the model mirrors the game exactly; menu_sim pins **all thirteen bits individually** plus an assertion that the whole set is reachable and that bit 7 of +0x1B4 is NOT a status — **a two-bit spot check would have PASSED on the broken version, which is the point.** **(2) "NO EFFECT HERE" ON EVERY SPELL WAS CORRECT, AND STILL THE WRONG THING TO SAY.** 0x004C2E50(id, slot) reads the spell's kernel.bin entry at 0x01CF4064 + 60*id and returns, by slot: stats -> entry+0x17+slot (signed), Elem-Atk -> +0x20 (value +0x21), **ST-Atk -> +0x26 (value +0x24)**, Elem-Def -> +0x22 (+0x23), ST-Def -> +0x28 (+0x25). **Only THIRTEEN spells in the entire game have a non-zero +0x26** — exactly one per junctionable status (Bio, Blind, Confuse, Sleep, Silence, Break, Death, Drain, Pain, Berserk, Zombie, Slow, Stop) — so on ST-Atk almost everything a player is carrying genuinely does nothing. Correct, but hearing it spell after spell sounds precisely like a hook that has given up. Now said ONCE on arrival via the new `JuncMagicHeader()`: *"Choose magic for Status attack. None of your magic affects this row"*, with the per-spell qualifier kept for the mixed case and **an empty inventory deliberately exempt** — blaming the row for the player having no magic would be a new wrong answer. (Twenty spells have a non-zero +0x28, so the defence side is far less sparse.) **(3) THE TWO ATTACK ROWS PREVIEWED AS "no change".** The delta walk only covered the defence arrays, so on Elem-Atk and ST-Atk — where what the row would BECOME is the entire question — every candidate reported doing nothing. An attack row is a SET plus one percentage, not a per-entry table, so there is no useful delta: the line now says the resulting set ("Sleep, quantity 30, Sleep 30 percent"). **(4) THE PAGE-TURN REPEAT IS THE v0.22.1 MAGIC BUG AGAIN.** Paging the spell list is **59 -> 60 -> 59** (state 60 at 0x004DED18 is page-left, 62 page-right; both rewrite +0x50 and hand back), and the poll recorded the TRANSIENT state, so the return to 59 looked like a fresh arrival and re-spoke the header. Same fix as then: **remember the last state SPOKEN IN, never the last state seen.** The dedup key now also carries the grid and magic cursors, so a page turn onto an identically-worded entry ("Empty", or the same spell held twice) still speaks while only the words are read. **menu_junction_compile REPLAYS the real 59->60->59 chain seven times and asserts 0 repeated headers / 7 spoken lines — replaying the transition rather than constructing its endpoints, which is now the rule that has caught the last three of these.** **FREE CONFIRMATION:** kernel.bin's per-spell ST-Atk masks give exactly one spell per bit and each one is the spell of that name (Death->0, Bio->1, Break->2, Blind->3, Silence->4, Berserk->5, Zombie->6, Sleep->7, Slow->8, Stop->9, Pain->1,3,4, Confuse->11, Drain->12), so **the 13-status ordering now agrees from THREE unrelated directions** — kernel element/status fields, the Status screen's live junctions, and the game's own mask assembler. **Verified: menu_sim OK (0 bad); menu_junction_compile OK (0 bad) — including the paging replay and the status-attack read through both halves of the mask; menu_magic_compile OK (0 bad); lint_seh OK (90 files); entryaim/trigwalk/trigseg/pathdecimate/vehsig OK; catalog_story and garden_aboard 0 failures; all four harnesses compile.** **BAT: (1) junction a STATUS spell (Sleep, Blind, Confuse, Silence, Death, Break, Berserk, Zombie, Slow, Stop, Drain, Pain or Bio) to ST-Atk — the grid row should now read "Status attack, Sleep, Sleep 30 percent", and the percentage should track how many of that spell you hold. (2) On the ST-Atk row press confirm and page through the list: the header should be spoken ONCE and never again, every page should still speak its line, and the eligible spells should preview "Sleep 30 percent" rather than "no change". (3) If none of your magic qualifies you should hear that on arrival, once. (4) Re-check a stat row and an Elem-Def row for the same paging behaviour. Grep `[JuncStats] state=` — 60 and 62 should never appear on a line that spoke.**

**Prior (v0.23.0 — #82: FINISHING THE JUNCTION MENU).** Aaron: *"use your offline sim and everything we learned from the Magic submenu to finish up the Junction menu... the character and party abilities list (command abilities are hooked already), junctioning magic to specific stats, junctioning magic to elemental/status attack and defense, and reading stat information... we'll need to think about how to communicate pertinent information to the player without being overwhelming."* Shipped: the junction grid, the magic list with the game's own live preview, the character ability list, the always-on party abilities, the equipped loadout, and number keys 0-9. **NOT OVERWHELMING IS THE DESIGN, NOT AN OMISSION:** 8 elements + 13 statuses on every cursor move would be unusable and A ZERO IS NOT NEWS — resistance rows name only what is non-zero, the preview reports only what MOVES (capped at 4, then ", and more"), and the full 21-entry picture lives behind key 9 on demand; the vocabulary carries meaning without a number where it can (*immune*, *absorbs 30 percent*, *weak 20 percent*). **THREE THINGS THE DISASSEMBLY CORRECTED, AND THE FIRST IS THE ONE TO REMEMBER. (1) STATE 37 IS A SLIDE-IN ANIMATION, NOT THE GRID.** 0x004DB008 walks +0x40 from 0 to 0x1000 and only then hands over. The first draft polled the grid there, which would have announced a row AT MOST ONCE PER VISIT, on whichever frame the poll happened to land — indistinguishable from a flaky hook and undiagnosable from a BAT. The steady grid state is **52 (0x004DB29F)**, the handler that reads the D-pad against the row count, reached via 49→50→51→52. **That is the THIRD time in three builds that the bug was 'a state the game passes THROUGH is not a state the player is ever IN' — so it gets a gate, not another BAT:** the new `tests/menu_junction_compile.cpp` walks all 74 states and asserts exactly two speak, and menu_sim pins 37 as NOT the grid. **(2) THE CHARACTER-ABILITY CURSOR MOVES WITH THE LIST.** 0x004DAE12 addresses it as `module[0x52 + kind]` (kind at +0x274): commands +0x271, character abilities +0x272. The mod read +0x271 unconditionally — **which is precisely why Aaron's sentence reads "command abilities are hooked already"**; browsing character abilities was reading a cursor that had stopped moving. Contents now come from the game's own arrays (0x01D8B258 count 0x01D8B690; 0x01D8B280 count 0x01D8B691; built by 0x004E0110 from the union bitmap 0x01D8B580), and `BuildAbilityRightPanel` is DELETED — **a reconstruction can agree on membership and still disagree on ORDER, and order is the only thing a cursor index means.** **(3) THE ELEMENTAL AND STATUS ROWS ARE LOCKED BY THE SAME MASK AS THE STATS** — 0x004DE531 gates them by 0x200 / 0x400 / 0x6800 / 0x19000, which land on exactly ability ids 10 / 11 / 12,14,15 / 13,16,17 once you subtract one (mask bit N = ability N+1, HP-J is ability 1 at bit 0). Five independent agreements is a derivation; one would have been a coincidence. Without it a character with no Elem-Def-J heard "Elemental defence 1, empty" forever and never learned why. **ELIGIBILITY IS THE MAGIC SCREEN'S GREYED-SPELL PROBLEM AGAIN:** pMenuStateA+0x24A is a 32-bit mask the game builds itself (0x004DE485, asking 0x004C2E50(spellId, slot) about each stock entry) and ineligible spells are drawn **DIM** — invisible to a screen reader — so the list says "no effect here", deliberately distinct from "no change" (legal, but does not help). **THE GRID CURSOR IS NOT THE SLOT NUMBER:** +0x276 is 0..19 and indexes a 20-byte table at 0x00B88604 (`cursor = group*5 + index`, 4 groups; cell 15 duplicates HP and is the blank the game itself skips at 0x004DB314/0x004DB343). Reading it as a slot would put Status-Attack's label on HP-J. **ELEMENTS AND STATUSES ARE DERIVED FROM kernel.bin, NOT LOOKED UP** — the labels here are SPRITES, so unlike the Magic menu there is no in-game string to read back and a wrong ordering would be UNDETECTABLE IN PLAY; every bit is pinned by a spell whose own name states it (Quake→Earth, Break→Petrify, Pain→Poison/Darkness/Silence/Curse, which fixes bit 10). menu_tts_status.inl reached the same orders from live junctions, from a completely different direction, and now shares the tables. **The two 464-byte stat blocks (0x01CFF000 live/preview, 0x01D8B3B0 baseline) are what the game's own arrow compares**, so "Str 42 to 68" costs two reads and no arithmetic; pct = raw − neutral with NO division (800 elem / 100 status), matching menu_tts_status.inl's field-validated scale. **Verified: menu_sim OK (0 bad) — grid table (19 slots reachable exactly once, cell 15 blank), stat/locked/blank rows, elemental + status attack and defence, locked elem/status rows, defence row counts, preview incl. "no effect here" vs "no change", both readouts, the percentage anchors, the number keys, the four steady state numbers with 37 pinned as NOT the grid, and 20,000 randomised views with no overflow. menu_junction_compile OK (0 bad) — 74-state gate, live grid and magic lines through the real offsets, repeat suppression, char id from +0x261 with the cached fallback, loadout and party-ability readouts, per-list cursor offsets. menu_magic_compile OK (0 bad); lint_seh OK (90 files) AND IT CAUGHT A C2712 in JunctionNumberKeys before the build did — second time that check has paid for itself. entryaim/trigwalk/trigseg/pathdecimate/vehsig OK; catalog_story and garden_aboard 0 failures; all harnesses compile.** **FIXED EN ROUTE: tests/catalog_harness.cpp HAS NOT COMPILED SINCE v0.20.29** — it keeps its own copy of `CapturedTriggerLine` and `isCameraTransition` was added to the real struct and not to the copy. A duplicated struct fails by NOT BUILDING, which is a silent failure when nobody runs that harness. **BAT: open Junction, pick a character, choose Junction. (1) Move around the grid — every row should say slot, what is in it, and the number, with locked rows saying "locked" and the blank cell saying "blank"; check the two elemental/status columns as well as the stat page. (2) Confirm on a stat row and move through the magic list — each candidate should say quantity and "Str 42 to 68" or "no change", and any spell the row will not take should say "no effect here". (3) Do the same on an Elem-Def row and listen for "Ice 0 to 50 percent". (4) Press 0, 1, 2-8 and 9 on the grid. (5) Back out to the action row, choose Ability, and move into the CHARACTER ability list (not just commands) — it should now speak; press 0 for the loadout, 1 for party abilities, 2 for the game's description. Grep the log for `[JuncStats] state=` — if state is ever something other than 52 or 59 on a line that spoke, the state gate is wrong; and for `[AbilTTS] RIGHT kind=` to see which list the cursor was in.** **NOT DONE, DELIBERATELY: the commit and unjunction events are not announced as distinct events** (the grid line re-announces on return, so the result is audible); state 38's two options are still read as "GF"/"Magic" by code that predates this investigation, consistent with the entry table at 0x00B8866C but not decoded from mngrp.bin. docs/JUNCTION_MENU.md names both.

**Prior (v0.22.5 — #81: THE LATCH WAS DISARMED BY THE VERY FRAMES IT HAD TO SURVIVE).** **(1) THE ALL TRANSFER WAS STILL SILENT.** v0.22.4's detection-by-effect was right; its CLEARING RULE was not — it disarmed on any poll that was not state 97, and the chain is **99 -> 104 -> 105 -> 96 -> 97** where 104 and 96 both map to MP_NONE. A poll landing on either **disarmed the latch one or two frames BEFORE the transfer it existed to detect.** **And the v0.22.4 test PASSED, because it stepped straight from 99 to 97 and never visited the transient states.** That is the SECOND time in two builds that a green test covered a path the game cannot take (v0.22.1 tested state 105 directly, which the poll can never observe). The latch now survives until the player is demonstrably out of the flow — action row, closing, or a different giver — and menu_sim walks the REAL chain including 104/105/96, asserting the latch is still armed at 104. **(2) THE STEP PROMPT REPEATED WITH EVERY CHARACTER.** v0.22.3 made a character change force the header, which on the All steps prefixed "Select member to receive magic" to every single left/right. **The prompt belongs to the STEP, the name to the LINE:** new `MagicLineNamesCharacter()` suppresses the header on the two All steps and the Exchange partner picker, and deliberately NOT on the spell list or action row, whose lines name nobody and where v0.22.3's fix is still exactly right. Gated both ways. **METHOD RULE, and it is the one to remember from this whole arc: both recent failures had the same shape — a test that CONSTRUCTS a state the game only reaches through a SEQUENCE, without walking the sequence. The tests were not wrong about the logic; they were wrong about the world. Where a transition matters, replay the transition rather than jumping to its endpoints.** **Verified: menu_sim OK (0 bad); menu_magic_compile OK (0 bad); entryaim/trigwalk/trigseg/pathdecimate/vehsig OK; catalog_story and garden_aboard 0 failures; lint_seh OK (89 files); all four harnesses compile.** **BAT: complete one All transfer (expect "All magic moved from X to Y") and cycle a few characters on each step to confirm the prompt is spoken once rather than before every name.**

**Prior (v0.22.4 — #81: THE ALL TRANSFER RAN SILENTLY).** **v0.22.3's character-change fix is CONFIRMED WORKING** — the log shows three consecutive `"Use"` lines at char=2/0/5, which only appear BECAUSE the character changed (the string-equality check suppresses an unchanged line), plus the spell list re-announcing across char=5 -> char=2. **AND THE BAT FINALLY COMPLETED AN ALL TRANSFER, WHICH EXPOSED A REAL DEFECT: THE MOD SAID NOTHING.** Receiver Zell(1), giver Irvine(2); afterwards **Zell's Exchange list reads "Life 75 / Scan 88 / Cure 92" — Irvine's exact loadout from earlier in the SAME log** — and there are ZERO phase=14 announcements. **Cause: state 105 performs the transfer and lasts ONE FRAME.** The chain is 99 -> confirm -> 104 -> 105 -> 96 -> 97 and everything but 99 and 97 is transient, so a polling reader cannot observe it. **v0.22.1 implemented the announcement on state 105 and unit-tested it, and the test PASSED — because a test can hand itself state 105 whenever it likes. The game will not.** **Fixed by watching the EFFECT, not the state:** latch the giver's TOTAL HELD while the giver step is up and announce when it reaches zero; a cancel leaves the total untouched, so the two outcomes separate cleanly without ever seeing the state that did the work. Three details the log itself dictated: the latch **re-snapshots every tick** (the log shows the giver moving Squall -> Irvine, so a once-only latch would have named the wrong character); it requires the same giver on both sides of the comparison; and a giver holding nothing cannot produce a false positive. State **107** also joins 106 as the pre-flight-warning phase (106 opens the box, 107 polls it) for the same one-frame reason. **METHOD RULE EARNED HERE, and it generalises well beyond this screen: a state that only the game can enter TRANSIENTLY cannot be verified by a test that constructs that state directly. Where the effect is observable in the savemap, watch the effect.** **And this is the value of writing down "never observed live" rather than assuming tested code works** — v0.22.3's notes named this exact gap, and one BAT later it contained a bug, a SILENT one that a tester cannot report because nothing happens. **Verified: menu_sim OK (0 bad) with a new block replaying the log (completed transfer, cancel, giver changed mid-step, empty giver); menu_magic_compile OK (0 bad); entryaim/trigwalk/trigseg/pathdecimate/vehsig OK; catalog_story and garden_aboard 0 failures; lint_seh OK (89 files); all four harnesses compile.** **BAT: complete one All transfer and confirm "All magic moved from X to Y"; then cancel out of another and confirm silence. That is the last untested path in the Magic submenu.**

**Prior (v0.22.3 — #81: THE ONE THING THE LOG FOUND THAT THE PLAYER DID NOT).** Aaron BAT'd v0.22.2 clean (*"That worked great! I think it is good to go"*) and asked for a full log review before calling Magic production-ready. **The log is excellent: 1,448 lines, ZERO errors.** The help bar reads the game's own text including the per-spell DESCRIPTIONS it shows on the list ("Revive from KO", "Restore HP", "Scan enemy's HP and weakness", "Fire magic damage/one enemy"); castability is right LIVE (Scan 88 and Thunder 90 say "cannot be cast here", Life 75 and Cure 92 do not — the seven-spell mmagic.bin set behaving exactly as extracted); the Exchange split ran `Fire, move 0, keep 100` -> `move 50, keep 50` and the savemap agrees afterwards (both Zell and Rinoa read Fire 50); the slot formula held through a page change with a STALE cursor (`page=1 cur=3 -> slot 7`), the case the game's own draw path handles identically; and `Magic module at 0x01D76CB8 (pool slot 2)` — the walk CONFIRMS the slot-2 inference rather than depending on it. **THE ONE DEFECT: L1/R1 CHANGES THE CHARACTER WITHOUT LEAVING THE PHASE, so nothing was said about it.** The log has the spell list going `char=2` -> `char=5` announced only as "Cure, quantity 82, slot 1 of 32" — **you would believe you were still looking at Irvine's magic when you were looking at Selphie's.** On a screen whose entire purpose is "whose spells are these and how many" that is the worst thing it could get wrong, **and it is invisible in play because both lines are individually correct** — which is exactly why the log review was worth doing. The action row had the same shape: `char=1` -> `char=5` audible only as the position qualifier quietly disappearing when the enable mask happened to change. A character change is now an ARRIVAL, so the header fires; **and the list header NAMES THE CHARACTER** ("Selphie's magic") instead of the generic "Magic list" that said nothing precisely when it mattered. Which character an announcement is ABOUT differs by phase — the partner panel is about the partner, everything else about the screen's own character — so swapping the partner re-announces in the partner panel and changing it on the action row stays quiet. **A latent bug fell out of writing the test:** the header used the cached `charName` rather than deriving from `charId`, and those can desync because a swap updates charId first — the fixture produced "Squall" where the character was Selphie. It now derives from charId and cannot desync. **Cosmetic, deliberately not fixed:** the help bar renders the game's interpuncts as spaces ("Rearrange in order of Restore Attack Indirect"). That is the game's own text, it reads fine aloud, and special-casing the character inside the SHARED decoder would risk every other help string for no gain. **NOT EXERCISED IN ANY BAT YET: an All transfer has never been COMPLETED in a log** — the v0.22.2 run reached the receiver step and backed out, and the "works as expected" confirmation came from play rather than a log line. States 105 (transfer) and 106 (pre-flight warning) are implemented and unit-tested but never observed live. Worth one run when convenient; not a blocker. **Verified: menu_sim OK (0 bad) with a new block replaying the log's own transitions (spell-list swap, action-row swap, partner swap, and the case that must NOT fire); menu_magic_compile OK (0 bad); entryaim/trigwalk/trigseg/pathdecimate/vehsig OK; catalog_story and garden_aboard 0 failures; lint_seh OK (89 files); all four harnesses compile.** **BAT: short one — in the spell list press L1/R1 to change character and confirm it now says whose magic you are looking at; same on the action row. Then Magic is done.**

**Prior (v0.22.2 — #81: THE GARBLED HELP BAR, AND ONE NAME TOO MANY).** BAT of v0.22.1: **"All now works as expected"** — which settles the direction correction (step 1 receiver, step 2 giver) that contradicted Aaron's original description. Two defects left. **(1) HELP TEXT WAS GARBLED THROUGHOUT — "Use magic" was read aloud as "AaI'UEIOE".** `FF8TextDecode::DecodeMenuText` indexes its 224-entry glyph table with a **GLYPH INDEX**, because its existing caller hands it the GCW buffer, which the RENDERER has already converted. mngrp.bin is one stage earlier and holds **TEXT-STREAM bytes**, and a text byte is `glyph + 0x20` — so every character came out 32 slots too high. Proof from the real file (group 8 string 1 = `59 71 63 20 6B 5F 65 67 61`): indexed raw -> `AaI'UEIOE`; minus 0x20 -> `Use magic`. **The v0.22.1 comment that the bar and the scrape "were always reading the same encoding, just at different points in the pipeline" was HALF RIGHT AND THEREFORE WRONG — same TABLE, different OFFSET, and the offset was the whole bug.** Method note, third time this pattern has cost a build: **a confident sentence asserting two things are equivalent is where these keep coming from.** New `MagicTextToGlyphs()` in the model does the shift and drops control codes plus the parameter byte of a 0x0A..0x0E variable (the game's own strings contain `Can't carry {0A}5`); **DecodeMenuText is LEFT ALONE — its other callers pass glyph indices and are correct.** **(2) EXCHANGE REPEATED THE PARTNER'S NAME ON EVERY SLOT.** The owner prefix now lives in the PHASE HEADER ("Zell's magic"), spoken once on arrival in a panel, never on the per-slot line — the panel cannot change while you move within it, so the name is news exactly once. v0.22.1 put it on every line for a real reason (two lists on one screen, "Cure, quantity 47" ambiguous between them); the header solves that without the repetition. **NEW GATE, AND WHAT IT CAN ACTUALLY PROVE: menu_sim now decodes THREE byte sequences lifted VERBATIM out of mngrp.bin** ("Use magic" and both All step prompts) **and asserts the UN-SHIFTED reading is WRONG** — a fixture that decodes correctly either way proves nothing. menu_magic_compile's stub decoder is now the exact inverse of the shift, and its header states plainly that it checks CONTROL FLOW (pointer read, sentinels, fault path, declining off-screen) and NOT encoding: **a stub cannot falsify its own convention; only the real bytes can.** **Verified: menu_sim OK (0 bad); menu_magic_compile OK (0 bad); entryaim/trigwalk/trigseg/pathdecimate/vehsig OK; catalog_story and garden_aboard 0 failures; lint_seh OK (89 files); all four harnesses compile.** **BAT: press "/" on the action row, the spell list, the sort popup and both Exchange panels — it should now be the game's own words. Then walk Exchange and check the partner is named once per panel rather than once per slot.**

**Prior (v0.22.1 — #81: THE FOUR MAGIC BUGS).** v0.22.0 BAT-confirmed Use and Rearrange clean; these are the other four Aaron reported. **(1) "Magic list" was spoken on EVERY page turn** — paging runs 13 -> 14/16 -> 13 and the poll recorded the TRANSIENT phase, so the return looked like a fresh arrival. It now keeps the last SPOKEN phase; header once, on arrival. **(2) "/" DID NOTHING on the Magic screen** — AnnounceHelpText scrapes the RENDERED text from the GCW buffer hunting a dash separator or a known prefix, and the Magic bar has neither, so the key was silently a no-op. It never needed the scrape: **the module caches a pointer to the exact string the bar draws** (module +0x24; draw 0x004F6996 pushes callback 0x004F70C0 with ctx &module[+0x24]), and the getter 0x004BD630 does NO copying — it returns a pointer straight into the loaded mngrp.bin. Raw NUL-terminated FF8 bytes in long-lived data written with one aligned dword store, so cross-thread reads are safe. NULL / 0x01D7714C (getter fallback) / 0x01CFF84C (empty-string constant) = no text. **Stated not hidden: the game does not refresh +0x24 in target select (20) or All step 2 (99), so those read slightly STALE text — never wrong text.** **(3) EXCHANGE WAS ENTIRELY SILENT** — states 26 (your list) / 28 (choose partner) / 44 (their list) / 52,55 (Give All, Take All, Split) / 63 (quantity split). Both columns share the ONE per-character cursor array at +0x38; column B pages on **+0x46**, so partner slot = `(cursor[B] & 3) + pageB*4`. Split counters **+0x58 / +0x59**, sum conserved, 0..100, spell id +0x5A. **Each panel now names WHOSE list it is** — two lists on one screen and "Cure, quantity 47" spoken identically for both was the exact ambiguity that made it unusable; the split reads BOTH counters because the two numbers move in opposite directions. **(4) ALL WAS CONFUSING — AND THE DIRECTION IS THE REVERSE OF AARON'S DESCRIPTION.** He reported give-then-receive; **state 97 picks the RECEIVER and state 99 the GIVER.** Proof: state 105 calls `0x004F5FA0(arg1=+0x62, arg2=+0x64)` where 0x4C2C70 ADDS arg1's spells to arg2 and 0x4C2D50 REMOVES them from arg1 — so the step-2 character loses and step-1 gains; and step 2's mask is `available & ~(1 << +0x64)`, which only makes sense if step 1 already fixed a role. **The game's own help bar agrees**: group 8 entry 12 on step 1 = "Select member to receive magic", entry 13 on step 2 = "Select member to transfer magic" — so the mod speaks the game's wording VERBATIM rather than an interpretation. Announcing "choose who gives" on the receiver step would have been an accessibility bug dressed as a feature. No Yes/No confirm in this flow: the second confirm runs a pre-flight and either commits (105) or opens a warning box (106); both announced. **ROOT CAUSE OF (3) AND (4): v0.22.0 routed on `screenMode == 3`, and BOTH flows use mode 3 AND mode 4 — so Exchange and All were literally indistinguishable and neither could be narrated. Routing is now on the STATE number, which for every screen here is reached from nowhere else in the machine.** **lint_seh CAUGHT A C2712 BEFORE MSVC COULD** — the first draft of the help reader mixed `__try` with a `std::string`, which MSVC rejects (no object unwinding in an SEH frame). The raw copy is now its own frame. That gate paid for itself. **Verified: menu_sim OK (0 bad) — new: the paging regression (one header, five slot lines across five pages), both Exchange panels naming their owner, column B's independent page, both popups, the split's paired counters, the All direction pinned in both steps AND in the transfer line, and a routing test proving screen mode alone cannot separate the two flows; fuzz extended to all eleven speaking phases. menu_magic_compile OK (0 bad) — new: the help-bar reader against a real mapped module, both "no text" sentinels, and declining off the Magic screen (so the GCW scrape still runs everywhere else). entryaim/trigwalk/trigseg/pathdecimate/vehsig OK; catalog_story and garden_aboard 0 failures; lint_seh OK (89 files); all four harnesses compile.** **BAT: page through the spell list (header once); press "/" on the action row, the list and the sort popup; run Exchange end to end including Split; and on All, listen to which step says "receive" and which says "transfer" and confirm the transfer runs that way. If it does not, the two group-8 strings are mislabelled BY THE GAME and that is itself the finding.**

**Prior (v0.22.0 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #81: THE MAGIC SUBMENU).** Aaron's overnight task. Action row, the 32-slot spell list, cast target select, the sort popup and the discard confirmation, plus an OFFLINE SIMULATOR for the main menu and a bug it found in Ability. **THE STRUCTURAL FINDING, WHICH MATTERS BEYOND THIS SCREEN: everything this mod has called `pMenuStateA + 0x2xx` for two years is a field of a MENU MODULE OBJECT.** Pool at 0x01D76BC8, stride 0x78, 10 slots, MRU list head 0x01D76B48, allocator 0x004BE540, header {next, prev, update fn, draw fn, state u16, in-use u8}. Main menu = slot 1, open submenu = slot 2 — so **`+0x22E` ("focus") is slot2+0x10, the STATE WORD of a 114-state machine** (hence 3/13/20/72: they are jump-table indices), and **`+0x230` ("phase") is slot 2's IN-USE FLAG and never was a phase.** Slot 2 is an allocation-order COINCIDENCE, so the hook WALKS THE LIST and matches Magic by update fn **0x004F02F0** (submenu dispatch table 0x00B87ED8 idx 3 — whose Status/Save/Switch/Junction indices match the +0x1E8 values the mod already observed, confirming the identification from two independent directions). **THE SLOT FORMULA — the one computation that decides whether the right spell is named: `slot = (module[0x38+charId] & 3) + module[0x42]*4`.** 1 col x 4 rows x 8 pages; the cursor is stored PER CHARACTER and lags one frame after a page change, so only its low 2 bits are trustworthy (the game's own draw path masks it the same way at 0x004F0597). **TRAP AVOIDED: pMenuStateA+0x272 is the ITEM menu's list cursor** — in the Magic module that byte is written only during the post-sort redraw, so reading it would have named a plausible but wrong spell on every other frame. **LABELS ARE DECODED, NOT GUESSED** — from Data/lang-en/menu.fs -> mngrp.bin section 1 bank 8 (the "group 8" the code passes to 0x004BD630), via the entry tables the code itself indexes (0x00B88A90 action row = pairs {0,1,11,2}; 0x00B88A9C sort = pairs {15..21}): **Use / Exchange / All / Rearrange** + 7 sort orders. That also settles what disassembly could NOT: action 1 = Exchange (one member), action 2 = All (everyone). Screen says "Exchg."; the mod says "Exchange" (an abbreviation is a pixel constraint, meaningless aloud), and the sort interpuncts are read as "then". **Uncastable spells are GREYED NOT HIDDEN** (draw 0x004F71DB picks colour 1 vs 7) — invisible to a blind player and the difference between a spell that casts and one that silently refuses, so the list says "cannot be cast here", **in the Use flow only** (in Exchange/Rearrange every spell is fair game and the qualifier would be a lie). mmagic.bin (ptr 0x01D2BB10, stride 4, byte0 bit0) gives exactly Cure/Cura/Curaga/Life/Full-Life/Esuna/Dispel — extracted offline, and exactly what FF8 allows. **NEW ARCHITECTURE WORTH COPYING: the wording lives in src/menu_magic_model.inl as PURE FUNCTIONS of a MagicView struct** (no Win32, no SEH, no absolute addresses); src/menu_tts_magic.inl is responsible only for finding the module and reading bytes. Every previous submenu put both in one file, so the only way to test the wording was to play it — which is why Junction is still "partially" done after two years. **tests/menu_sim.cpp drives all six phases offline and CAUGHT A REAL DEFECT ON ITS FIRST RUN** ("No target, 4 of 3" when the target cursor ran past the character mask). MenuSim models the MODULE POOL + a state machine, not "the Magic screen", so Ability/GF/Config can reuse it. tests/menu_magic_compile.cpp is the only pre-MSVC syntax check the hook gets and runs the module walk against a REAL MAPPED POOL (all 10 slots + out-of-pool, misaligned and cyclic lists). **BUG FIXED EN ROUTE: menu_tts_ability.inl's spell table listed Float/Drain/Pain at ids 38/39/40 — those are Blind/Confuse/Sleep** (Float 47, Drain 44, Pain 45), so an Ability refine preview yielding any of the three read a DIFFERENT spell's stock and spoke that number, on the one line whose whole job is "you already have N of these". Canonical 57-entry table now in menu_magic_model.inl; the cross-check that makes it trustworthy is that mmagic.bin's usable bit lands on exactly the 7 castable spells, which a wrong-id table could not produce. **WHAT THE OFFLINE GATES CANNOT DO: prove an ADDRESS is right.** If an offset is wrong the model and the simulator are wrong together and both pass. They prove that GIVEN the right bytes the mod says the right words in every phase at every cursor position. Addresses are what the BAT is for. **Verified: menu_sim OK (0 bad) — 16 spell-id anchors, the slot formula over all 32 slots with a deliberately stale cursor, list wording incl. empties and the greyed qualifier, the castable set + the 0x40 lock, the action row under 4 enable masks, target select over a sparse mask, the sort popup, the discard confirmation and its precedence, phase routing for 11 states, the module walk in all 10 slots, and 20,000 randomised states with no overflow and no silent speaking phase. menu_magic_compile OK (0 bad); entryaim/trigwalk/trigseg/pathdecimate/vehsig OK; catalog_story and garden_aboard 0 failures; lint_seh OK (89 files); all four harnesses compile.** **BAT: open Magic, pick a character, then — action row left/right; Use -> the spell list (up/down in a page, left/right to page, check a Cure reads normally and a Meltdown says "cannot be cast here"); confirm a castable spell for target select; Rearrange for the 7 sort orders; and if willing, trigger a discard and listen to the confirmation before answering. Grep the log for `[MagicTTS] Magic module at 0x… (pool slot N)` — if N is not 2 the module walk just earned its keep — and for the per-line `[MagicTTS] phase=… -> slot N : "…"`, which names the slot the mod thought it read if a spell name is wrong.** **NOT DONE, DELIBERATELY: the two-column Exchange/All screens** (sub-flows "Give All", "Split", "Take All"). v0.22.0 announces which member is being chosen (screen mode 3) then goes quiet on the two-column screen (mode 4) rather than read a layout it has not verified; docs/MAGIC_MENU.md names the offsets to check first.

**Prior (v0.21.8 — **BAT-CONFIRMED 2026-08-16: all four testable retargets arrived — Deling City at 37 units, the Tomb at 2, Winhill and Trabia in; zero real errors in 27,878 lines** — #79: THREE THINGS THE BAT LOG SAID).** **(1) TRABIA ENTERED AT dist=1360 AND THAT WAS THE TELL — ITS DOOR IS TWICE THE SIZE THE TABLE CLAIMED.** v0.21.7 restricted the aim to segment 149 (prog 3, dest 19, no bound) as the conservative choice, but **prog 4 grants the SAME dest 19 in segment 150 whenever Yoff>4096**, and the patch runs straight across the seam — 194 walkable entry tris in 149 and **172 more in 150**. He entered at (49966,-58834) — Yoff 6702, segment 150 — **1,363u from the aim and 818u OUTSIDE its bbox**, getting in only by passing through the eastern half en route to the western one. Taking both halves centres the aim in the real 2048x2048 door and raises the edge margin **418 -> 737, the largest in the table**; the whole rectangle is Yoff 5120..7168, entirely above the split. **Being conservative about a clause is right; being conservative about which HALF of a door to aim at is just aiming at the far side.** **(2) THE D-DISTRICT PRISON DOOR NEVER OPENS.** Aaron: *"it is not possible to enter the D-District Prison. You can see it on the world map but can never enter it from the world map. It is entered via a story sequence triggered in Deling City."* The geometry is REAL and correct — prog 16, 260 walkable entry polys, story>=350, and the drive went onto them — and no field loads. The prison is a mobile drilling rig that relocates and sinks, so its world-map presence is most likely vestigial by disc 3. **THE ROW STAYS DELIBERATELY** for the two reasons he asked for: the drive still delivers the player to the gate for an equivalent experience, and the planner still routes other journeys around a real firing area. **Written down so a future "arrived but nothing loaded" here is NOT read as a regression and re-investigated from scratch.** **(3) WINHILL HAS TWO DOORS AND v0.21.7 PICKED THE RIGHT ONE.** Aaron: *"the one that auto-drive took me to now is the one in the north part of town."* That is dest 14, the high side of the Xoff 6144 split, field 0x029E — the half the v0.21.6 whole-patch aim would have missed. Dest 15 is the other door, uncatalogued by his call. Bbox now from triangle VERTICES rather than the raster samples the aim search uses (raster under-reports by up to one step); west edge Xoff 6145, one unit inside the clause, since exactly 6144 satisfies neither `>6144` nor `<6143` and would open no door at all. **NEW GATE, WITH AN HONEST BOUND: the four positions where the game actually let him through must sit within the 200u position-SAMPLING LAG of their own area — NOT strict containment**, because `lastPos` on the arrival line is the drive's last sample and not the frame the engine ran its polygon test on; checked against raw triangles all four are 59-166u outside one, so asserting containment would assert something the instrument cannot measure. **Verified to FAIL on the v0.21.7 Trabia row at 818u**, so it is a real gate and not a rubber stamp. **CHECKED AND NOT A BUG:** the 9 km prison drive's four [NAVBLK-PRUNE] releases, two on learned blocks at the Tomb 30 km away — the prune picks the block NEAREST THE START and keeps anything FootBlockedCached calls genuinely blocked, so it released the two actually near the route first, then the only others left, and the plan succeeded. Recorded so the next reader does not re-open it. **Loose end, cosmetic:** the arrival line named `glrent1` for BOTH Deling City (field 0x02EE) and the Tomb (0x0318), and gave an EMPTY name for Winhill (0x029E) — two ids should not resolve to one name. Ids are right and nothing depends on the string; the field-name lookup is worth a look sometime. **Verified: entryaim_test OK (0 bad) at 22 rows; trigwalk_test OK; trigseg_test OK; pathdecimate_test OK; vehsig_test OK; catalog_story_test 0 failures; garden_aboard_test 0 failures; lint_seh OK (88 files); all four harnesses compile.** **BAT: Trabia Garden only — it is the one coordinate that changed. Approaching from the east should now enter at a much smaller dist. Nothing else in this build alters a drive; Winhill's bbox moved two units and the rest is comments.**

**Prior (v0.21.7 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #79: THE FIVE HELD-BACK RETARGETS, RESOLVED OFFLINE).** Aaron: *"Can you figure out those five retargets offline using your simulator and available data?"* Yes — and not by measuring distances more carefully. **v0.21.6 bound patches to destinations by NEAREST CENTROID, which is a guess. The authority is: a patch sits in a SEGMENT, a segment has exactly ONE entry PROGRAM, and that program names its DESTINATIONS with clause bounds.** Deling City seg 264 / prog 8 / dest 8 / story>=333; D-District Prison 361/16/10/>=350; Trabia Garden 149/3/19/>=750; Winhill 393/24/dest 14|15/>=750; Great Salt Lake 373/21/24/>=1600. **The first four markers are IN the same segment as their own patch** — icon and door in one 8192u square with one program — so the binding is a fact, not an inference, and the 1-2 km is just how far a city icon sits from its gate. **WINHILL WAS THE ONE WORTH HOLDING BACK: program 24 SPLITS segment 393 at Xoff 6144 (dest 14 high, 15 low), the marker is at Xoff 7059, and the v0.21.6 whole-patch aim came out at Xoff 5825 — the LOW half, a DIFFERENT DESTINATION.** Shipping it would have been a confidently-wrong coordinate wearing a generated-therefore-trustworthy label. New aim AND its whole bbox clear the split (west edge Xoff 6147); the low half is left uncovered so the planner does not treat it as a no-go zone. **GREAT SALT LAKE vs CHOCOBO FOREST 3 SETTLED:** prog 21 has NO chocobo clause and a story>=1600 gate, while every forest program (1,2,5,31,35,36 = forests 2,1,4,5,7,6) has veh-49 and NO story gate — so segment 373 is Great Salt Lake, which fits a post-Lunar-Cry location. **Chocobo Forest 3's marker is in segment 374, which has NO walkable entry polygon at all** — that marker is wrong; the only unclaimed forest-shaped program is prog 14 (seg 279, split at Xoff 2560, dests 22/23, patches at (59348,-31753) and (61205,-30894)) which is 29 km away, so it is a CANDIDATE, not a conclusion. Not touched — guessing is what cost five builds at Edea's House. **footOnly is now DERIVED, not judged:** a veh-132 (FOOT_ALT) clause = car-enterable. Reproduces all seven hand-set flags (Dollet/Balamb Town false; Timber/Fire Cavern/Galbadia Station true) and makes Deling City and D-District Prison car-enterable, which v0.21.6's blanket `true` had wrong. **Confirmed in INSTRUCTIONS: sub_544630 @0x544702 copies bit 3 of the polygon you LEFT FROM into the UNK21 bit** (`mov cl,[ecx+0xe]; shr cl,3; and cl,1; ... mov [eax+0x6d],cl`) — the v0.21.6 BAT's re-entry-inhibit reading, now from the exe rather than from behaviour. **Also mapped: 20 of 38 programs are now bound; the 18 unbound are tabulated in the doc.** Three worth a later pass, all story-gated past the current save: FOUR programs share destination 26 (segs 406/407/438/439, >=1750) which is what Esthar City's four gates should look like (its marker is in seg 374 with the other two strays); Sorceress Memorial (prog 29, seg 441, 248 tris) and Tears' Point (prog 32, seg 506) each have their marker in the RIGHT segment with a real patch, so both are straightforward retargets at disc 4; and Galbadia Missile Base (prog 15, seg 327, story>=350) has an entry program but NO entry polygons in its segment — combined with Aaron's *"the base blows up after the mission on disc two"*, the likely explanation is that the world map swaps terrain blocks by story state and wmx.obj holds the post-destruction version. **Verified: entryaim_test OK (0 bad) at 22 rows — THREE NEW ASSERTIONS: Winhill's aim AND its whole bbox clear the Xoff 6144 split and are not the v0.21.6 coordinate; the four same-segment bindings still aim inside their own marker's segment; plus the existing invariants. trigwalk_test OK; trigseg_test OK; pathdecimate_test OK; vehsig_test OK; catalog_story_test 0 failures; garden_aboard_test 0 failures; lint_seh OK (88 files); all four harnesses compile.** **BAT: four of the five are testable at story 912 — Deling City (>=333), D-District Prison (>=350), Trabia Garden (>=750) and WINHILL (>=750), the interesting one since its aim moved to the other half of a split square. Great Salt Lake is gated at >=1600 and cannot be confirmed until much later; it ships because it is strictly better than the marker it replaces and cannot regress anything that already refuses. Read the `fieldName` on Winhill's `[DRIVE] Arrival via game-mode` line — it names which of destinations 14 and 15 the high side actually is.**

**Prior (v0.21.6 — **BAT-CONFIRMED 2026-08-16: `ehenter2` entered at 171 units from the aim after five builds of refusing, and Chocobo Forest 7 — the control whose aim moved 728u — at **34 units**, dead-on rather than lucky; zero errors, 51 `[ENTRYPATCH]` lines all correct** — #79: EDEA'S HOUSE — THE DOOR IS SEVEN TRIANGLES WIDE).** **TWO THINGS THE BAT SETTLED. (1) `0xFF21` (UNK21) IS A RE-ENTRY INHIBIT, NOT A WORLD-STATE LOCK.** Exactly one tick in the run refused program 34 — `unk21 bit=1` at 00:39:53, the frame he came back OUT of `ehenter2` still standing on the entry polygon; a second later he had stepped off and the bit was 0. v0.21.2 and v0.21.3 both carried "UNK21 might be holding the door shut" as a live hypothesis; it never was, and the gate is proven correct because it bit once, exactly where the engine needs it to. **(2) GALBADIA MISSILE BASE'S MISSING ENTRY POLYGONS ARE CORRECT** — Aaron: *"the base blows up after the mission on disc two, so there is no way to enter it at this point in the game."* Open question closed. **Known imprecision, observed and benign:** `[ENTRYPATCH]` read "standing INSIDE" for four ticks at Chocobo 7 before the field loaded — the bbox is a rectangle and therefore a superset of the triangles, and/or the engine wants a step ONTO a polygon rather than a standstill; aim margin plus a moving drive covers it, but do not read one INSIDE line as "it should have opened this instant".  **The refusal was never a story gate, a master gate or the destination table. It was 234 units of ground.** `sub_545EA0` opens with `mov eax,[0x020409FC]; test byte ptr [eax+0x0E], 8` — **that pointer is the POLYGON UNDER THE PLAYER.** It churned every frame because the polygon under your feet changes every frame; v0.21.3 read that as a broken instrument and retracted it, and it was measuring exactly the right thing and reading it correctly — **bit 3 clear, everywhere Aaron walked.** Being an entry polygon is not enough: it must ALSO be foot-walkable, and that is a different byte (`0x0F` bit 7, the move validator's flag the navmesh feed already uses). Map-wide there are **7,359 entry polygons and 2,877 foot-walkable ones**, and the gap is where this bug lived. **Segment 652 holds 103 entry polygons. SEVEN are foot-walkable** — the other 96 are the orphanage building — covering `x[-29975,-29310] y[69632,70078]`, about 665×446 units, roughly **a three-hundredth of the segment.** The shipped marker `(-28950,70090)` was **234 units east of it, against the wall**: v0.21.1's page-8 archaeology found the right 103-polygon structure and then aimed at the wrong face (*"east is the side the player arrives from"* — east is where the wall is). In the 2026-08-16 BAT the closest approach was `(-29144,70004)`, **287 units short**, with every gate the exe names passing on every frame because they all genuinely did pass. New aim **(-29459,69772)** — the interior point of the seven triangles farthest from their edge, 140 units of margin in every direction. **WHY v0.21.5 SAID MATCH ANYWAY:** programs 34/35 carry no coordinate clauses, so `[TRIGWALK]`'s finest positional unit is the 8192u segment — it printed `MATCH, destination 18` at twenty-three positions spanning 8 km while nothing loaded, and told him *"every condition the exe names is satisfied and the refusal is elsewhere."* **That read as a finding and was a blind spot with a confident sentence attached.** The sentence is gone; the polygon gate now gets its own `[ENTRYPATCH]` line beside every verdict. **THE UNCOMFORTABLE PART: `world_map_trigger_data.inl` has said “a field entry fires only while standing on a wmx poly with byte14 bit 3 set” since v0.18.3.206.** Three sessions then hunted the exe for a gate already documented in the file. The table was never wrong — **it was 7 rows long and Edea's House was not one of them.** **Method rule earned here: when a decode is confidently wrong, check whether an earlier decode in the same file already said the right thing before re-reading the exe.** The table is now GENERATED by `offline/gen_entryaims.py` (entry polys → keep foot-walkable → flood-fill into patches → aim = interior point farthest from the edge), and it reproduces the five aims found BY HAND IN THE FIELD to **Timber 91u, Dollet 54u, Balamb Town 47u, Fire Cavern 83u, Galbadia Station 379u — all inside their own proven bboxes**; Balamb Town's proven box turns out to be 43 entry polys of which 7 are walkable, the same 103/7 shape as Edea's. **Ten new firing areas shipped** (Edea's House, Tomb, Centra Ruins, Shumi, Chocobo Forests 1/2/4/5/6/7 — every marker within 800u of its own door); the seven hand-proven rows untouched. **Five held back for their own BAT** (Deling City 1,966u, Great Salt Lake 1,717u, D-District Prison 1,462u, Trabia 1,443u, Winhill 1,234u), plus two open questions: Galbadia Missile Base has NO entry polygon within 17 km though it is walked into, and Great Salt Lake / Chocobo Forest 3 resolve to the same patch. See `docs/WORLDMAP_ENTRY_POLYGONS.md`. Side effect worth having: planner2 prices non-target firing areas at +4096, so 7→17 areas means ten more real doors it routes AROUND — accidental field entry while passing a town should get rarer. **Verified: entryaim_test OK (0 bad) — NEW GATE: every aim inside its own bbox, names unique, all five field-proven points still inside their areas, Edea's bbox asserted to be the seven walkable triangles with all four historical positions outside it, the Forest 7 control, and no two firing areas overlapping (they double as no-go zones, so an overlap would make a destination permanently expensive). trigwalk_test OK (0 bad); trigseg_test OK (0 bad); pathdecimate_test OK (0 bad); vehsig_test OK (0 bad); catalog_story_test 0 failures; garden_aboard_test 0 failures; lint_seh OK (88 files); world_map_harness, garden_harness, chase_harness and minigame_bgbtl_compile all compile.** **BAT: walk to Edea's House — that is the test. Then re-walk Chocobo Forest 7, the control, whose aim moved 728u (it worked before by luck of the arrival radius, and should now work by aim). If the door still refuses, the `[ENTRYPATCH]` line says whether he was standing on entry ground when it happened.**

**Prior (v0.21.5 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #79: THE ENTRY INTERPRETER RE-IMPLEMENTED FROM THE EXE AND RUN LIVE).** **Reading single addresses from the mod thread has been wrong TWICE** — the .21.3 "master gate" (`test byte ptr [eax+0x0E], 8`) reads a PER-FRAME POINTER that churns every tick (01FAACD8, 01FAC720, 01FB6528, 0201A878...) and printed `bit3=CLEAR` on every line INCLUDING WHILE AN ENTRY SUCCEEDED. A sampled address proves nothing when you cannot tell a stable value from a transient. **The control experiment is what proves things: Chocobo Forest 7, the segment next door, OPENED ON DEMAND** — so the walker runs, entries work, and Edea's House alone is refused. This build evaluates the WHOLE 38-program set with the semantics from the dispatch tables at 0x546CAC/0x546D3C, giving a known-good and a known-bad through IDENTICAL CODE. **RESULT:** `Chocobo Forest 7 -> program 35, destination 38 (the game agrees)` and **`Edea's House -> program 34, MATCH, destination 18`.** Every condition the exe names is satisfied at the position the screenshot puts at the orphanage wall. **That moves the search off the CONDITIONS and onto what happens after them: `sub_545EA0` extracts the destination and hands it to `sub_544630` — DESTINATION 18 and the table that turns an id into a field are the next thing to decode.** New `[TRIGWALK]` line once a second and at every sweep start, reporting only the programs covering the player's current square. Gates proven to bite (story 899 fails the window, UNK21 bit 1 fails, the skip bypasses, a Ragnarok fails the vehicle clause, the old marker fails the segment) so a MATCH means something. **Verified: trigwalk_test OK (0 bad) — NEW GATE. trigseg_test needed a one-line stub once TriggerEvalTick gained the walk call and CAUGHT THAT AS A LINK ERROR IMMEDIATELY. pathdecimate_test, vehsig_test, catalog_story_test, garden_harness, aboard_test, world_map_harness, minigame_bgbtl_compile, lint_seh (88 files) all pass. READ-ONLY — no steering, planning or catalog logic changed.** **BAT: stand at Edea's House a few seconds, then at Chocobo Forest 7 a few seconds, send ff8_world.log. Both MATCH => the model is right and the difference is downstream in the destination table. Disagreement => the model is wrong and the line names the clause.**

**Prior (v0.21.4 — **BAT-CONFIRMED 2026-08-16: Chocobo Forest 7 and then a THIRTY-ONE KILOMETRE single leg from the Galbadia Missile Base to the Tomb of the Unknown King, both arrived** — the longest foot drive this mod has completed, and exactly the length class the silent truncation used to eat. **The missing SFX in that session was NOT this build: the ducker was balanced 82/82, both audio hooks installed cleanly, volumes sat at configured values, and a restart brought SFX back with no mod change.** Worth knowing anyway, from reading it: **`SfxVolumeUp`/`Down` only push the value to the game `if (s_sfxHookInstalled)`** — otherwise they update the number, SPEAK it, and save it to config while changing nothing, so the announcement can never tell the player the control is inert. Originally (NOT MSVC-built — #70: THE FOOT AUTO-DRIVE REVIEWED AGAINST THE GARDEN SYSTEM, AND FIVE DEFECTS IT WAS CARRYING).** Aaron: *"I suspect the walking auto drive system could be improved based on the findings from the B-Garden system research."* Two full read-throughs (planner + executor) written up with file:line evidence in **docs/FOOT_AUTODRIVE_REVIEW.md** and the project doc `claude/FOOT_AUTODRIVE_REVIEW.md`. **SHIPPED, the five that are unambiguous:** **(1) A CUTSCENE IS NOT A STALL.** The Garden has paused on an open dialog since .20.87; `IsDialogOpen()` was declared for that fix and **the foot executor never called it.** The freeze detector fires at ~0.65 s and its FIRST ACT is `CamwLearnBlock`, writing into an overlay that is DELIBERATELY PERMANENT — so every world-map cutscene libelled open ground forever, then pressed keys into a locked engine. Now pauses, releases keys, and on resume bumps `s_driveWatchdogGen` (the mechanism .18.3.216 already built for battles). **(2) THE OVERLAY WENT SILENTLY STERILE AT 256.** `AddNavBlock` returning false is also the "no new knowledge" signal that drives the fence-inflation recovery — whose job is to add blocks. At 256 every recovery was a no-op and every replan sterile, unlogged. Now evicts oldest-first and logs. **(3) THE GRID PLANNER TRUNCATED LONG ROUTES AND MARKED THEM PLANNED.** A 1,500-cell route kept 768 waypoints and **ended 732 cells short in open country**, no log line. Now decimates and always keeps the goal. **(4) `s_drivePathWorld` NEVER RESET BETWEEN DRIVES** — it gates the whole route-watchdog block and is ALSO written by the Garden executor, so whether a foot drive had watchdogs could depend on the previous drive. **(5) DIAGNOSTICS SHIPPED ON** — `WM_MOTION_DIAG` wrote one UNTHROTTLED synchronous log line per poll tick (120-180 file writes/second, every drive); `DRIVE_STEER_DIAG`'s own comment said "set false before push". Both off. **⚠ METHOD NOTE, AND THE REVIEW WAS WRONG ABOUT ITS OWN TOP ITEM.** It ranked "the LOS clamp and lookahead are computed then discarded — one line to fix" first. The discard is real but **DELIBERATE AND DOCUMENTED**: v0.18.3.155 records the far selector oscillating between adjacent corridor cells, the character limit-cycling, and the offline sim carrying it 20 km with sequential follow vs ~700u with the selector. **An audit that reads only the code will recommend reverting fixes whose reasons live in the comments.** The real fix is the Garden's commitment+LOS pair behind a harness — item 4 in the review's order of work, not a one-liner. **Verified: pathdecimate_test OK (0 bad) — NEW GATE, and it earned its place immediately: the FIRST version of the decimation fix picked stride 2 for a 1,536-cell route, every stride-aligned index came out odd, and the goal was never emitted. The test caught it before the build left the machine.** Also trigseg_test, vehsig_test, catalog_story_test, garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad, aboard_test, world_map_harness, minigame_bgbtl_compile 0/0, lint_seh OK (88 files). **BAT: drive somewhere long on foot — the log should be MUCH quieter (no [MFRAME], no [YAWDRIVE]). Walk into a world-map cutscene if you can: it should say "cutscene ... pausing; this is not a stall" instead of learning a phantom obstacle. Items 2-5 are fault-path only; a clean drive executes none of them.**

**Prior (v0.21.2 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #79: THE TRIGGER DECODE WAS WRONG, AND THE EXE SAYS SO).** Aaron's F11 shot shows **Squall standing beside the orphanage LIGHTHOUSE** — the marker is right, the door is not opening for another reason. **THE OPCODE MEANINGS ARE THE OTHER WAY ROUND.** `sub_546100` dispatches through the jump table at **0x546CAC** indexed by the byte map at **0x546D3C**; read both out of the exe and the research doc this module was built on does not survive: **`0xFF06` is NOT a location/field id — it is a SEGMENT TEST** (slot 2 -> 0x00546192, which calls **sub_553910**: `row=((Y+0x48000) mod 0x30000)>>13, col=((X+0x60000)&0x3FFFF)>>13, index=row*32+col` — 32x24 squares of 8192); **`0xFF08` is the DESTINATION id, not a region test**; and **`0xFF0F/10/11/12` are the X/Y bounds inside the segment** (0x005463A7 Xoff<, 0x00546406 Yoff<, 0x00546461 Xoff>, 0x005464C0 Yoff>). The transcription reproduces the mod's OWN arithmetic: (-23150,62853) prints as `seg(13,19)` in every PLAN-DEBUG line and 19*32+13 = **621**. **RE-DECODED, PROGRAM 34 IS EDEA'S HOUSE:** `SEGMENT==652` (x[-32768,-24576] y[65536,73728]), `STORY>=900`, `UNK21 0`, on foot -> `DEST 18`. **Aaron's story is 912, he is on foot, and the lighthouse (-29585,70739) is INSIDE that box — every condition the mod can model passes.** And **program 32, which .21.1 reported as the story-locked Edea's House entrance, is SEGMENT==506 = x[81920,90112] y[24576,32768] — the far EAST of the map.** My "the game has the door locked" call two builds ago rode on that mis-decode; Aaron was right to reject it. **The v0.21.1 marker IS inside 652 and the old one was not, so that move was correct even though it did not open the door.** **TWO UNMODELLED CONDITIONS REMAIN, NEITHER DECIDABLE STATICALLY, SO THIS BUILD INSTRUMENTS RATHER THAN GUESSES: (1) UNK21** (0xFF21 @ 0x00546A11): `if ([0x2040A34]!=0) pass; else pass only if ((*(byte*)([0x20403A4]+0x6D))&1) == operand` — program 34's operand is **0**, so that bit must read 0. **(2) WHOSE POSITION IS TESTED:** every position opcode uses cached block coords at `[0x2040A24]/[0x2040A28]` (/4) when **`[0x2040A30] != 0`**, else the live player position — **if a vehicle still owns it the test runs against the GARDEN, parked 6.3 km away in segment 621, a different square, and no amount of walking would ever open that door.** New `[TRIGEVAL]` line once a second on the world map and again at sweep start logs all of it. **Verified: trigseg_test OK (0 bad) — NEW GATE: five points whose segment the mod already printed from its own independently written arithmetic reproduce exactly, segment 652's box is asserted, four segment boxes round-trip, and segment 506 is asserted to be in the far east; vehsig_test OK; catalog_story_test 0 failures; lint_seh OK (88 files); minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors / 0 bad; garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass. READ-ONLY INSTRUMENT — no steering, planning or catalog logic changed.** **BAT: walk to Edea's House and LET IT SWEEP — the sweep is the point now. Send ff8_world.log. `posFlag` non-zero with `cachedSeg != liveSeg` = the game is testing the GARDEN's position; `UNK21 bit=1` = a world-state flag holds the door; both clean = the test fires and something downstream eats it. Standing anywhere in x -32768..-24576, y 65536..73728 for a few seconds produces the same lines.**
