**Prior (v0.21.0 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #79: THE AUTO-DRIVE CALLED A WALKING MAN A VEHICLE, FIVE TIMES, AND ORBITED EDEA'S HOUSE).** Aaron: *"It would get 1-2km out, then announce vehicle detected, then wouldn't finish the approach and the distance would increase once again."* **THE LOG IN SEVEN LINES:** `21:29:27 YAWDRIVE dist=4750` → `21:29:31 dist=1604` → **`21:29:33 dist=16`** (the FOOT law closing 4,750 units without a stumble) → `21:29:33 VEHSIG VEHICLE DETECTED (24 of 24)` → `21:29:34 HDG-DIAG dist=293` → `21:29:38 dist=2469` → **an orbit at 1200-1400 that never closes.** Same shape at 21:21:18 (dist 4), 21:27:06 (dist 84), 21:28:41 (dist 14) — **every verdict lands the instant the drive reaches a WAYPOINT.** **WHY: THE MOD MANUFACTURES THE EVIDENCE ITSELF.** At a waypoint it SLEWS THE CAMERA to the new bearing in one write and the character then rotates to catch up over several frames — through all of them he is still walking the OLD heading, so the motion sides with `mh` by construction. Two adjacent frames say it: `cam=3403 mh=3403` (agree, no vote) then `cam=3855 mh=3403` (gap 452, he has not turned yet), and the turn that follows reads `1977, 1465, 953, 441` — the gap CLOSING as the rotation completes. **Of the 25 voting frames in the ring at the first verdict, EVERY ONE came from a turn.** The old comment claimed the verdict was "unreachable on foot (0/153 disagreement frames)"; that control data was honest, it just never contained a mod-driven camera slew. **TWO GUARDS: (1) a RUN of closing gaps is a turn and cannot vote** — one shrinking frame is noise, two in a row is a rotation; a vehicle's gap persists or wobbles but does not systematically close. **(2) THE ENGINE'S OWN vehicleId VETOES THE VERDICT** — `0x020409E0` read **0, on foot, at EVERY drive start of that session** while the heuristic declared a vehicle five times. **A heuristic does not overrule a direct reading.** An unreadable id (-1) still makes no claim, so the fallback keeps the job it was built for. **⚠ THE REASON THIS SURVIVED: NOTHING IN THE TREE COMPILES `world_map_drive_exec.inl`** — no host harness reaches it, so 66 KB of steering logic had no check before a Windows build. **The policy is extracted to `src/world_map_vehsig.inl` and `tests/vehsig_test.cpp` replays the 25 REAL frames from `ff8_world.log` through it:** the OLD policy latches (asserted first, so a fixture that stops reproducing the defect fails loudly instead of passing vacuously), the new one does not with the id readable or not, a vehicle with an unreadable id is still caught (steady OR wobbling gap), the id veto is announced once not once per frame, the `1977,1465,953,441` rotation contributes 2 votes instead of 4, and `VehSigReset` really resets. **Verified: vehsig_test OK (0 bad) — NEW GATE, add it to the run list; lint_seh OK (88 files); minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors / 0 bad; garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass. `world_map_drive_exec.inl` 66,357 → 66,207; `field_navigation.cpp` untouched at 81,645.** **BAT: stand outside Edea's House on foot and auto-drive to it — NO "Vehicle detected.", and the distance falls and keeps falling. Grep `[VEHSIG]`: silence is the pass. Then drive somewhere in the car to confirm `[VEHID] ... steering as VEHICLE from drive start` still fires — that is the path doing the work now.**

**Prior (v0.20.130 — **BAT-CONFIRMED 2026-08-16, THE MINI-GAME IS PRODUCTION READY: the loss/retry path is now played too — `18:57:21 "You are down."` with NOTHING after it (.128), `movie narration resumed (the round is lost)` (.129), field 95 suppressed, then `briefing 2 ... Squall 644/800 Foe 600/600` — the HP+200 bonus applied and the soldier NOT carried over — and F9 from the HOST PHASE skipped cleanly to Galbadia Garden. Across the win run and the loss run: ZERO errors, ZERO exceptions, ZERO phantom [POLL] speech, no [BGBTL] activity outside the fight windows, ~50 log lines per run. The settled state is written up in the project doc `claude/GARDEN_BATTLE_MINIGAME.md`.** Originally — #minigame-bgbtl: THE BRIEFING VETOED THE SOLDIER AND LEFT SQUALL SWINGING).** Aaron, on BAT: *"as I press punch or kick on the controls screen I can hear Squall striking the enemy. When I press enter to actually start, the enemy's HP is not full."* **THE 2026-08-15 LOG HAD ALREADY MEASURED THIS AND NOBODY LOOKED AT THE NUMBER:** `16:12:06 briefing 1 opened / armed Squall 440/600 Foe 0/0` → 34.5 s of tapping W, D, A, X to have the keys named → `16:12:41 briefing ended` → **`Foe 431/600`. 169 damage dealt before "Game start."** The control run is in the same log: briefing 2 lasted 5.4 s, he pressed nothing but Enter, and it reads `Foe 600/600`. **CAUSE — the price of the thing that makes the briefing work at all:** since .123 the field is NOT frozen (freezing `field_main` would stop the dialog box ever being drawn), so **`squ_punchkeyscan0` runs behind the Game Controls**, its four BTNTESTs read the same button word the key learner reads, and every tap REQ'd `squ_punching0`/`squ_kicking0` → `gal_hpcalc0` → `30 + rnd/4` off the soldier. **The calibration step was a free hit on the enemy, and a careful player who tapped each key twice to lock a binding started the round against a soldier already down a quarter of his health — which makes the fight EASIER and makes every "how many heavy punches" answer wrong.** **FIX: the briefing vetoes BOTH fighters.** Squall's three attack scripts redirect to `squall::push` — his own entity's `PUSH8 n ; RET 8`, the trick the soldier's veto has used since .123. **`squ_guarding0` is deliberately NOT vetoed:** it only raises the guard flag and the block key is the one control worth letting the player feel while being told about it. **LABELS DERIVED THEN VERIFIED IN THE SCRIPTS: a `.sym` group (count, start) spans count+1 slots and its names run header, default, talk, push, … so `push` is always start+3.** bg2f_31 squall = (18, 0) → **squall::push = 3**, keyscan 12; bgbtl_1 squall = (14, 22) → **squall::push = 25**, keyscan 28 — both keyscan numbers are what the log prints, and disassembly confirms `PUSH8 3 ; RET 8` and `PUSH8 25 ; RET 8`. The same arithmetic reproduces gal0::push = 45 and g_hei0::push = 48, already proven in play. **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors / 0 bad with eleven new cases — all six of Squall's attack labels redirect to his push in the right field, the soldier is still vetoed alongside him, the guard AND the keyscan itself come through untouched, and the skip veto still leaves Squall's punch alone; lint_seh OK (88 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass. `field_navigation.cpp` untouched at 81,645.** **ALSO IN .130, FROM THE SAME BRIEFING: "Punch, W." FIFTY-THREE TIMES** — `ff8_field.log` has 53 `[BGBTL-LEARN]` lines, every one of them `Punch`, and `ff8_mod.log` has eleven `"Punch, W." (interrupt)` inside two seconds. **A held key AUTO-REPEATS and every repeat is a fresh rising edge on the button word**, so the learner re-announced it and each announcement interrupted the last — the sentence never finished saying itself. **The learner now debounces per mask at 1200 ms**; a deliberate re-tap still confirms the binding, because pressing a key and hearing nothing reads as "the key is not registering". The probe drives six auto-repeats 100 ms apart and requires EXACTLY ONE announcement, then a re-tap after the window and requires that it speaks — it reports `a held key announced 6 times in 600 ms` against the old code. **BAT: on the Game Controls screen tap every key several times — the mod names each one and NOTHING ELSE HAPPENS, no strike, no impact sound, no HP movement. Then press Enter: the first health report must have the soldier at 100. Sit on the briefing half a minute tapping, which is what caught it.**

**Prior (v0.20.129 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: THE MOVIE STOPS TALKING OVER THE FIGHT, AND THE PUSH UTILITY STOPS DYING ON A THREE-DAY-OLD LOCK FILE).** **`disc01_33h.avi` plays behind the WHOLE Garden battle and carries its own audio description.** From the 2026-08-15 log: `16:14:07 [FMV_AD] Cue 2: "Turquoise energy and fire flash; Galbadia Garden presses in at the edge."` then `16:14:08 "Heavy punch ready. Let go of block and press D."` — **one second apart, both spoken with interrupt, and only one of them is something the player can act on.** The heavy punch already has a priority path because the HEALTH REPORT was cutting it off; it should not also have to out-shout the scenery. **The briefing has suppressed FMV narration since .123 — but `EndBriefing` resumed it, and `EndBriefing` is what STARTS THE ROUND.** The suppression covered the stretch where nothing happens and lifted for the stretch where everything does. **Narration is now held from the briefing until the round is DECIDED, and given back by whichever of the four things decides it: the win, the loss, F9, or `Disarm` (which resumes unconditionally, so no path can leave a player's descriptions off).** **Nothing is lost — `fmv_audio_desc` consumes each cue on its timestamp rather than queueing it, so a suppressed cue is DROPPED, and every cue worth hearing in that movie (Rinoa on the cable, the soldiers below, the run for the entrance) falls AFTER the win.** **⚠ WHY THREE PUSHES DIED ON `.git/index.lock`, AND THE RULE THAT COMES OUT OF IT: git commands run against the mod folder THROUGH THE COWORK DESKTOP BRIDGE CANNOT UNLINK THEIR OWN LOCK** — `warning: unable to unlink '.git/index.lock': Operation not permitted` — **so every `git status` I ran left one behind, and the next push hit it** (2026-08-02, 2026-08-12, 2026-08-15; the stale files are in `_to_delete/`). **DO NOT RUN GIT THROUGH THE BRIDGE.** The push utility runs natively and is unaffected. `push_to_github.bat` also gains a **Step 0**: if `.git\index.lock` exists AND `tasklist` shows no `git.exe`, remove it and log that it did; if a git process IS running the lock is real, leave it and let Step 1 fail with git's own message. (`Utilities/` is git-ignored, so that stays local.) **Verified: `minigame_bgbtl_compile` 0 errors / 0 bad with the policy written as its own assertion — narration must survive `EndBriefing` (the check FAILS on the old code and passes on this one) and must be back on after both the HP-driven win and the disarm; lint_seh OK (88 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass. `field_navigation.cpp` untouched at 81,645 — 275 from the hard fail.** **BAT: from "Game start." to the moment you win, lose or press F9, the ONLY voice should be the mod's. The instant you win, the descriptions come back and the rescue scene narrates as it always has. Grep `[FMV_AD]` — held cues log as `[SUPPRESSED -- the mod holds the channel]`.**

**Prior (v0.20.128 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: THE ARC IS DONE, THE SCAFFOLDING COMES DOWN, AND THE LOG HAD TWO WARTS LEFT).** Aaron: *"Please review the log in full and in detail to check for any other problems or issues with the mini-game that we may have overlooked. After that look for any diagnostics, dead code, etc. left over from building this out and do the necessary clean up."* **THE REVIEW (16:12–16:16 run — two attempts, a deliberate loss, a heavy punch 600→148, six blocks, best streak 3, F9 into a rescue FMV that played in full and handed off on the game's OWN MAPJUMP3 at clock 1185): the mini-game machinery is clean. Three things around it were not.** **(1) THE FIGHT ANNOUNCED ITS OWN ARCHIVE FILENAME, once per attempt** — `announced fieldId=0x0098 name='bgbtl_1'` at 16:12:29 and 16:14:17. `FIELD_DISPLAY_NAMES[152]` is a placeholder (`// 152: bgbtl_1 (???)`), so the player heard a filename two seconds before the Game Controls opened. **Field 152 joins field 95 on `NO_NAME_FIELDS` — it is a fight, not a place.** **(2) THE LOSS WAS ANNOUNCED TWICE** — `16:13:06 "You are down."` then `16:13:07 "You 0"`: the outcome path returns, but the next poll saw the rounded percentage move and reported it as if the fight were live. **Health reports now stop at the outcome.** **(3) `+4294933093ms`** — DWORD underflow in the REQ timestamps, because `EndBriefing` restarts the clock while the ring still holds events stamped during the briefing. Gone with the line that printed it. **THE SCAFFOLDING: a run emitted 711 `[BGBTL]` lines and now emits ~75** — deleted `[BGBTL-REQ]` (260/run), `[BGBTL-CLOCK]` (188), `[BGBTL-KEY]` (102), `[BGBTL-BTN]` (57), `[BGBTL-STATE]` (28), the functions behind them (`LogKeys`, `LogKeyChanges`, `WatchButtons`, `WatchState`, `ReadButtonsAnySource` and its `frozen` plumbing, `KeyCand`), **7 orphaned statics and 14 unused constants.** **ONE LINE WAS ALSO JUST WRONG:** `[BGBTL] Squall's guard script REQ'd (label 31, squall::squ_kicking0)` — label 31 IS `squ_kicking0`; the guard is 32. It predates the `.sym` off-by-one fix and printed its own contradiction every run. Deleted rather than corrected: `s_guardSeen` has come from `WatchStreak` since .120, because var 340 increments once per BLOCKED HIT where the flag has one rising edge per HOLD. **⚠ `minigame/` WOULD HAVE BEEN PUSHED BY `git add -A`** — extracted `bgbtl_1`/`bg2f_31` field archives, `chara.one`, disassembled `FF8_EN.exe` handlers: **718 KB of game data, not ours to redistribute. Added to `.gitignore` with the reason beside it.** **`FF8OPC_VERSION` SAID "0.20.118" THROUGH TEN BUILDS** — every banner since .119 named a build that was not running. Bumped to 0.20.128, its 3 KB inline comment cut to a paragraph, one history line written for each of the ten. **Verified: `minigame_bgbtl_compile` 0 errors / 0 bad — the `WatchState` assertions are replaced by ones holding the same ground through surviving code (`GuardVarFor` per field, and `ApplyBlockAssist` writing that byte and NEVER var 1031, the soldier's); the probe's `#include <cmath>` also moved above the SEH macros, since `#define __try if(1)` turns libstdc++'s own `__try/__catch` into a syntax error. lint_seh OK (88 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **`field_navigation.cpp` untouched at 81,645 — 275 from the hard fail. `field_minigame_bgbtl.inl` 71,785 → 64,716.** **NOT CHANGED, YOURS TO CALL: (a) the background movie's audio description plays DURING the fight** — *"Turquoise energy and fire flash…"* at 16:14:07, one second before *"Heavy punch ready"* had to fight it for the channel; the briefing already suppresses FMV narration and extending that to the whole round would clear it. **(b) the game's own legend and name labels ("Punch Block Kick", "Squall", "Galbadian Soldier") are read at each fight start** while the briefing covers the same ground. **BAT: nothing about the fight should feel different. No "bgbtl_1" on entry; a deliberate loss says "You are down." and then NOTHING; `[BGBTL]` in ff8_field.log is ~75 lines, not 700, and the banner reads v0.20.128.**

**Prior (v0.20.127 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #dialog: THE PHANTOM IS FOUR PHANTOMS, AND A FIELD CHANGE LEAVES EXACTLY THE DEBRIS AN FMV DOES).** Aaron: *"there is a repeat of phantom dialogue after Squall finishes his speech... Irvine's dialogue from before where he says to be CERTAIN there is nothing Squall can do to save Rinoa, inadvertently repeats. Irvine is not in the room and this is a phantom."* **THE LOG, IN SIX LINES:** `15:39:56 [GETSTR] Irvine "...until you're CERTAIN that there's nothing more you can do!"` ... three and a half minutes later ... `15:43:33 MAPJUMPO -> destField=255` / `15:43:35 [fieldload] id=255 'bgsido5a'` / **`15:43:35 [POLL] win[0] "the attack."` + `win[1] "l you're CERTAIN that there's nothing more you can do!"` + `win[2] "'s nothing more you can do!"` + `win[3] "an do!"`** — **FOUR of them in a single poll the instant the empty office finished loading.** Aaron heard the second because it is the longest; the others are the same sentence chewed shorter. **Every one starts MID-WORD — "l you're" is the tail of "until you're", "an do!" the tail of "can do!" — which is the tell: the window objects still point into the PREVIOUS field's dialogue buffer, partway through wherever its paging had reached.** The engine does not clear them on a field change and the poller had no way to know the field had changed. **THE FIX ALREADY EXISTED FOR A DIFFERENT TRANSITION:** PollWindows has carried an FMV guard since v04.16 (snapshot the windows as "already spoken" when a movie ends, then suppress and re-snapshot while pointers settle). **A field change is the same event with a different cause and now gets the same treatment — snapshot on the change, keep absorbing for 2 s.** The shared loop is factored into `SnapshotWindowsAsSpoken()` rather than copied a third time, and the FMV paths call it too so the three guards cannot drift apart. **Only the POLLER is held back — the new field's own dialogue arrives through the opcode hooks (MES/AMESW/ASK), which are untouched, so nothing real is delayed and this cannot swallow a line the game actually shows.** **Verified: a dedicated probe for the new helper (two windows holding the exact fragments from Aaron's log are both captured, show_dialog per-window tracking reset, critical section balanced) — `field_dialog.cpp` has no host harness so this is the check that exists; lint_seh OK (88 files); minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors; garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **`field_navigation.cpp` untouched at 81,645 — 275 from the hard fail.** **BAT: play the run-up to the Garden battle — after Squall's speech, back in the EMPTY Headmaster's Office lower level, there should be SILENCE: no Irvine, no fragments. Grep `[POLL] field 0x` for each field change it absorbs.**

**Prior (v0.20.126 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: THE MOD WAS CARRYING THE SOLDIER'S DAMAGE BETWEEN ATTEMPTS, AND AARON FOUND IT BY THE FIGHT FEELING TOO EASY).** *"I only did a single power punch and it caused me to win. I thought it usually took two. The mod isn't causing a problem where the enemy's partial health is being carried over between attempts, is it?"* **IT WAS, AND THE LOG NAMES THE LINE:** `15:46:03 Squall reached -20 -- loss` (soldier left on 248/600) -> `15:49:47 briefing HP frozen at Squall 604, Foe 248` -> **`15:49:54 briefing HP drifted (Foe 248->600) -- restoring`** -> `15:50:07 squ_punching1` -> `15:50:08 foe HP reached -195 -- WIN`. **THAT "DRIFT" WAS NOT A LEAK, IT WAS THE GAME:** `squall::squ_out0` runs `PUSHI 600 ; WRVARW 356` to set the scene up for a fresh attempt, and **HoldBriefingHp reverted it to the 248 the soldier had been left on.** One heavy punch then finished a soldier who started the round at 41%. **HoldBriefingHp is DELETED.** It was written in .118 to stop the enemy scoring hits while the Game Controls were up, back when nothing else could — but **v0.20.123 replaced that job entirely**: the briefing vetoes the soldier's attack REQs, so g0_punching0/g0_kicking0 never run, so they never REQ squ_punched_up0, so squ_hpcalc0 is never reached and NO DAMAGE IS POSSIBLE. Two mechanisms for one job and the redundant one was overwriting the scene's own initialisation. **One mechanism now, and it is the one that cannot get this wrong: if no attack runs, no HP moves, and everything the SCRIPT writes is left alone.** **The Game Controls screenshot is clean** — seven lines, all inside the box, clear space under the last, the game's legend untouched below it. **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors with the regression written as its own assertion (a briefing opening on a leftover `Foe 248` must let squ_out0's 600 stand, and Squall's retry bonus with it — it fails on the old code and passes on this one); lint_seh OK (88 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` unchanged at 81,645 — 275 from the hard fail. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT: lose on purpose, then check the retry — the soldier back to full and TWO heavy punches needed again. Grep `[BGBTL-HP] armed` on the second attempt; the foe should read 600/600.**

**Prior (v0.20.125 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: ONE LINE OF FmvSkip ATE THE WIN ANNOUNCEMENT, AND THE SOLDIER'S HP BAR WAS LYING).** **(1) "SKIPPING VIDEO" INSTEAD OF "YOU WIN", AND BOTH HALVES WERE THE SAME LINE.** Aaron: *"I still did not hear You Win once I pressed F9. I instead heard it say skipping video which was weird since it did not skip any video that I could tell."* `FmvSkip::RequestSkip()` — the CODE path added in .117 so the mod could end the battle movie behind the scenes — set `g_skipRequested`, and that flag is what makes `FmvSkip::OnFrame` say **"Skipping video"** with interrupt on, talking over the mod's own "Skipping. You win…" a fraction of a second later. And nothing appeared to skip because ending `disc01_32h.avi` just moves the host phase along, which reads as the scene continuing. **RequestSkip() is now SILENT — it is a code path, and a caller that wants the player told says so itself. Backspace is untouched.** **(2) HE FALLS — and the bar was wrong.** The 15:26:42 screenshot shows Squall alone on the wire: at clock 580 director0 takes the win branch and REQs `gal0::gal0_timeover0`, the soldier's fall animation, so F9 gives the same ending a won fight does. **But the same shot caught the Galbadian Soldier's bar still FULL RED while the mod had just said "You win."** The bars are NOT the HP variables: **opcode 315 (0x00529BF0) writes `[0x01D9CF5C + id*16]`** (id 0 = Squall, id 1 = foe) and the scripts only call it from inside squ_hpcalc0 / gal_hpcalc0 — i.e. only when a punch actually lands. The skip writes the variables directly, so the bars never heard about it. **Both gauges are now written by the skip and by the win called from foe-HP-zero.** **(3) The box cleared the legend as intended, but the measurer returned `210x108` for seven lines and the last sat on the bottom border — AMES's `+0x11` allowance leaves nothing under a full-height final line, so +8 of slack.** **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors, probe now maps the gauge page and asserts the soldier's bar is emptied and Squall's refilled by the skip (not just the variables); lint_seh OK (88 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` unchanged at 81,645 — 275 from the hard fail. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT: (1) F9 — expect "Skipping. You win…" and nothing about video; (2) F11 after F9 — the soldier's bar empty, Squall's full; (3) F11 on the Game Controls — clear space under the last line.**

**Prior (v0.20.124 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: BOX MOVED UP, DOUBLE-TAP REMOVED, WIN CALLED WHEN IT IS WON, GAME OVER SCREEN UNNAMED).** **(1) THE SCREENSHOTS SHOW NOTHING CLIPPED — the game's own measurer sized the box correctly — but FF8 DRAWS ITS OWN "W Punch / A Block / X Kick" LEGEND AT THE LOWER LEFT and a centred box lands on it**, hiding two lines. **Six lines instead of eleven, no blank spacers, and parked at `y = 8`** instead of vertically centred, which clears both the legend and the two HP bars. **(2) DOUBLE-TAP F9 REMOVED** at Aaron's request — it worked (14:33:14 requested, field 675 by 14:33:18) but existed to save a wait that only mattered while the fight was dangerous, and it cut the rescue to do it. **That also removes the last code in this module that ever wrote var 80, and the probe now asserts it outright: four clock values, twenty ticks each, the fight clock must come out exactly as it went in — the standing guard against .119's 82 seconds of dead scene.** **(3) "YOU WIN" MOVES BACK TO FOE-HP-ZERO, BUT MADE TRUE RATHER THAN OPTIMISTIC.** The reason it ever moved to the field-675 transition is worth keeping in view: **v0.20.111 announced it at foe zero and the foe then sat at 0/600 for SEVENTY SECONDS, kept swinging, and Squall bled 1000 -> -31 and LOST** — zero foe HP does not end the fight, the round is decided at clock 580 on `foeHP < squallHP`. So the foe reaching zero now **engages the same protection F9 uses (attacks vetoed at the REQ, both HP pinned) and THEN says "You win."** F9 marks the win the same way; the field-675 line only speaks if neither already has. **(4) FIELD 95 `testbl6` IS A MENU, NOT A PLACE** — the "Try again / Try again with HP+200" choice — so it is on a short suppression list in `field_announce.cpp`, a list rather than a blanked table entry so FIELD_DISPLAY_NAMES stays a pure id-to-name map with the reason written beside each omission. **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors with the two new assertions (clock never written anywhere in the round; the win is called from HP AND engages its protection); lint_seh OK (88 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` unchanged at 81,645 — 275 from the hard fail. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT: (1) F11 the Game Controls — box at the top, nothing over it, the game's legend has the lower left to itself; (2) win on merit — "You win." the moment the soldier hits zero, and nothing lands after it; (3) F9 once — says "You win", rescue plays in full, a second press only says "Already skipping"; (4) lose on purpose — the Game Over options announce with no field name in front of them.**

**Prior (v0.20.123 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: THE GAME CONTROLS ARE ON SCREEN, AND THE field_main PAUSE HAD TO GO TO GET THEM THERE).** **WINDOW RENDERING HANGS OFF field_main**: `field_main -> 0x00471010 -> 0x0052BC00 -> 0x004A0880`, so the one-byte RET that has paused this scene since v0.20.106 **guaranteed no dialog box could ever be drawn or even finish opening.** The two features are mutually exclusive as built. **THE BRIEFING NOW PAUSES THE FIGHT, NOT THE FIELD** — the soldier's attack REQs are rewritten to the entity's own `push` script and both HP values are pinned, the mechanism the F9 skip proved on 2026-08-15. Three bonuses beyond the picture: the key learner reads the REAL button word instead of the engine-side stand-in it needed while frozen; the FMV keeps playing; and **there is no patched byte to restore any more, so no heartbeat, no guard thread, and no way for a fault in the mod to strand the player's game** — that whole class of danger is deleted. **The one thing the freeze bought that the veto does not is a stopped clock**, so `CLOCK_BRIEF_LIMIT` hands the fight back at clock 420 (resolution is 580) with "Starting now." — nobody loses to a reading speed. **THE BOX IS COPIED FROM AMES (opcode 0x65, 0x005291E0), the auto-SIZING message variant:** `set_window_object 0x004A0410` / `measure_text 0x004A0EC0` (low word = width, high = height) / `w = lo+0x10, h = hi+0x11` / clamp `x+w < 0x130`, `y+h < 0xE0`, min 8 / `set_window_geometry 0x004A07A0` / `open_window 0x004A0620` / `set_current_window 0x0049FD50` / `[0x00B8EE90]+0xD3,+0xD4 |= 1<<win`; WINCLOSE (0x00529B60) reverses it via `0x0049FBF0`. **THE SIZING IS THE GAME'S OWN** — 0x004A0EC0 walks the string with real font metrics; the mod only word-wraps to 34 columns first. **Window 7** (the scene's legend uses 4). Text encoded to FF8's charset (inverse of ff8_text_decode.h: A-Z 0x45+, a-z 0x5F+, 0-9 0x21+, newline 0x02) and it names the player's learned keys. If the window fails to open the briefing is still spoken. **COSMETIC ITEM FIXED: the key table now ships SEEDED** (W punch, X kick, A block, D heavy — FF8 PC's stock bindings and exactly what two runs measured on 0x01CE48B0) and deliberately **UNLOCKED**, so a first real press on a differently-configured machine overwrites the seed. No more "(key unknown)" on the first call of a session. **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors, and the probe now maps an EXECUTABLE page over the six engine routines filled with `ret`, plus `mov eax,imm32; ret` at the measurer, so OpenBriefDialog runs its real geometry maths on a real measurement rather than a C++ stub; new assertions cover byte-exact FF8 encoding, wrapping that never exceeds its column and does break lines, a surviving source newline, open/close setting and clearing the window bit, the briefing handing back at clock 420 but not 100, and the heavy seed being present and NOT locked. lint_seh OK (88 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` unchanged at 81,645 — 275 from the hard fail. New file `src/field_minigame_bgbtl_dialog.inl` (9,353). SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT: (1) LOOK at the Game Controls screen — a proper FF8 box, centred, every line inside it, with your own key names; (2) the scene is no longer frozen behind it, so confirm the movie plays on and you still take NO damage while reading (that is the veto, not a pause); (3) everything else as before. If the box does not appear, grep `[BGBTL-DLG]` — it logs the measurement and the derived box, or the exception.**

**Prior (v0.20.122 — BAT'd 2026-08-15, #minigame-bgbtl: ✅ THE SCENE IS DONE. AARON WON THE FIGHT ON MERIT AND THE SKIP WORKS.)** *"I heard the power punch, and it successfully landed. I did that a couple times and won the battle. I also tried using F9 to skip and that worked, I was able to proceed into G-Garden and no errors occurred and the next scene started as expected."* **The confirming log line is the summary: `11 attacks cued, 9 BLOCKED, best streak reached 6` — two heavy punches at 14:32:42 and 14:32:54, the foe at -281, won on health; SKIP ALL requested 14:33:14, movie ended 14:33:16, disarmed at field 675 at 14:33:18 — four seconds, clean exit; `KEYS LEARNED: punch=W kick=X block=A heavy=D`.** Compare the 2026-08-14 baseline: 75 attacks, 0 blocks, four straight losses. **ONE COSMETIC LEFTOVER, NOT WORTH A CYCLE ON ITS OWN:** the first `HEAVY PUNCH announced` of a session can read `(key unknown)` if the player did not tap the heavy key during the briefing's calibration — it names the key correctly from the next press onward (14:32:33 unknown, 14:32:40 D). The spoken fallback already says "your fourth action key". **Original details of the build below.**

**(v0.20.122 build notes — #minigame-bgbtl: THE RETRY ARMED IN A HALLWAY, THE HEAVY PUNCH WAS TAKEN AWAY BEFORE IT COULD BE THROWN, AND THE SKIP CUED THE ATTACKS IT HAD JUST CANCELLED).** **(1) "TRY AGAIN" DROPS THE PLAYER IN THE B-GARDEN HALLWAY FIRST.** Aaron: *"All of the mini-game accessibility machinery needs to stop and wait to turn on again when the mini-game begins."* The log: `14:10:35 briefing 2 opened by the host field reloading` ... `14:11:28 the battle FMV finally starts` — **forty-six seconds of hallway with the REQ hook live, the assist pinning a guard flag and the block cue armed.** .121 briefed on the host field's RELOAD, which is the walk back to the fight, not the fight. **The module now DISARMS when the host field returns after a Game Over and waits for `disc01_32h.avi`** — the AVI latch clears once the name stops being reported, so the movie re-arms and re-briefs exactly like attempt one, at full health. **(2) THE HEAVY PUNCH: IT WORKS IN FIELD 152 AND THE LOG PROVES THE WHOLE MECHANIC** — `14:10:21 squ_punching1`, `14:10:22 g0_punched_up0`, `Foe 600 -> 66` in one hit. **In field 144 the same REQ fired and nothing happened, for two reasons that both punish the one move the scene requires:** (a) **var 340 is zeroed by any unguarded hit and you must release block to punch** — at 14:10:06 Squall took 332->285 in exactly that gap and `var340 3 -> 0` shut the keyscan's `var340 >= 3` gate, so SIX SECONDS of held D produced one attempt and then nothing; **Prior (v0.20.119 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: AARON HAS BEEN BLOCKING CORRECTLY FOR FOUR BATs; THE MOD WAS READING THE SOLDIER'S GUARD FLAG).** **NO BAT WAS SPENT ON ANY OF THIS** — it came out of the exe, the two field scripts and the 2026-08-15 log. **THE ROOT CAUSE IS ONE OFF-BY-ONE: A `.sym` GROUP IS `(count, start)` AND SPANS count+1 SLOTS, because the slot at `start` holds the GROUP HEADER.** bg2f_31 proves it in a line — squall is `(18,0)`, the next group starts at 19, so squall owns nineteen slots and the .sym lists nineteen strings. Read off by one, `squ_punchkeyscan0` lands on a positioning script; read correctly it lands on label 12, which IS the keyscan (four BTNTESTs and a JMP -53). **Everything downstream was wrong: the block is MASK 128, not 64; Squall's guard flag is var 1030 (field 144) / var 1028 (field 152), NOT var 1031 — 1031 is the SOLDIER'S; the soldier's attacks are 55/56 and 49/50, not the trio including the driver `g0_fall0`; and "tap block, holding does not work" is the exact opposite of the truth.** **THE PROOF HE WAS BLOCKING IS IN HIS OWN LOG AND IT IS ARITHMETIC:** `squ_hpcalc0` rolls a byte and reads one flag — **guarded 15+rnd/8 (15..46), unguarded 40+rnd/4 (40..103)** — and **ALL 35 RECORDED HITS FALL INSIDE ONE OF THOSE BANDS, with all nine guarded ones from the single attempt he spent holding the key (6 of his 9 hits blocked).** `BLOCK SUMMARY: 0 BLOCKED` was the instrument, not the player. **AND BLOCKING ALONE LOSES, WHICH NOTHING HAD EVER SAID:** director0 resolves at `var80 >= 580` on `foeHP < squallHP`, and the only thing that moves the soldier's health enough is `squ_punching1` — the keyscan's fourth BTNTEST, mask 32, gated on `var340 >= 3`, doing **300+rnd against 600 HP**. var340 counts CONSECUTIVE BLOCKS (`squ_punched_up0` increments on a blocked hit, zeroes on an unblocked one) and at exactly 3 the game REQs `director5::sys_mes` to tell a sighted player. **So the scene is "block three in a row, heavy punch, twice" — now what the briefing says, with the streak announced.** **THE WARNING IS 133 ms** (`g0_kicking0` WAITs 8 frames between REQ and hit, `g0_punching0` 10; his log agrees to a tick) so no announcement can ever be a prompt — but `squ_guarding0` only gives a ~50% DUTY CYCLE, so **while the block key is held the mod now pins the guard flag the script itself sets.** Offline model, calibrated on his measured 0.72 s attack interval: **nothing held 0% blocked / 100% knocked out; key held, game's own 46% blocked / 7% round wins; key held + assist 100% blocked / 91.8% round wins.** F9 toggles it. **THE MOD NO LONGER GUESSES WHICH KEY IS WHICH, BECAUSE IT CANNOT:** the four masks are PAD BITS and `0x004A2D60` only consults FF8's remap table when `[0x01CFE73C]&0x20` (clear on his machine), so the keyboard mapping lives in the 2013 wrapper, not the exe — **the briefing now names his keys by watching `0x01CE48B0` while he taps them, which is safe because the field is frozen there.** **F10 WAS ONE SHORT, LITERALLY:** `.117` wrote 579 and waited for the engine to carry it over, but **VAR 80 IS THE MOVIE'S FRAME NUMBER** (`0x0052A016` stores `0x005305A0`'s result at `[0x00B8EE90]+0x50`, only while a movie runs) and F10 had just ended the movie — **the log shows clock=579 for FIFTY-THREE SECONDS while Squall was punched from 600 to 147.** Now it writes **580**, and when the clock stalls with no movie behind it carries it on at 15/s (the rate measured off the rescue movie) up to but never past 1057, backing off the moment the engine drives it again. Rescue FMV confirmed as `disc01_33h.avi`. **THE "ENEMY ACCUMULATING HITS ON THE GAME CONTROLS SCREEN" WAS THE BRIEFING ARRIVING 43 SECONDS LATE ON A RETRY** — the AVI latch means a retry could only be triggered by the on-screen legend, and field 144 reloaded at 01:12:16 while the legend appeared at 01:12:59 with Squall already at 498/600. **The pause itself is airtight: across a 47-second briefing the fight clock moved 100 -> 106.** Retries now brief the moment the host field reloads. **NEW OFFLINE TOOLING: `minigame/jsm2.py`** — decoding verified against the engine's decoder at `0x00530760`, which proves a zero-high-byte dword is the WHOLE 24-bit OPCODE, not "push literal N" (every such dword in both scripts is <= 323, inside the 400-entry table at `0x00B8DE94`) — **`minigame/sim.py`** (fight model; only the animation tail is assumed, and it is calibrated and labelled) and **`minigame/handlers.txt`** (every opcode handler either script uses, plus the expression table: ADD SUB MUL DIV MOD NEG EQ GT GE LT LE NE AND OR XOR NOT). **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors, now mapping a real page at `0x01CE4000` so the assist and learner hit the address the script reads, and asserting the per-field guard byte with the SOLDIER'S flag explicitly not counting, the streak announcing once at 3 and re-arming at 0, the assist pinning only while held and only when enabled, the learner refusing to name a bit with two keys down and four names in one call not aliasing, the skip writing 580, and the governor nudging / capping at 1057 / leaving an engine-driven clock alone; lint_seh OK (86 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` UNCHANGED at 81,602 bytes — 318 from the hard fail. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT: (1) the briefing should open at FULL HEALTH every attempt including retries — tap your four action keys and it should name them; (2) HOLD the block key down and expect "Blocked." on most hits with small damage numbers; (3) listen for "Heavy punch ready", which names the key — release block, press it, and the soldier should lose most of his health; twice wins; (4) F9 toggles the hold; (5) F10 should reach the Rinoa rescue with no long stretch of punching. **Prior (v0.20.118 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: THE BRIEFING WAS COSTING HEALTH, AND THE BLOCK KEY REACHES THE ENGINE).** **AARON FOUND A REAL DEFECT AND IT IS THE WORST ONE IN THE ARC:** *"if you sit on the Game Controls screen the enemy is accumulating hits on Squall -- on one attempt I sat there a long while, then started the game, and the enemy did a flurry of hits that immediately resulted in a game over."* **The freeze stops field_main but plainly does NOT stop whatever schedules the soldier's attacks, and the backlog lands the instant the field resumes. A pause the player CHOSE to take was costing them the fight.** **FIXED BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by chasing the mechanism first: the briefing now snapshots BOTH HP values when it opens and restores them every tick while it is up, and LOGS every drift it corrects — so the run that fixes it also measures how leaky the pause is.** The briefing also now polls HP and the clock AT ALL; `Update()` returned early while briefing, **which is exactly why no previous log could have shown this**. **THE BLOCK KEY REACHES THE ENGINE — THE MAPPING WAS NEVER THE PROBLEM.** `[BGBTL-BTN]` caught **`held=0x0040`** on Aaron's presses: **bit 64, precisely the mask bgbtl_1's fight loop tests to REQ `squ_guarding0`.** **AND YET THE GUARD FLAG NEVER SET ACROSS 75 CUED ATTACKS AND FOUR ATTEMPTS** — so the failure is between the engine's button word and the script, not the keyboard, not the binding, not his timing. **Also measured, and it undercuts the tap-versus-hold framing entirely: THE BIT IS NEVER HELD LONGER THAN 156 ms EVEN WHEN HE HOLDS THE KEY DOWN — 131 button transitions in the attempt he spent holding it.** Either the engine consumes that word on read or something clears it; that is the next thread and it is far better defined than "tap or hold". **F10: THE HP HOLD NOW CONTINUES THROUGH THE ENDING.** `.117` released at clock 601 and Squall was then punched from **383 down to 147** across the rescue scene. The clock write still stops at the resolution (the ending runs on that same counter and holding it would stall the scene) **but the HP hold now runs until the module disarms** — it costs nothing once the winner is decided and it is the difference between watching the ending and being beaten up during it. **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors and it now asserts BOTH halves of that split — HP still pinned after the resolution, clock left untouched — alongside the host-phase FMV skip (exactly one request), the guard-flag detector and every earlier check; lint_seh OK (85 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` UNCHANGED at 81,602 bytes — 318 from the hard fail. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT: (1) SIT ON THE GAME CONTROLS SCREEN A GOOD WHILE, THEN START — health should be exactly where it was, no flurry; grep `briefing HP drifted` for how much the pause was leaking. (2) F10 — no damage from the moment it is pressed, all the way through the rescue scene. (3) Blocking is still expected to fail; that one is now a well-defined hunt on my side, not something for Aaron to keep testing.**

**Prior (v0.20.117 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: THE FIGHT LOOP FOUND, AND THE HOST CLOCK IS FROZEN).** Two answers out of the exe and the scripts, **no BAT spent.** **THE INPUT DISPATCHER IS bgbtl_1 LABEL 28 (dwords 295..350)** — misnamed `squ_punching0` by the SYM, but it is the FIGHT LOOP: reads var 356 (foe HP), loops, and tests FOUR BIT MASKS, REQ-ing one of Squall's actions for each — **mask 16 → REQ 29 squ_punching1 · mask 64 → REQ 31 squ_guarding0 (THE BLOCK) · mask 128 → REQ 32 · mask 32 → REQ 30 squ_kicking0.** The guard is REQ'd from **EXACTLY ONE SITE (dword 319)**, so a block really does go through that script — if bit 64 reaches whichever button word is read. **TAP OR HOLD, from the exe as Aaron asked: HOLDING IS NOT OBVIOUSLY WRONG.** The engine keeps **TWO** words — `0x01CD01F8 valid_buttons` (held, level) and `0x01CD0200 confirmed_buttons` (edge, **PLUS AUTO-REPEAT**) — and the repeat is visible in `engine_eval_is_button_pressed` (0x00468540): from +0x67 it compares an elapsed counter against a threshold and, when exceeded, re-copies the held state into confirmed and sets a repeat flag. **So a held key fires once immediately and again on every repeat — Aaron's instinct may well be right, and holding would look like INTERMITTENT blocking rather than none.** **Which word the fight loop reads is what decides it, and I could NOT settle that from the opcode table — the same table that has now misled me three times (squ_timeover0 as a failure, the per-field labels, the "never fired" guard). Not guessing a fourth time: BOTH words are now logged change-only with the three masks broken out, and one run answers it from data.** **F10: THE HOST FIELD'S CLOCK IS FROZEN — `clock=140` for the ENTIRE host phase in the `.116` log, it never moves.** That is why pressing F10 there left Aaron *"hearing the sound of Squall being punched for quite a while"*: there was nothing to shorten, and bg2f_31's ladder gates only on 1 and 9. **The phase ends when the battle FMV finishes and the script MAPJUMPOs to bgbtl_1, so the only way out is to END THE MOVIE.** New **`FmvSkip::RequestSkip()`** — the same three writes the Backspace path makes, factored out so a code-driven skip and a player-driven one cannot diverge — called ONCE when F10 fires outside bgbtl_1; then the existing clock bump to 579 ends the round and the game plays its own rescue scene. **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors and it now asserts the host-phase behaviour directly — RequestSkip called EXACTLY ONCE across 30 ticks and the frozen host clock left untouched — alongside the guard-flag detector (two rising edges, two blocks, no double-count) and every earlier check; lint_seh OK (85 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` IS 81,602 BYTES — 318 FROM THE HARD FAIL. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT: (1) F10 should now cut through the host phase instead of leaving Aaron standing in it — expect "Skipping video" then the round ending shortly after. (2) For the block, TRY HOLDING A FOR ONE STRETCH AND TAPPING FOR ANOTHER: `[BGBTL-BTN]` records both button words and `[BGBTL-STATE]` records the guard flag, so whichever works will be visible — and so will which word the game reads.**

**Prior (v0.20.116 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: AARON WAS RIGHT TO PUSH — I MISREAD THE ROUND DRIVER, AND THE BLOCK MAY BE INVISIBLE TO A REQ HOOK).** Aaron, told his 44 rounds contained no blocks: *"is it possible that you are mistaking punches for blocks? I didn't do any punches, but I did do blocks."* **Back to the scripts, and he is right on the substance.** **`squ_timeover0` (label 33) IS NOT "you failed to act":** its body reads Squall's HP (`OP_0x11 354`), branches on ≤ 0, clears the damage flag 1028 and the state bytes 340/1030/1032, and ends in a wait loop. **IT IS THE PER-ROUND DRIVER AND FIRES ONCE A ROUND NO MATTER WHAT HAPPENS.** Reporting 44 of them as 44 failures was wrong — and **the timing said so too: its offset from an attack ranges 172–1328 ms where a genuine response label (`squ_punched_up0`) is tight at 328–563.** I read a round boundary as a miss. **AND THE DETECTION ITSELF MAY BE BLIND:** `squ_guarding0` (label 31) really is the guard — its body writes **VAR 1031 = 1**, exactly mirroring bg2f_31's guard writing 1033 — **but a REQ is only ONE way that flag can be set. If the engine's own input handler sets 1031 directly, a block works perfectly and is COMPLETELY INVISIBLE to a REQ hook.** That fits BOTH Aaron's account and the trace, and it means **the "regression since .105" I was about to chase may not exist. THE PAUSE-ALTERNATION EXPERIMENT I HAD STARTED BUILDING IS DROPPED — its premise no longer holds and it would have degraded his run to test a conclusion I could not justify.** **SO THE FLAGS ARE WATCHED DIRECTLY: `[BGBTL-STATE]` logs vars 1027–1034 per tick, change-only, with the fight-relative timestamp; VAR 1031 GOING 0→1 IS A BLOCK whether or not a script was REQ'd for it, and that is what "Blocked." is announced from now.** The REQ path is kept but demoted to recording which mechanism is in play. **PLUS A CHANGE-ONLY W/A/X TRACE** — the `.115` sampling caught keys only at the instant of an attack cue, which cannot see a tap landing 150 ms later, so *"A held at 2 of 66"* said nothing about whether he pressed anything. **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors and it now DRIVES the guard-flag detector directly — two rising edges on var 1031 produce exactly two blocks, and a held flag is not double-counted — alongside every earlier check; lint_seh OK (85 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **ALSO CONFIRMED IN THE `.115` RUN: the fight-clock skip works** — `SKIP: fight clock 326 -> 579` then `SKIP done -- clock 601 is past the resolution`. `.114` fired exactly as designed. **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` UNCHANGED at 81,587 bytes — 333 from the hard fail. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT: play it as before, blocking on the tone. Expect "Blocked." when it lands. If it does not come, the log now answers why on its own — `[BGBTL-KEY]` shows every press, `[BGBTL-STATE]` shows whether the guard flag responded. Between those two there is nowhere left for this to hide.**

**Prior (v0.20.115 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: CORRECTION — BLOCKING WORKS. TAPPING WORKS; HOLDING DOES NOT).** **I WAS WRONG, AND MY OWN EARLIER TRACE PROVES IT.** `.113` reported `squ_guarding0: NEVER` and I concluded blocking was unreachable. **THE v0.20.105 WINNING RUN'S LABEL HISTOGRAM — WHICH I GENERATED MYSELF — CONTAINS `8 label=31 squall::squ_guarding0`.** That build armed on field 152 ONLY, so every REQ in it is bgbtl_1 and the name is right. **Aaron blocked eight times in the run he won. Blocking is reachable and always was — I checked the recent logs and not the one that mattered.** **WHAT THE `.113` LOG ACTUALLY SHOWS, read attempt by attempt as Aaron asked:** in attempt 1 he held A **contiguously across six consecutive attack cues** (+19562, +23828, +24265, +26328, +28406, +30875 — A=1 at every one, A=0 at every cue before he started). During that held stretch squall's bgbtl_1 labels were `27 punchkeyscan`, `28 punching0`, then **`32 punched_up` ×4 and `33 timeover` ×4 — label 31 guarding: ZERO.** **The hold was real, sustained, and produced nothing.** **CONCLUSION: THE GUARD FIRES ON A KEY-PRESS EDGE, NOT A HELD STATE. TAP THE BLOCK KEY ONCE PER ATTACK; HOLDING IT DOWN IS EXACTLY EQUIVALENT TO NEVER PRESSING IT.** That reconciles everything — `.105` (normal play, tapping) 8 blocks, `.113` (deliberate holding) 0. Briefing now says it outright: *"Tap block for each attack. Holding it down does not work."* **AND A BLOCK IS NOW ANNOUNCED: "Blocked."** — Aaron has no other way to know his timing landed, the game gives a blind player nothing to distinguish a block from a punch that missed, and **without that feedback a 200 ms window cannot be learned.** BLOCK SUMMARY counts them. **ALSO CONFIRMED THIS RUN, and it is the whole of last round's ask: THE SKIP REACHED THE RESCUE SCENE** — `00:08:56 SKIP engaged` → `00:09:44 disc01_33h.avi` (rescue FMV, with its existing AD: *"Rinoa grips a cable and slides down from the Garden's underside"*) → `00:11:04 G-Garden - Back Entrance` → *"You win."* → Rinoa's *"Squall!" / "Thank you."* **The `.112` HP hold already delivers exactly what he asked for.** **⚠ THIS BAT RAN v0.20.113 — `.114`'s FIGHT-CLOCK WORK IS NOT YET TESTED.** And measured against this run it is worth less than advertised: of the ~2 minutes, only the **13 s inside bgbtl_1** is shortenable; the 35 s before is the battle FMV in the host field (ladder gates on 1 and 9 only) and the 80 s after is the rescue scene itself. **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors with all `.114` checks intact (clock advance, skip release, 40-hit hold); lint_seh OK (85 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` UNCHANGED at 81,587 bytes — 333 from the hard fail. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT: TAP A ONCE PER TONE, do not hold. Expect "Blocked." when the timing lands — that is the feedback that makes the window learnable. Grep `BLOCK SUMMARY` for the count; eight was the `.105` score with no cue at all.**

**Prior (v0.20.114 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: THE FIGHT CLOCK, AND F10 NOW LANDS WHERE AARON ASKED).** Aaron: *"it should essentially bypass the mini-game and go to the FMV where Squall rescues Rinoa. It should not jump all the way to the G-Garden entrance."* **Found offline, no BAT spent.** **bgbtl_1's driver `director0::talk` IS A LADDER OF WAITS ON ONE VALUE:** `OP_0x0E 80` against **1, 20, 580, 750, 840, 1057**, then `MAPJUMP3 675` as the LAST instruction. At dword 995 — immediately after the 580 wait — it reads var 356 (foe HP) and 354 (Squall HP) and branches to REQ either gal0's fall or squall's. **SO THE FIGHT IS A FIXED-LENGTH ROUND: HP IS NOT CHECKED UNTIL THE CLOCK REACHES 580** — the entire explanation for *"the enemy kept punching me after I pressed F10"*, because zeroing the foe changes nothing until the script looks. **AND EVERYTHING BETWEEN 580 AND 1057 IS THE RESCUE SCENE, which is exactly what `.110`'s forged jump to 675 threw away — the Garden entrance is the LAST INSTRUCTION.** **THE CLOCK IS PINNED BY THE EXE, NOT INFERRED:** opcode-table entry 0x0E (table 0x00B8DE94) is handler **0x0051CB70**, body `mov ecx, dword ptr [eax + 0x1CFE9B8]` — a DWORD read at an unscaled byte offset, the 32-bit sibling of the RDVARB (0x0051CBB0) and RDVARW (0x0051CBF0) handlers that pinned HP. **`OP_0x0E` appears six times in bgbtl_1 and twice in bg2f_31 and its operand is 80 in ALL EIGHT** — it reads one thing — and bytes 80 AND 81 were both in the `.103` whole-block trace's "moved during fight" list, counting upward. **THE FIGHT CLOCK IS A uint32 AT 0x01CFE9B8 + 80.** **SO F10 NOW: (1) pins HP so the script picks the player at 580, (2) pushes the clock to 579 once bgbtl_1 is loaded so that moment is NOW, and (3) LETS GO — past clock 600 it stops writing anything, because the ending from 580 to 1057 runs on the SAME counter and holding it would stall the very scene Aaron wants.** The host field gates only on 1 and 9, so pressing F10 there holds HP and waits for bgbtl_1 — which is exactly what he did last BAT. **No forged transition: the game plays its own ending, rescue FMV included, and reaches Galbadia Garden by its own MAPJUMP3.** **NEW `[BGBTL-CLOCK]` line once a second** — the clock is the spine of the whole scene, so logging it makes future timing questions answerable without another BAT. **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors and it now asserts the clock is advanced to 579 under the 40-hit skip simulation AND that the skip RELEASES once the clock passes the resolution (no more HP writes), alongside every earlier check; lint_seh OK (85 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **The `.113` block instrument is untouched — the same run still answers the hold-A question.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` UNCHANGED at 81,587 bytes — 333 from the hard fail. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT, both questions on one run: (1) HOLD A DOWN through the fight, then grep `BLOCK SUMMARY`. (2) On another attempt press F10 — expect "Skipping. Ending the round now, then the rescue scene plays.", the round finishing within a second or two, and THEN THE RESCUE FMV, not a jump to the Garden entrance. Grep `[BGBTL-CLOCK]` and `SKIP: fight clock`.**

**Prior (v0.20.113 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: ANSWERING TWO QUESTIONS, AND INSTRUMENTING THE ONE I CANNOT).** Aaron: *"isn't there some way to tell when the enemy is about to start their wind-up animation? That is the signal a sighted player uses."* **THE REQ *IS* THE WIND-UP.** The soldier's animation script is INVOKED at the REQ we hook — that instant is the frame the animation starts, and the hit lands 172–375 ms later. **So a sighted player is NOT getting an earlier signal than the tone; they are getting a RICHER one** — which attack it is, and a visual rhythm to anticipate. Their window is the same ~200 ms; they just have more in it. **There is nothing in the script before the REQ to fire on — the director decides and REQs in the same block — so an earlier cue is not available, and claiming otherwise would mean inventing a mechanism that is not there.** **BUT THE ONE THING THAT WOULD SETTLE IT WAS NEVER MEASURED: `squall::squ_guarding0` HAS NEVER FIRED ONCE, IN ANY RUN.** Two very different explanations, indistinguishable from the trace: the presses arrived too late for the window, or they never reached the game at all. **NEW `[BGBTL-KEY]` INSTRUMENT: raw W/A/X sampled on EVERY soldier attack, the guard script watched for directly, and a BLOCK SUMMARY on exit — `N attacks cued, A held at M of them, squ_guarding0 fired: YES/NEVER`.** One run decides whether an auto-block assist is the answer. **WORTH TRYING FIRST BECAUSE IT COSTS NOTHING: HOLD A DOWN rather than tapping at the tone** — if the guard is a held state rather than a timed press, that alone makes the fight playable, and the summary line will say so. Aaron: *"is that going to stop me getting hit by the enemy?"* **NO, AND THE ANNOUNCEMENT NOW SAYS SO.** The `.112` hold pins Squall at full every tick so damage is UNDONE, but **the foe keeps swinging and the game keeps playing its hit sound** — and a player told "skipping" who then hears themselves being punched will reasonably assume it failed, which is exactly what `.111` did to him. F10 now says *"Skip on. He will keep swinging and you will still hear it, but you cannot lose. Wait for the round to end."* **MAKING THE HITTING ACTUALLY STOP MEANS ENDING THE FIGHT EARLY, WHICH MEANS FINDING ITS TIMER — NOT YET LOCATED, AND NOT GUESSED AT HERE.** **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors with all `.112` checks intact including the 40-hit skip-hold simulation; lint_seh OK (85 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` UNCHANGED at 81,587 bytes — 333 from the hard fail. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT: ONE RUN, ONE QUESTION — HOLD A DOWN through the fight, not taps at the tone. Then grep `BLOCK SUMMARY`: `fired: YES` → holding works and the fight is playable as-is; `fired: NEVER` with `A held at N>0` → presses reach the game but the guard needs something else, so auto-block is the answer; `A held at 0` → the presses are not reaching the game at all, a different and more fixable problem.**

**Prior (v0.20.112 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: THE WIN CONDITION IS NOT WHAT IT LOOKS LIKE, AND THE BLOCK WINDOW IS BELOW REACTION TIME).** Three runs, and the log answers all three. **RUN 2 — BLOCKING: ZERO SUCCESSFUL BLOCKS, AND IT IS NOT AARON'S FAULT. `squall::squ_guarding0` NEVER FIRES ONCE** in the whole attempt, in either field; every single attack lands. **The reason corrects a number this module has carried since `.102`: the gap from the soldier's attack REQ to the DAMAGE is 47–375 ms, median ~200** (`+203→+375`, `+891→+1063`, `+1906→+2156`, `+2734→+2922`, `+3359→+3531` in bg2f_31; 265–375 ms in bgbtl_1). **THE "700 ms REACTION WINDOW" WAS MEASURED TO `squ_timeover0`, WHICH IS THE ROUND TIMER, NOT THE HIT.** A 180 ms tone starting ~200 ms before the punch has barely finished when the punch lands — **blocking on the cue is below human reaction time, and a sighted player has the wind-up ANIMATION, which starts earlier than anything the script exposes.** No louder or earlier tone closes this; **there is nothing earlier to fire on. An auto-block assist would — Aaron's call, asked inline.** **RUN 3 — THE SKIP: FOE HP AT ZERO IS NOT A WIN, AND THE MOD SAID IT WAS.** Aaron: *"I heard it say I won but then I still heard the enemy hitting me until my health hit 0."* **The foe sat at 0/600 for SEVENTY SECONDS, kept attacking, and Squall was ground 1000 → −31 and LOST** (`23:38:44 Foe 0/600` win announced → `23:39:53 Squall −31`). **The fight ends when SQUALL reaches zero (immediate loss) or when the TIMER expires with most-HP-wins — the game's own hint said so all along. Zero foe HP only means the player cannot lose the HP RACE; it does not stop the foe swinging.** So **"You win." now fires ONLY on the transition to field 675** — the only win signal ever observed — and foe-at-zero says something true instead: *"Foe out of health. You are ahead."* **THE SKIP IS NOW A MODE, NOT A WRITE: F10 PINS the foe at 0 and Squall at full EVERY TICK** (not on the 250 ms health poll — one hit at low HP is the whole difference) **until the timer ends the fight. Nothing forged, so nothing can desync — which is what crashed in `.110`.** The outcome check also moved ABOVE the "nothing changed" gate in PollHealth: **a decision this important must not depend on a rounded percentage having moved.** **RUN 1 confirmed the good news: the tone fires just before the hit, in the host field, from the first attack — the `.111` per-field label fix works.** **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors and it now SIMULATES THE FAILURE — engage the skip, take 40 hits of 90 damage while the script re-inflates the foe, and assert Squall is still pinned at 1000 and the foe still at 0 — plus the corrected foe-at-zero reporting, alongside every earlier check; lint_seh OK (85 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` UNCHANGED at 81,587 bytes — 333 from the hard fail. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT: (1) F10 should now actually work — "Skipping. You cannot lose now. Wait for the fight to end.", health pinned at full, fight running to its timer before Galbadia Garden loads; a minute or two is the game's own ending, not a hang. (2) No "You win." until actually in Galbadia Garden; foe-at-zero says "Foe out of health. You are ahead." (3) Blocking is STILL EXPECTED TO FAIL — that is the open question, not a regression.**

**Prior (v0.20.111 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: LABELS ARE PER FIELD, AND THE FORGED JUMP IS GONE).** Aaron: *"I sat back and let the enemy hit me in order to listen for the block sound, and did not hear it until I was almost dead."* **THE TONE WAS NEVER LATE — IT WAS NEVER FIRED. LABEL NUMBERS ARE AN INDEX INTO THE FIELD'S OWN ENTRY-POINT TABLE AND MEAN NOTHING ACROSS FIELDS.** `.110` cued on 48/49 (bgbtl_1's `gal0::g0_punching0/g0_kicking0`) **while most of the fight runs in the HOST field**, where those numbers are something else entirely: six attacks in bg2f_31 with **NOT ONE CUE**, then the field changed to 152 at +23562 ms and the tones finally started — by which point Squall was at 11/600. **The correlation in that same log is unambiguous, ~700 ms of warning every time:** `+7468 lbl 55 -> +8203 dmg 440->382` · `+9546 lbl 56 -> 382->280` · `+12546 lbl 56 -> 280->193` · `+14812 lbl 55 -> 193->146` · `+16875 lbl 56 -> 146->106` · `+19546 lbl 55 -> 106->11`. **bg2f_31's own group table puts g_hei0 at labels 45..57, so 54/55/56 are its punching/kicking/guarding trio.** Both tables ship keyed by field id, **an UNKNOWN field never cues blind**, and `[BGBTL-REQ]` now carries the field id, the per-field NAME and a `<- BLOCK` marker **so the next mismatch shows up as a wrong name rather than as silence**. **THE FORGED VICTORY JUMP IS GONE — IT CRASHED.** Aaron: *"I tried using the F10 skip functionality, and it caused the game to crash when I entered G-Garden."* **Writing `push 675, 1019, 3384, 0, 128 ; MAPJUMP3` reproduces the BYTES the winning script writes but NONE OF ITS STATE** — director0::talk reaches that line having finished the fight and torn its scene down, and firing it mid-fight (in the host field, where that script is not even loaded) arrives in Galbadia Garden with the previous scene half dismantled. **THE SKIP NOW USES THE GAME'S OWN PATH: zero the foe's HP, top the player up, and let the script resolve the win exactly as it does on merit — knockout, scene, transition, flags. Nothing forged, so nothing can desync.** Slower (~68 s from zero HP to the field change, measured in the `.110` BAT) but the win is announced immediately from HP anyway. **The transition finding is KEPT IN THE SOURCE as a comment because it is still true and someone will be tempted again.** **THE TONE IS A REAL WAVEFORM NOW:** Beep() has no volume control at all, so it is replaced by a synthesised 22 kHz 16-bit PCM buffer played through `PlaySound(SND_MEMORY|SND_ASYNC)` at full scale — **two rising tones, 1046 Hz then 1568 Hz (C6→G6), 90 ms each with a 4 ms taper**, 180 ms total, well inside the 700 ms window and unmistakably not a game sound. **`winmm.lib` ADDED TO deploy.bat — the one build-system change in this release.** Briefing now says *"The mod plays a tone..."* / *"The mod also calls out health..."* rather than "I will" (Aaron: *"just seems weird for the mod to refer to itself in first person"*). **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors and it now asserts the two label tables AGAINST EACH OTHER (host accepts 54/55/56 and rejects 48/49/57; bgbtl_1 the reverse; unknown field rejects both), that label 55 RESOLVES TO A DIFFERENT NAME per field, and that the tone buffer actually contains a waveform (peak 21304 in the first 64 samples) — alongside every earlier check; lint_seh OK (85 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` UNCHANGED at 81,587 bytes — 333 from the hard fail. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT: (1) the tone should be LOUD and obviously synthetic, two rising notes — press A on the briefing screen to hear it. (2) IT SHOULD NOW FIRE FROM THE VERY FIRST ATTACK, in the host field, ~700 ms before damage — sit back again and every hit should be announced in advance. (3) F10 must no longer crash: expect "Skipping. The fight is won.", then "Foe defeated. You win.", then up to a minute to reach Galbadia Garden through the game's own ending. (4) The briefing says "The mod", not "I will". Grep `[BGBTL-REQ]` — every line now shows `fld=` and a resolved name, attacks marked `<- BLOCK`.**

**Prior (v0.20.110 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: THE TONE, AND TWO THINGS THE MOD GOT WRONG ABOUT ITS OWN NUMBERS).** Aaron after the `.109` BAT (*"that worked well"*): three fixes. **(1) TONE IS NOW THE BLOCK CUE BY DEFAULT** (1000 Hz, 70 ms, still on a worker thread) — he heard both and chose it, **which is exactly what the F9 toggle existed to settle**. The briefing says so, and **PRESSING A ON THE BRIEFING SCREEN PLAYS THE TONE** so the player learns the sound before it matters — the same key that will do the blocking, on a frozen field where it does nothing else. F9 still toggles back to speech mid-fight. **(2) THE MOD LIED ABOUT THE ONE NUMBER IT EXISTS TO REPORT.** Aaron: *"I clearly heard it say the foe was at 0 after a hit, but then the foe still hit me."* The log has it exactly — `Foe 2/600 (0%)`: 0.33% rounded to nearest-5 is 0, so **a fighter with 2 HP left was announced as empty**. **A live fighter now floors at 5%; zero means zero.** **(3) THE WIN WAS ANNOUNCED A MINUTE LATE.** `.109` called it from the transition to field 675, but the BAT shows the foe finished at **22:51:30** and that transition not arriving until **22:52:38** — sixty-eight seconds, because the knockout and the rest of the scene run first. **The outcome is now called from HP the moment it is decided** (*"Foe defeated. You win."* / *"You are down."*), with the field transition kept only as a backstop. **GUARDED ON HAVING SEEN THE FIGHTER ALIVE, BECAUSE HP CARRIES OVER BETWEEN ATTEMPTS: in this same BAT the foe still read 98/600 for FORTY SECONDS into the retry** (`22:50:09 Foe 98/600` → `22:50:49 Foe 600/600`) **before the script re-initialised it — without that guard a retry would announce a win before the first punch.** **ALSO CONFIRMED, CLOSING THE LAST OPEN QUESTION IN THE HP ARC: after "Try again with HP+200" the log reads `Squall 800/800` — so VAR 380 IS THE RETRY BONUS, 600+200, and reading max rather than assuming it was the right call.** **ANSWERED, NOT BUILT:** Aaron asked whether the briefing screen could show an in-game dialog instead of a paused frame. **What is on screen now:** with `field_main` RET'd out the render pass still runs, so it is **the FMV still playing and moving**, with the fighters frozen mid-pose and the legend box and both HP bars overlaid — not a still frame. **It IS possible** — `DialogInject` Phase 1 synthesises a script context and calls `opcode_mes` directly, BAT-proven to render (2026-05-09), and no ASK is needed. **But the dialog state machine is driven by `field_main`, which is precisely what the briefing RETs out**, so it would have to be opened and allowed to reach state 0xD (~450 ms) BEFORE the freeze, and **a dialog that cannot close while frozen would block the fight — a session-ending failure mode traded for polish**, so it is written down rather than shipped blind. **⚠ ALSO: Aaron pressed F11 during the briefing and NOTHING WAS LOGGED in either log for the whole session — the screenshot never happened. F9, X and A all registered, so it is not the key poll. UNEXPLAINED, worth a look.** **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors and it now asserts the zero-floor (2/600 and 1/800 non-zero, −1/600 zero) and the OUTCOME LOGIC END-TO-END against the mapped variable page — stale carry-over ignored, 2 HP not a defeat, 0 HP a win — alongside every `.108`/`.109` check; lint_seh OK (85 files); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` UNCHANGED at 81,587 bytes — 333 from the hard fail. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT: (1) the briefing should describe the tone and A should PLAY it on that screen. (2) blocking cued by the tone, not speech. (3) "Foe 0" must never be spoken while the foe can still fight — the lowest before a defeat is 5. (4) "Foe defeated. You win." the moment the foe drops, not a minute later at the scene change.**

**Prior (v0.20.109 LOCAL — #minigame-bgbtl: BUILD FIX + THE LINT THAT SHOULD HAVE CAUGHT IT).** `field_minigame_bgbtl.inl(780): error C2712: Cannot use __try in functions that require object unwinding`. **ONE LINE:** `.108` put `const std::string avi = FmvSkip::GetCurrentAviName();` INSIDE the `__try` in `FreezeWatchdog`; std::string needs unwinding and MSVC refuses SEH in any function that does. Split into a tiny `IsGardenBattleAvi()` helper with no SEH — **which it never wanted anyway**, since it reads FmvSkip's own state through a normal C++ call, not engine memory through a raw pointer, so there was nothing there for a structured handler to catch. FreezeWatchdog keeps the heartbeat and the AVI latch and loses the `__try` entirely. **THE REAL LESSON IS THE GAP, NOT THE TYPO: THE HOST HARNESSES COMPILE WITH g++, WHERE `__try` IS A MACRO EXPANDING TO `if(1)` — so this entire class of error is INVISIBLE to every pre-build check the mod has.** That is the same shape as the `.89` declaration-order bug: MSVC-only, and therefore only ever caught by Aaron's build. **Twice is a pattern, so it gets a check: NEW `tests/lint_seh.py`** walks src/, finds every function containing `__try`, and flags any that also contains a type needing unwinding. **The rule MSVC applies is PER-FUNCTION, not per-block** — an object declared OUTSIDE the __try still triggers C2712 — so the lint scans whole brace-balanced function bodies rather than just the guarded region. **Verified BOTH WAYS: it reports the exact `.108` construct as a C2712 risk, and it reports the shipped tree clean across ALL 85 FILES THAT USE __try. RUN IT BEFORE ANY BUILD THAT TOUCHES AN SEH BLOCK.** **Verified: lint_seh OK (85 files, 0 problems); minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors with all `.108` behaviour intact — ten-minute no-cap check, FMV arm, unrelated-movie negative, once-per-attempt, Game Over re-arm, AVI latch, patch/restore on every exit, HP replay, legend filter; garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **NO BEHAVIOUR CHANGE FROM `.108` — the pause still has no time limit, the guard thread still watches the heartbeat, and THE `.108` BAT STANDS EXACTLY AS WRITTEN.** `field_navigation.cpp` unchanged at 81,587 bytes, 333 from the hard fail. **SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.**

**Prior (v0.20.108 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: THE CAP IS GONE; LIVENESS REPLACES IT).** Aaron: *"why have the cap at all?"* **He is right, and the cap was guarding the wrong thing. The danger a timeout protected against was never the player taking their time — it is THE MOD ITSELF DYING while the game's main loop is patched out with a RET. A timeout cannot tell those two apart, so it punished the first in order to protect against the second**, and in the `.107` BAT it did exactly that: the fight started while Aaron was still deciding. **THE RIGHT TEST IS LIVENESS, NOT ELAPSED TIME.** `FreezeWatchdog` — which runs above Update()'s early returns — now **stamps a heartbeat every tick while the field is frozen, and a DEDICATED GUARD THREAD restores the byte if that stamp goes stale for 3 s.** `FreezeField -> patch 0xC3, stamp, start guard` / `FreezeWatchdog -> stamp, every tick, forever` / `guard -> stale > 3 s ? restore and exit`. **That condition is true ONLY when something has actually gone wrong; it is never true because a player thought about it for a while.** The guard writes one byte and clears two flags — it never speaks and never touches the event ring, because by the time it runs the thread that owns those is gone. **SO THE BRIEFING NOW HAS NO TIME LIMIT AT ALL: it holds until X, and the reminder simply repeats.** **WORDING, per Aaron:** the 12-second reminder AND the closing line of the briefing are now the same sentence — *"Press X to confirm, press F9 to hear the controls again."* **ALSO FIXED, QUIETLY: the fight clock restarts when the briefing ENDS**, so `[BGBTL-REQ]` offsets read from the start of the FIGHT rather than from the arm, and **the 5-minute arm cap no longer inherits however long the player spent listening — which would have been a second timeout wearing a different hat.** **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors and it now asserts that TEN MINUTES OF WAITING leaves the briefing up and the field still frozen (byte still 0xC3) and that X still ends it cleanly afterwards — alongside the FMV arm, the unrelated-movie negative, the once-per-attempt rule, the Game Over re-arm, the AVI latch, patch/restore on every exit path, the HP replay and the legend filter; garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` UNCHANGED at 81,587 bytes — 333 from the hard fail; all of this went into the .inl. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT: (1) the briefing should now wait INDEFINITELY — leave it a minute or two and confirm the fight never starts on its own, just the reminder every twelve seconds. (2) X resumes with "Game start." (3) Everything else as `.107`: armed at the movie with 600/600, no mid-fight re-briefing, F10 skips. Grep `[BGBTL] GUARD:` — THAT LINE SHOULD NEVER APPEAR; if it does, the mod's own loop stopped and the guard caught it, which is the only case the old cap should ever have fired in.**

**Prior (v0.20.107 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: THE PAUSE HELD; THE CAP LET GO).** Aaron: *"the mini-game started on its own without my pressing the X key. It let the battle proceed for a few hits, then the instructions reappeared. I think we need to pause the fmv / background as well."* **THE FREEZE WORKED PERFECTLY AND THE LOG PROVES IT: between `[BGBTL] field PAUSED` at 22:13:00 and `field RESUMED` at 22:13:30 there are ZERO REQ events and ZERO HP changes** — HP sat at Squall 440/600, Foe 600/600 for the entire thirty seconds, and the first REQ (`+30016ms`) and first damage (`22:13:31`) land AFTER the resume. **Nothing about the FMV was driving the fight. WHAT STARTED IT WAS OUR OWN 30-SECOND CAP**, which fired because no confirm ever arrived. **So the fix is not to pause more, it is to STOP GIVING UP: cap 30 s → 180 s, and while the freeze holds the player hears "Press X to begin." every 12 s so silence can never be mistaken for a hang. A SAFETY NET THAT FIRES IN ORDINARY USE IS NOT A SAFETY NET, IT IS A TIMER.** **THE SECOND DEFECT WAS REAL AND AARON SAW IT:** the game re-shows its legend at EVERY phase change and `.106` re-opened the briefing on each one — **twice in the middle of a live fight (22:15:07, 22:15:46)**. **Briefing is now ONCE PER ATTEMPT;** only a pass through the Game Over screen (field 95) earns another. **AND HIS INSTINCT ABOUT TIMING WAS RIGHT EVEN IF THE MECHANISM WAS NOT: `[BGBTL-HP] armed Squall 440/600 (75%)` — HE HAD ALREADY LOST A QUARTER OF HIS HEALTH BEFORE THE LEGEND EVER APPEARED**, in the seven seconds between the movie starting and the UI showing. So the module now **ALSO ARMS ON THE FMV ITSELF** (`disc01_32h.avi`, which plays nowhere else), from `FreezeWatchdog` — above Update()'s early returns — **freezing the scene before the first punch lands**. The name outlives playback, so the arm is **LATCHED**: without that the module would re-freeze the game the instant the fight ended, **and that is a test, not a hope**. **F10 = SKIP, on Aaron's request** (*"map F10 to the skip function during the mini-game and inform the player in the instructions"*), **DURING the fight as well as on the Game Over screen, and the briefing says so** — a player who cannot make the 700 ms window should not have to lose first to get past the scene. **SkipToVictory THAWS FIRST**, because the transition it writes is executed by the field script, which is exactly what the freeze stops. F9 keeps repeat-briefing / cue-toggle. **INSTRUMENT KEPT: `[BGBTL-KEY]` logs the raw X / Enter / F10 state once a second for the whole briefing** — whether a confirm was never pressed or never seen is not something to guess at twice. **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors and it now ALSO asserts the FMV arms the scene, an UNRELATED movie does not, the legend does NOT re-brief within one attempt, a Game Over DOES, and the latched AVI cannot re-freeze after the fight — plus the existing patch/restore, HP replay and legend-filter checks; garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ PROCESS NOTE WORTH KEEPING: a `g++ ... ; ./probe` shell line RAN A STALE BINARY when the compile failed and reported OK. The probe is now built with `if g++ ...; then` so a failed build can never be read as a pass. It went unnoticed for one edit cycle.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` IS 81,587 BYTES — 333 FROM THE 81,920 HARD FAIL. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT.** **BAT: (1) the briefing should open AS THE MOVIE STARTS, before any damage — `[BGBTL-HP] armed` should read 600/600 for both. (2) THE GAME MUST WAIT: press nothing and you should hear "Press X to begin." every twelve seconds indefinitely, not the fight starting. (3) X resumes with "Game start." (4) The instructions must NOT come back mid-fight, only after a Game Over. (5) F10 skips straight to Galbadia Garden, from the fight or the Game Over screen. If X still does not register, `[BGBTL-KEY]` answers it outright. CAUTION: F10 is the Windows menu key — inert in a full-screen game with no menu bar, but if it does anything odd it moves.**

**Prior (v0.20.106 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: PAUSE, BRIEF, CONFIRM).** Aaron after the `.105` BAT: *"it was really hard to hear the controls with everything going on -- the game battle sounds, the punching sounds of the mini-game, the audio descriptions, the instructions, and the block announcement. Would it be possible to basically pause the game when the scene starts to inform the player how to do this mini-game, and upon pressing X the confirm key in-game it resumes."* **FIVE THINGS WERE COMPETING FOR ONE PAIR OF EARS at the exact moment the player has to learn the controls. Speaking louder or shorter does not fix that; removing four of the five does.** **THE PAUSE IS THE ENGINE'S OWN PAUSE POINT, NOT AN INVENTION:** `field_main_loop` (0x0046FEE0) calls `field_main` (0x00471F70) once per frame at +0x148, and the disassembly shows it **SKIPS THAT CALL ENTIRELY** when FF8's own pause flag `[0x01CD2EBC]` is non-zero (`cmp eax,edi / jne 0x470033 / call 0x471f70`). **So not calling field_main is exactly what the game does when it pauses itself.** We do it with a **ONE-BYTE RET at the entry** — atomic on x86, no trampoline, one write to undo — and field_main takes no arguments (the call site pushes nothing), so a bare RET is a correct no-op. **Rendering, input and the frame limiter all live OUTSIDE it and keep running; only field logic stops — the script VM, the entity animation, the mini-game timer AND ITS PUNCH SOUNDS.** **VERIFIED BY SIGNATURE, NOT BY FAITH:** the five entry bytes are checked against `mov eax,[0x01CE4A64]` (`A1 64 4A CE 01`) before anything is written, and **on mismatch the briefing still speaks — it just does not pause**, and logs why. **THE OTHER FOUR NOISES:** the fight and its punch sounds stop with field_main; **FMV NARRATION IS SUPPRESSED** for the duration via a new `FmvAudioDesc::SetSuppressed` (cues still advance and LOG on schedule so the track stays in sync, they just do not reach the screen reader — an AD cue firing mid-briefing would interrupt it, **the same defect class `.103` fixed for our own cues**); and the music ducks by itself because the existing ducker follows `ScreenReader::IsSpeaking`. **THE FLOW:** legend → freeze → *"Garden battle.
