You fight the soldier hand to hand. W punches. A blocks. X kicks. I will say block when he attacks, and call out health as it changes. Press X to begin. Press F9 to hear this again."* → X → *"Game start."* → thaw. **F9 REPEATS THE BRIEFING** — there are no cues to toggle while frozen so the key is free, and a blind player who missed a word should not have to guess or restart the scene. **THE RESUME WAITS FOR THE KEY TO BE RELEASED, because X is also Kick** — thawing on the press would throw a kick the player did not ask for on the very next frame. A Game Over retry re-shows the legend, which re-opens the briefing. **THE FREEZE CANNOT STRAND THE GAME, AND THAT IS CHECKED RATHER THAN REASONED ABOUT** — a mod that leaves field_main patched has not inconvenienced the player, it has ended their session: **(1) 30-second cap inside the briefing; (2) thaw on EVERY Disarm path including the 5-minute arm cap; (3) a WATCHDOG called from `PollBattlePauseResume`, which sits ABOVE Update()'s `IsOnField()`/`HasFieldStateArrays()` early-returns — because `GardenBattle::Update()` owns the un-pause and lives BELOW them, so if either gate ever went false while frozen the cap would never run.** **KNOWN AND ACCEPTED: freezing field logic does NOT stop the FMV**, so the movie runs on during the briefing and phase one of the fight is correspondingly shorter. That costs the player nothing — they are listening, not fighting, and not being punched either — and the fight continues in bgbtl_1 regardless. Freeze duration is logged so the BAT can measure the drift. **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors and it now MAPS REAL PAGES at 0x00471000 and 0x01CFE000 so the probe exercises the ACTUAL patch path — signature check, 0xC3 written, original byte restored — and asserts the byte is back on BOTH exits (player confirm, and disarm without confirming), alongside the HP replay and the legend filter; garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` IS 81,489 BYTES — 431 FROM THE 81,920 HARD FAIL (+85 for the FmvAudioDesc forward declaration). SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT THAT NEEDS ROOM.** **BAT: (1) when the legend appears EVERYTHING SHOULD GO QUIET and the briefing plays alone — no punch sounds, no FMV narration, music ducked. (2) F9 repeats it. (3) X resumes, with "Game start." (4) Then the fight as in `.105` — Block cues and health. (5) IF ANYTHING GOES WRONG THE GAME MUST UN-PAUSE ON ITS OWN WITHIN ABOUT 30 SECONDS — that is the one failure mode worth knowing about, and it is deliberately bounded. Grep `[BGBTL] field PAUSED` / `field RESUMED`; the gap between them is how far the movie ran ahead.**

**Prior (v0.20.105 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: HEALTH IS FOUND, ENTIRELY OFFLINE, NO BAT SPENT).** Aaron: *"go back to the exe and game files with what you've learned and see if you can find the health values in the mini-game so we don't have to keep doing BAT cycles to pin it down."* **Done. Four int16s in the field variable block: SQUALL max @ `0x01CFE9B8+350`, SQUALL current @ `+354`, FOE max @ `+352`, FOE current @ `+356`.** **THREE INDEPENDENT STEPS, AND THEY AGREE. (1) THE SCRIPT NAMES THE PAIR:** `bgbtl_1`'s `rinoa::gal_hpcalc0` reads damage byte **1028** — which `squall::squ_punched_up0` writes — and does `WRVARW 354`; its structural twin reads byte **1029** — which `gal0::g0_punched_up0` writes — and does `WRVARW 356`. **So 354 falls when SQUALL is hit and 356 when the SOLDIER is hit, and which is which is not a guess.** **(2) THE HOST FIELD INITIALISES ALL FOUR:** `bg2f_31`, pulled out of `field.fs` by byte range and decompressed here exactly as `bgbtl_1` was — `rinoa::m6  PSHM 600, RDVARW 380 -> WRVARW 350 ... -> WRVARW 354` and `rinoa::m11  PSHM 600 -> WRVARW 352 ; PSHM 600 -> WRVARW 356`. **Both start at 600, and SQUALL'S IS 600 ADJUSTED BY VAR 380 — that is the Game Over menu's "Try again with HP+200" — so MAX IS READ, NEVER ASSUMED.** **bg2f_31 ALSO CARRIES `stc0::squ_hpcalc0`, `stc0::gal_hpcalc0` AND `g_hei0::g0_punching0/kicking0/guarding0/punched_up0`, WHICH INDEPENDENTLY CONFIRMS `.104`'s FINDING THAT THE FIGHT RUNS IN THE HOST FIELD BEFORE bgbtl_1 LOADS** — that was inferred from two screenshots; now the host field's own script says it. **(3) THE EXE PINS THE ADDRESSING:** the RDVARW handler at **0x0051CBF0** is `movsx ecx, word ptr [eax + 0x1CFE9B8]` — **operand is a RAW BYTE OFFSET, no scaling, and the read is SIGN-EXTENDED** (RDVARB twin at 0x0051CBB0). **Signed matters: current HP goes NEGATIVE rather than clamping — the `.103` trace caught the soldier at −18 — so percentages clamp.** **AND IT ALL AGREES WITH THE TRACE WE ALREADY HAD:** across 4,096 watched bytes, **354 and 356 were the ONLY non-counter bytes that moved on damage**, every one of the twelve landed hits moved one of them, and they stopped dead when the hits stopped; at arm time the soldier read **400** and Squall **251**, both of 600 — exactly right for seventeen seconds of unwatched fighting before `.104` learned to arm early. **SO HEALTH SHIPS, in Aaron's own short form and on EVERY change rather than at thresholds**, because the measured rate makes it affordable — twelve damage events in 92 seconds, one every two to seven: `"You 75"`, `"You 60, Foe 45"` when both move in one poll, `"Foe down."` at zero. Percentages round to the nearest 5, and **REPLAYING THE REAL RECORDED SEQUENCES THROUGH THE POLICY** gives foe `65 60 50 45 35 30 25 10 5 0` and Squall `40 35 20 5` — **strictly falling, all distinct, so NO recorded change is lost to rounding and none repeats. That replay is now a test, not an argument.** **REMOVED: "Foe hit." / "You hurt."** (the number says who AND how much in one breath) **and the 4,096-byte change detector** (it did its job and its job is over). **"Foe down." now fires on foe HP reaching zero — a real measurement, unlike g0_fall0 which fires at scene setup.** **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors and it now REPLAYS the recorded HP sequences and asserts the policy (0 bad) alongside the legend filter, plus −18 clamping to 0 and an unreadable max announcing nothing; garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **`field_navigation.cpp` UNCHANGED at 81,404 bytes — 516 from the hard fail; everything went into the .inl. THE SPLIT IS STILL OWED.** **BAT: (1) health spoken on every change — "You 60", "Foe 45" — in percentages of 600. (2) "Foe down." when the soldier reaches zero. (3) controls still arrive while the FMV plays (`.104`). (4) F9 toggles speech/tone, volume must NOT change. Grep `[BGBTL-HP]` — the `armed` line gives both fighters' raw HP and max at wake-up, and IF SQUALL'S MAX READS 800 AFTER A "Try again with HP+200" THAT CONFIRMS VAR 380 IS THE RETRY BONUS and closes the last open question in this arc.**

**Prior (v0.20.104 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: THE FIGHT STARTS 15 SECONDS BEFORE ITS FIELD LOADS).** **Aaron took two F11 screenshots at the exact moment he could not hear anything, and they overturn the `.103` diagnosis.** At **21:07:25 and 21:07:28 the mini-game is FULLY ON SCREEN** — legend box, both named health bars, **Squall's bar visibly lower in the second shot than the first** — and **FIELD 152 DOES NOT LOAD UNTIL 21:07:40.** The engine keeps reporting the PREVIOUS field for fifteen seconds while the fight plays over the FMV: `FMV started disc01_32h.avi` 21:07:16 → the game's own `"Punch Block Kick"` legend spoken 21:07:23 → both screenshots 21:07:25/28 → `MAPJUMPO destField=152` 21:07:38 → module armed 21:07:40. **Keying off the field id therefore missed the opening of EVERY fight**, which is exactly *"did not hear the controls or the damage announcements until several hits into the fight"*. **`.103` BLAMED THE FMV AUDIO DESCRIPTION AND WAS WRONG** — the AD genuinely did finish before the field loaded, that part holds, but **THE LATE SIGNAL WAS THE FIELD LOAD, NOT THE SPEECH. Aaron was right that the FMV was involved; the mechanism was not the one either of us named.** **THE TRIGGER IS NOW THE GAME'S OWN LEGEND:** `"Punch"`+`"Block"`+`"Kick"` in one decoded window, fed from `field_dialog`'s `show_dialog` hook **exactly like ChaseAskOverlay and TrainModeAskOverlay** — three strstr calls and out. That is the instant the mini-game UI appears, in whatever field hosts it. **THE HOST FIELD IS RECORDED, NOT ASSUMED** (144 this run) because the lesson of this whole arc is that **the field hosting the fight is not the field the fight is named after**; the field id survives only as a backstop. Disarm on the first field outside `{host, 152, 95}` plus a hard 5-minute cap — **arming on a string means the REQ hook is briefly live in an ordinary Garden field, a REAL WIDENING of the `.102` fence, accepted because that string appears nowhere else in the game and named here rather than buried.** **HP IS NOT IN THE FIELD VARIABLE BLOCK, SETTLED BY MEASUREMENT: the `.103` change detector watched all 4,096 bytes across a full 92-SECOND RUN — AND IT WAS A WIN, THE FIRST EVER RECORDED.** 129 bytes moved; the scans taken at the exact moment of each of the **TWELVE landed hits** show ONLY free-running counters (`soldier-hit 1 changed: [80]26->27`, every single time). Bytes 4/5/8/9/12/80/81/180/181/244/245/3380/3381 step ~20 per tick — frame counters. **Nothing in the block tracks damage.** Percentages need a different hunt (**the HP-bar draw call is the obvious next place**); the differ now filters the counters so the log stays readable. **THE WINNING RUN ALSO KILLED THE LAST BAD CUE: `gal0::g0_fall0` fired EXACTLY ONCE, at +297 ms, IN A RUN AARON WON** — it is never defeat, `.103`'s time gate was treating a symptom, and **"Soldier down." is gone**; the win is announced from the **observed** transition to field 675 instead (*"You win."*). Also measured: **`squ_punched_up0` never fired at all** (Aaron took no damage), and **the soldier attacks RARELY — 3 kicks and 1 punch in 92 seconds against 12 landed hits** — so the announcement load is far lighter than any model predicted, including the offline sim that started this design. **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors and it now EXERCISES the legend filter (matches the legend, rejects ordinary dialogue) rather than only compiling it; garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **`field_navigation.cpp` UNCHANGED at 81,404 bytes — every line of this build went into the .inl — still 516 from the hard fail, THE SPLIT IS STILL OWED. field_dialog.cpp 16,405 and field_dialog_show_dialog.inl 11,442, both far inside the guard.** **BAT: (1) THE CONTROLS SHOULD NOW ARRIVE WHILE THE FMV IS STILL PLAYING, right after the game's own "Punch Block Kick" is read, not fifteen seconds later — that is the whole build. (2) No "Defeated." and no "Soldier down." at any point. (3) "You win." when the fight ends in your favour. (4) F9 still toggles speech/tone mid-fight and volume must NOT change. (5) On a retry from the Game Over screen the keys should be spoken again. Grep `[BGBTL] ARMED` — its timestamp against `[FmvSkip] FMV started` is the number that says whether this worked.**

**Prior (v0.20.103 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: THE TRACE CORRECTED ALMOST EVERY GUESS).** The `.102` instrument worked, and what it measured invalidates most of what `.102` announced — which is what an instrument-first build is for. **THE FMV WAS NOT THE PROBLEM.** Aaron: *"Not hearing the announcement of the keys or the block until the game is partway done. This may be because of the audio descriptions for the background fmv."* **The mod log says otherwise: `disc01_32h.avi` and its AD finished at 20:42:07, THREE SECONDS BEFORE the field loaded at 20:42:10, and the last AD cue spoke at 20:42:00.** No overlap; **no AD suppression is needed and none was added.** **THE MOD WAS TALKING OVER ITSELF**, and the four log lines say it exactly: `"Defeated."` (the label-47 bug) → `"Block"` (label 48, +328 ms) → `"Punch, W. Block, A. Kick, X."` (our 400 ms entry delay) → `"Block"` (label 49, +766 ms) **which cut the controls line off ~360 ms in — Aaron heard about "Punch, W."** (Separately: at 20:41:54, mid-FMV, the mod already spoke the game's own legend *"Punch Block Kick"*, buried between two AD cues — the information existed, sixteen seconds early and unlabelled.) **FOUR MEASURED CORRECTIONS. (1) `gal0::g0_fall0` (47) IS NOT DEFEAT** — it fires at **+125 ms in BOTH runs** as scene setup (the soldier drops into frame), so `.102` announced *"Defeated."* a tenth of a second into every attempt; now gated on elapsed > 4 s and logged as setup when it is not. **(2) THE REACTION WINDOW IS 700 ms, MEASURED, NOT GUESSED:** `squall::squ_timeover0` is the per-round expiry and lands a strikingly consistent interval after the soldier acts — **703, 703, 703, 562 ms** across the two runs. **That is the number the cue toggle existed to find.** A one-word cue only just fits inside it; a sentence does not. **(3) THE FIGHT IS ~10 s WITH EVENTS 1.2–1.7 s APART**, not the 900 ms exchange rate the offline announcer sim assumed — **there is far more speech budget than predicted**, which is why hit reports now ship as well. **(4) THE SOLDIER ACTS ON 48/49/50:** 48 `g0_punching0` fires once in the opening, **49 `g0_kicking0` is the recurring attack**, 50 `g0_guarding0` is HIM defending and gets no cue. **THE CONTROLS LINE IS NOW FIRST AND PROTECTED:** spoken at **0 ms** (was 400) as **"W punch. A block. X kick."** — key first, because the key is the part that must survive — and **every cue is suppressed until `ScreenReader::IsSpeaking()` goes false** (hard cap 3.5 s). Gating on real speech state rather than a guessed interval matters because Aaron's NVDA rate is far faster than any default; a fixed timer would waste most of the opening. **Every suppressed cue is logged, so the gate's cost is visible rather than invisible.** **HIT REPORTS SHIP, HEALTH STILL DOES NOT: "Foe hit." on `g0_punched_up0`, "You hurt." on `squ_punched_up0`** — which answers the actual complaint behind the health request, since **the game plays the SAME hit sound for both fighters** and so carries no information about who took the damage. A report will not interrupt a Block cue less than 450 ms old; only the cue is time-critical. **THE HP HUNT RESTARTS PROPERLY: `.102` dumped two guessed windows and BOTH WERE DEAD** — 1020..1040 zero all fight bar a transient 1 at 1031/1032, and 330..350 never moved at all. Replaced by a **CHANGE DETECTOR over 4,096 bytes** that reports only what moves and prints **one summary line of every byte that moved all fight** — HP cannot hide from it, and it stays quiet because almost nothing in there changes. **F7 WAS ALREADY TAKEN AND THE LOG PROVES IT:** one press produced `"Block cue: tone."` AND `"Music volume 30 percent"` (`GameAudio::VolumeDown`, dinput8.cpp). **Rebound to F9** — a full source sweep shows F1–F8, F11, F12 all bound and **only F9 and F10 free**; F9 chosen because F10 activates the window menu on Windows. **Also: the tone cue now runs `Beep()` ON A WORKER THREAD** — `Beep` is synchronous and was stalling the game thread 60 ms, a twelfth of the reaction window, in the one scene where that matters (`PlaySound` would be nicer but `deploy.bat` does not link `winmm.lib`). **STILL A GUESS, AND SAID SO: "Block" on 48/49, and label 47 as defeat once gated — BOTH BAT RUNS WERE LOSSES, so no winning trace exists yet.** **Verified: minigame_bgbtl_compile 0 errors (probe extended with CreateThread/CloseHandle/IsSpeaking stubs); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` IS 81,404 BYTES — 516 FROM THE 81,920 HARD FAIL (+19 for the IsSpeaking forward declaration). SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT THAT NEEDS ROOM.** **BAT: same scene, log still matters more than the experience. (1) On entry, "W punch. A block. X kick." COMPLETE, before anything else speaks. (2) NO "Defeated." at the start. (3) "Block" when the soldier attacks; "Foe hit." / "You hurt." on damage. (4) F9 mid-fight swaps to the tone and volume must NOT change — with a 700 ms window the real question is which of the two Aaron can act on. (5) On a loss, F9 on the Game Over screen skips to Galbadia Garden (the game's own menu also offers "Try again with HP+200", and its hint says most HP when time runs out wins — the Skip is a last resort, not the only way through). Grep `[BGBTL]`, `[BGBTL-REQ]`, `[BGBTL-VARS]`; the line that unlocks health announcements is the single `moved during fight:` summary at the end.**

**Prior (v0.20.102 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #minigame-bgbtl: THE GARDEN-BATTLE FIGHT, INSTRUMENT FIRST).** Aaron reached the punch/kick/block mini-game during the Garden battle and it is unplayable blind. **Field 152 `bgbtl_1`; Game Over is field 95 `testbl6`; winning MAPJUMP3s to field 675 `ggback1`.** **ENTIRELY OFFLINE ANALYSIS, NO BAT SPENT:** the field archive was pulled out of `field.fs` by byte range and decompressed here, and **`bgbtl_1.sym` names all 81 script methods** — every event Aaron asked for has a named script behind it (`squ_punching0/kicking0/guarding0`, `g0_punching0/kicking0/guarding0`, `squ/g0_punched_up0`, `g0_fall0`, `squ_hpcalc0`, `gal_hpcalc0`). The fight is driven by entity 12's `director0::talk` REQ-ing them, so **the whole event stream is ONE opcode: 0x014 REQ, operand = target entity, top of VM stack = label. CONFIRMED 18 OF 18** — every REQ in the driver resolves to a label owned by the entity it names. New offline tooling in `minigame/`: `jsmdis.py` (built from the mod's own parser so offline and runtime cannot drift), `SCRIPT_MAP.txt`, `optable.json`, `announcer.py`. **THE ANNOUNCER SIM EARNED ITS KEEP BEFORE A LINE OF C++: announcing every move interrupting, as originally specified, cuts off 97% of lines at a 900 ms exchange rate (34 of 35) — unusable, and known so before anything was built.** Aaron's revised policy — *"just announce 'block' when the player should block an incoming punch, and other than that we just announce health each time it changes ... keep it short like 'You 75, Foe 50'"* — measures at **11%**, and the single casualty is the controls line, **which is why it is now spoken ON ENTRY rather than at the first exchange.** **HOT-PATH SAFETY IS WHY THE HOOK IS FENCED:** `field_nav_mapjump_diag.inl` records that hooking SET3 *"is hot-path enough that ANY interception hangs the infirmary cutscene"*, and **REQ is far hotter than SET3 — it is how every script in every field is invoked.** So the hook is installed **ONLY while bgbtl_1 is loaded**, removed on any field change, and its body reads two words into a **256-slot lock-free ring and chains** — no logging, no allocation, no speech, no locks inside it; everything else happens on the mod's own tick, and the read is inside `__try` so a bad VM context drops one event rather than the game. **THE SKIP IS EXACT, NOT INVENTED:** `director0::talk` ends with `push 675, 1019, 3384, 0, 128` then `MAPJUMP3 77` — field 675 is `ggback1`, and **the `.101` BAT log confirms it independently** (`[fieldload] id=675 name='ggback1'` right after Aaron won). **F7 on the Game Over screen writes exactly that transition block (0x01CE4760)** — no invented story flag, no forced FMV jump; precedent is the Dollet Chase and Timber Train skips. **F7 during the fight swaps the Block cue between speech and a tone (`Beep(880,60)`), because the reaction window is NOT YET MEASURED and one visit to this scene is far too expensive to spend finding out** — both cues ship and Aaron switches in place. **HEALTH IS DELIBERATELY NOT ANNOUNCED YET: the script never reads or writes HP — it is engine-side — and announcing a guessed address is worse than announcing nothing.** This build **dumps the field variable block (0x01CFE9B8) on entry, on every `*_punched_up0`, and once a second**; the HP bytes are the ones that only ever fall, and fall exactly when a hit fires. **Build 1 traces, build 2 announces from measurement.** The same run settles the other unknown: **`g0_punching0` is not a single punch, it is the soldier's LOOP**, REQ-ing `g0_kicking0`/`g0_guarding0` from inside itself before branching back — so 48/49 is a **shipped guess** the trace corrects, and nothing else changes when it does. **CORRECTED ON THE RECORD: `field.fl` index is NOT the field id** (`bgbtl_1` is fl 123 / id 152), which invalidates an earlier claim comparing Galbadia Garden fl indices against trigger-table ids. **Also retracted, and worth keeping written: the mod's `field_archive_jsm_opnames.inl` is CORRECT** — the 394-entry table at `0x00B8DE4C` that appeared to contradict it is a **second-stage expression dispatcher reached through opcode 0x013**; the real field opcode table is `0x00B8DE94`, 18 entries later, verified on four opcodes against the mod's own BAT-resolved log. **Verified: NEW `tests/minigame_bgbtl_compile.cpp` 0 errors — no host harness compiles `field_navigation.cpp`, so this is the ONLY pre-MSVC syntax check the new module gets (the same gap that let the `.89` declaration-order bug reach a build); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad, catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures, aboard_test and world_map_harness pass, all unchanged; include order 847 < 893 < 900 < 1260.** **⚠ `field_navigation.cpp` IS 81,385 BYTES — 535 FROM THE 81,920 HARD FAIL. SPLIT BEFORE THE NEXT EDIT THAT NEEDS ROOM.** **BAT — expensive to reach, so this run is designed to answer everything at once; play the fight normally and SEND `ff8_field.log`, the trace matters more than the experience this time. (1) On entry: "Punch, W. Block, A. Kick, X." (2) During: "Block" when the soldier attacks — if it fires at the wrong moments that is the expected outcome of a guess, and the log fixes it. (3) Press F7 once during the fight for the tone cue, and say which one you could actually react to. (4) "Defeated." when the soldier goes down. (5) If you lose, F7 on the Game Over screen should drop you into Galbadia Garden as though you had won. Grep `[BGBTL]`, `[BGBTL-REQ]`, `[BGBTL-VARS]`.**

**Prior (v0.20.101 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: THE WHITE SEED SHIP HAS A WINDOW TOO, AND BOTH ITS EDGES ARE ALREADY-VALIDATED SIGNALS).** Aaron: *"the White SeeD Ship -- it only becomes available on Disc 3 and disappears from the World Map when the player receives the Ragnarok."* Until now it was offered **unconditionally**, so on discs 1 and 2 the mod would drive a blind player to a ship that is not there — the same defect class `.99` retired for the two Garden sites. **BOTH EDGES WERE ALREADY IN HAND: opening = disc ≥ 3 (savemap header +0x44, the same field the Galbadia window uses); closing = `ragnarok_pos` going live (WORLDMAP struct +0x18).** **THE CLOSING EDGE NEEDED NO NEW DISCOVERY** — `ragnarok_pos` is the exact analogue of `bgu_pos`, whose *zero while parked, live the moment you have it* behaviour is **proven across all 41 saves**, and Ragnarok is empty in every one of them, correctly, because it is a disc-3 vehicle Aaron does not have yet. **So unlike Mobile Galbadia Garden, NEITHER EDGE HERE IS A GUESS** — both are fields whose semantics were established by measurement earlier in this same arc. That is the return on having read the saves properly. `WmStoryRetired` gains one more name and remains the single predicate every builder asks (the `.88` rule). `[STORY]` now carries both windows on one line, verdict beside evidence: `... -> Mobile Galbadia Garden PRESENT | Ragnarok not held -> White SeeD Ship absent`. **Verified: catalog_story_test 13 checks / 0 failures (static/mobile/aboard + 4 Galbadia states + 5 White SeeD states covering BOTH edges); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass; no berth, grid, route or planner change — this build only gates catalog membership.** **⚠ THE WHITE SEED SHIP DESTINATION HAS NEVER BEEN EXERCISED** — disc-3 content, Aaron is on disc 2 — so its coordinate (4887,51285) and berth (2944,53376) came from the original research document and have never been driven to. **DOES IT MOVE? NO** — Aaron: *"the White SeeD Ship is immobile for the time it is on the World Map"* — so a fixed marker is the right model and that question is closed. **WHAT IS NOT CLOSED IS WHETHER THE MARKER IS IN THE RIGHT PLACE:** (4887,51285) is terrain 7 grass, 479 above sea with water 58 cells inside 1,000u (right kind of place), but **texture page 128 with NO page-8 feature near it and NO wmsetus arrival record within 4,472 units** (the nearest is record 16, Centra Ruins' own). **That is the same profile as the two markers that turned out wrong this week** — Shumi's old coordinate and the page-8 candidate for Galbadia Garden. Not proof (the Galbadia trigger had this profile and was right), but it is unverified in exactly the way that has already cost eleven builds. **FIRST THING TO CHECK ON REACHING DISC 3.** **BAT: includes `.100`, which was never built — one build covers both. (1) Load slot2_save30: Mobile Galbadia Garden listed, driving to it starts the Garden battle. (2) Load the post-battle save (disc 3): absent, and `[STORY]` gives the post-battle values of all three candidate bytes. (3) IMMEDIATELY CHECKABLE ON ANY DISC-2 SAVE: the White SeeD Ship must now be ABSENT from the catalog.**

**Prior (v0.20.100 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: MOBILE GALBADIA GARDEN, AND IT EXISTS FOR A WINDOW).** Aaron: *"That trigger is where Mobile Galbadia Garden is located on the World Map, so that is how it should be identified in the catalog. Also, once the battle of the Gardens is over, Galbadia Garden disappears from the world map forever."* **COORDINATE (−24982,65761)**, pinned from the `.99` BAT where auto-drive to Edea's House crossed it unasked: `[DRIVE] Manual field entry at (-24982,65761) -- nearest location Edea's House is 3437u away (> 3000), not capturing` + `[fieldload] id=253 name='bgsido_4'` / *B-Garden - Headmaster's Office 5* / Nida *"Squall, take a look."* **Driving onto it loads field 253, so ARRIVAL NEEDS NO SPECIAL HANDLING** — the drive already ends when a field loads. **drive_in like FH: park IS the marker, walk 0**, planner cell (127,414) WALK clearance 5, 16 gexec3 routes from four starts, **zero replans**. **NO MAP ARCHAEOLOGY COULD HAVE FOUND IT: terrain 7, texture page 128, nothing but more of the same for 1,000 units, nearest page-8 patch 5,249 units away. The page-8 method finds places the map DRAWS; a scripted event zone is a pure coordinate test with no geometry — and the pre-BAT candidate (−29632,70108) was 6,365 UNITS WRONG.** It was flagged as a lead not a conclusion, and it was wrong. Route crossing left alone on Aaron's instruction — it is a trigger the player wants. **THE GATE IS A TWO-SIDED WINDOW.** Closing edge given outright: *"by the time the battle of the Gardens is over, you are on disc 3"* — savemap header **+0x44, 0-indexed uint32**, disc-2 transition at slot2_save11 in the 41-save sweep; disc ≥ 3 hides it unconditionally. **OPENING EDGE IS A CANDIDATE, NARROWED NOT CONFIRMED:** bytes identical in ALL 40 saves before slot2_save30 and changed in slot2_save30 (the only save where it is present; every earlier one is either "at its original site" or "vanished") cut an 84-byte diff to **three fields — +0x0B94 (0000→01b7, append-only uint16 list), +0x0E8D (00→02, ISOLATED byte with static neighbours), +0x0FDC (00→03, churning region)**. **+0x0E8D ships** — the only one shaped like a state enum, zero across 100,000 s of play and stepping to 2 exactly when the Garden appears. **THE DISC RULE MAKES BEING WRONG CHEAP: a wrong byte can only show it at the wrong time WITHIN disc 2, never after.** All three are logged every catalog build (`[STORY] disc=.. gg[0x0E8D]=.. alt[0x0B94]=.. alt[0x0FDC]=.. -> PRESENT/absent`) — verdict and evidence on one line, the `.93` rule. **LATENT HARNESS BUG FOUND BY ADDING A BERTH: `garden_harness` iterated `s_gardenParks` with the hardcoded CATALOG count (39) instead of `GARDEN_PARK_COUNT`; the two happened to match, and a 26th berth would have silently skipped one.** **Verified: catalog_story_test 8 checks / 0 failures (static/mobile/aboard + all four window states); garden_harness 26 ok / 0 bad; aboard_test and world_map_harness pass; route regression NOT re-run because the only berth change is one drive_in row, which regress84 skips by construction exactly as it skips FH, and the other 24 rows are byte-identical.** **BAT NEEDS A SAVE RELOAD — the window has closed on the live playthrough. (1) Load slot2_save30: Mobile Galbadia Garden MUST be listed and driving to it should start the Garden battle. (2) Load the current post-battle save (disc 3): it MUST be absent, and the `[STORY]` line gives the post-battle values of all three candidates — THAT is the missing measurement, and it costs a save load.**

**Prior (v0.20.99 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: THE CATALOG WAS NOT STORY-AWARE, AND THE SIGNAL WAS ALREADY IN THE FILE).** Aaron: *"The original location for Balamb Garden ... is no longer accessible since Garden is now mobile. This entry shouldn't be in the catalog anymore at this point in the game."* Same for Galbadia Garden, *"essentially vanished from the world map at this point."* **Both were still offered as fixed coordinates — choosing either would drive a blind player sixty kilometres to empty ground.** **NO NEW MACHINERY WAS NEEDED: `bgu_pos` (WORLDMAP +0x24), the same read that already builds "Mobile Balamb Garden", is ZERO while the Garden is parked and live the moment it moves — and that is now MEASURED ACROSS THE WHOLE PLAYTHROUGH.** **FF8 STEAM SAVES READ OFFLINE: an `.ff8` is a 4-byte length + LZS -> exactly 8,192 bytes, savemap at +0x184** (anchored on locID/HP/level/gil/save-count/played-time, cross-checked on two saves 16 min apart: save count 90->92, played 40,780->41,718 s). All **39 of Aaron's saves** read directly, no diagnostic build, no BAT: **bgu=(0,0) in slot1_save01 through slot2_save27 — 37 saves, every one empty — and (20271,−24355) in slot2_save28/29.** That coordinate is the exact `GARDEN_START` the berth generator has used since `.84`, a free second confirmation. Galbadia rides the same signal on Aaron's testimony that *"G-Garden is already mobile by the time B-Garden becomes mobile"*, so no window exists where the Garden is mobile and the Galbadia site is occupied. **ALSO SETTLED, NEGATIVELY AND CHEAPLY: the four unexamined WORLDMAP slots (+0x0C/+0x3C/+0x48/+0x54) are EMPTY IN ALL 39 SAVES.** Galbadia Garden has no position record there; the tidy "one slot is both flag and coordinate" hypothesis **died before a line of code was written for it**, and the Edea's House reappearance will need a coordinate AND a flag of its own. The struct is now pinned as eight 12-byte slots, 0x00–0x5F (+0x60 is not a position record). **ONE PREDICATE, `WmStoryRetired`, ASKED BY EVERY BUILDER** — the catalog assembles in three places and the ABOARD path rebuilds from `s_locations` rather than the compacted `s_catalog`, so it asks a second time. **That is the exact shape of the `.88` bug (a condition armed in one place, asked about in three, which silently vanished Shumi from the catalog it had just been added to)**, so aboard gets its own check rather than being assumed to follow. **HARNESS GAP WORTH NAMING: NO HOST HARNESS COMPILED `world_catalog.inl` AT ALL** — `catalog_harness.cpp` is the FIELD catalog, and the world catalog has only ever been compiled by MSVC, which is the same gap that let the `.89` declaration-order bug reach a build. **NEW `tests/catalog_story_test.cpp`** compiles the real file and checks all three states: `STATIC 39, both present` / `MOBILE 38, both gone` / `Mobile Balamb Garden present` / `ABOARD 37, both gone` — **4 checks, 0 failures** (38 not 37 because the mobile branch also APPENDS the ride you can actually reach). **Verified: world_map_harness PASS; garden_harness and aboard_test untouched and passing — this build touches no grid bit, no berth, no route; all files inside the 80 KB guard.** **BAT: stand on the world map and open the catalog — BALAMB GARDEN AND GALBADIA GARDEN MUST BOTH BE GONE, with "Mobile Balamb Garden" still there pointing at where you parked. Then board and check again — same two absent. Grep `[STORY]`.**

**Prior (v0.20.98 — BAT'd AND CONFIRMED. #80's SHUMI ARC IS CLOSED, eleven builds after it opened).** Aaron: *"Finally made it to Shumi Village!"* **`[DRIVE] Arrival via game-mode (mode=1 MODE_FIELD, fieldId=0x03A5, fieldName='tmdome1', target=Shumi Village, dist=171, lastPos=(12774,-83801), elapsed=563ms)`. THE GAME NAMED IT.** Garden parked (13371,−81750) `parkBit=1 canDisembark=1` walk 2,307 **0 replans**; 67 s for the Garden leg, 3 s for the walk. **AND THE TRIGGER GEOMETRY CLOSES THE `.91`/`.92` LOOP EXACTLY: the field fired at (12774,−83801), 524 UNITS FROM THE DOME CENTRE, while record 20 is 726 out. The entry trigger lives between those two numbers** — which is precisely why standing on the arrival point for fifty seconds did nothing and why crossing the apron does. The arrival point is outside the trigger BY DESIGN; the apron is inside it. **Correction for the record: Shumi's fields start at 933 (0x3A5 `tmdome1`), not 934.** **STILL OPEN, same class, none urgent:** `Chocobo Forest 3` (51893,−3959) has no page-8 patch within 3,936u while an unclaimed 32-cell terrain-1 forest sits at **(51968,−64256)** with arrival record 35 (52145,−63615) — the walkthroughs' *"North of Trabia Garden"* forest; pointing CF3 at that record gives berth (53632,−61568), walk 2,485, corridor clear. `Chocobo Forest 4` (97253,−48250) sits by a 47-cell terrain-2 patch that is not the forest signature. `Deling City`'s marker is not foot-walkable and gets snapped 128u — Shumi's defect, two orders of magnitude smaller, and working today.

**How it was fixed (v0.20.98 — #80: THE MARKER WAS THE BUILDING; IT HAD TO BE THE DOOR).** `.97` BAT, Aaron: *"Garden landed on the land mass and said it was close by, but when I got off the Garden and tried to use auto-drive to walk to the Shumi Village it seemed to run into a wall, and ended up going into the Chocobo Forest nearby while trying to correct."* **HALF OF `.97` IS SETTLED: the Garden crossed the world, landed on Winter Island and parked at (12952,−81477), `parkBit=1 canDisembark=1`, walk 2,572, ZERO REPLANS — the first time any build has put the hull within walking distance of Shumi. (b) IS SOLVED.** The walk is not. **THE WEDGE, in the log's own numbers:** `final approach zone (dist=994)` then `Hard wedge` at **778**, and every un-wedge burst pushes it OUT — 778 → 1,305 → 1,901 → 2,478 — until it drifts south-west and trips the Chocobo Forest trigger at (10729,−80800). The frames before the wedge name the wall: the walker is at **(12137,−83179) → (11962,−83245), moving WEST while the bearing to target reads 139**, on tri #503, ground rising −977 → −1013 as it slides. That is a hull pressed against a face. **CAUSE: the goal was not walkable ground at all — (12274,−83958) is the CENTRE OF THE BUILDING FOOTPRINT**, so the planner snapped to whatever was nearest and its last waypoint (11712,−83392) sat 798 units short on the blind south-west side. **THE DOME HAS ONE DOOR AND IT IS ON THE EAST.** Flood the foot mask from the Garden's own step-off with the ENGINE's gate — the navmesh logs it at load, `66900 engine-gate-blocked connections (>=200 over ~190u)` — and exactly **FIVE cells of the structure come back reachable: (12736, −83776/−83904/−83968/−84032/−84288), all height −878, terrain 9, flat snow.** One 640-unit strip on the east face; everything else around the dome clears the gate and is wall. **And wmsetus record 20 (13000,−83977) sits 264 units due east of that strip's middle — you step out of Shumi, cross the apron, and you are on the world map. THE APRON IS THE ENTRANCE.** Destination is now **(12736,−83968)**. **THE BERTH MOVES TOO, for the reason `.96` already taught:** (12672,−81536) parks fine but sits WEST of the dome, so the walk ran at the blind face — the walking version of the beach, same destination, open from one side and sealed from the other. New berth **(13184,−81536), walk 2,472**, 512 units east of the one that already parked cleanly. **TWO NEW GENERATOR CONSTRAINTS, AND ONLY ONE GETS A VOTE. (5) THE MARKER MUST BE FOOT-WALKABLE UNDER THE ENGINE GATE — real, and it IS the `.97` defect:** `gen_berths.py` has always snapped an unwalkable marker to the nearest walkable cell SILENTLY, which is exactly how a building footprint became a destination; it now prints every time it fires (it also fires on Deling City, 128 units — worth a look later). **(6) CLEAR FINAL-APPROACH CORRIDOR — ADVISORY ONLY.** As a scoring rule it reshuffled seven berths, and audited against the SHIPPED table it flags **Balamb Town, Timber and Chocobo Forest 2 — all of which Aaron has arrived at fine**, because a normal town's trigger fires well before you touch the building. So it is reported, never scored, and Shumi's berth was picked with it BY HAND because here the BAT is the evidence that the approach side matters. **This is deliberately the opposite of `.90`–`.95`: a model that disagrees with a BAT does not get to steer.** **Verified: parity clean; 24 of 39 berths and the other 23 UNCHANGED from `.97`; 25 ok / 0 bad; `Shumi plan from Balamb: OK (381 waypoints)`; `Shumi berth to Chocobo Forest 2 berth: 1055 units OK (same shore)`; aboard_test passes.** **BAT: drive to Shumi Village (the Garden leg is known good — expect a park near (13184,−81536) and "26 hundred units north"), step off, walk. The approach now comes up the EAST side. Either the village loads, or the drive announces arrival STANDING ON THE APRON at (12736,−83968) — and the doorstep is a far better place to be stuck than 778 units out on the back wall.**

**Prior (v0.20.97 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: SHUMI VILLAGE WAS NEVER WHERE THE CATALOG SAID IT WAS).** Aaron, after nine builds: *"Go back to the game files and exe … Keep going until you have pinned down a definitive answer on (a) where Shumi is located on the world map and (b) how to reach it using the Garden."* **He was right to push. THE COORDINATE WAS WRONG, and every build from `.88` on was engineering a cliff climb to reach a place that is not there.** **(a) THE WORLD MAP LABELS ITS OWN SETTLEMENTS: byte 14 of a `wmx` polygon is the texture page, and every named place sits on a patch of PAGE 8.** `wmsetus` section 8 is a 62-record table of `int32 X, int32 Z, int16 height, int16 facing` — the spot the game stands you at when you leave a field — and the decode is confirmed by its heights matching the walkmesh under them (record 0: −658 vs −652; record 1: −304 vs −304). **Line the two up and the arrival record is ALWAYS 600–1,300 units OUTSIDE the page-8 patch** — Balamb Town 1,102, Timber 993, Deling City 1,280, Winhill 809, Dollet 1,072, Trabia Garden 1,221 — because if it were inside the entry trigger you would re-enter the town the instant you left it. **Winter Island has exactly TWO page-8 patches: 32 cells of terrain 1 at (10752,−80384), and 71 cells of terrain 29 at (12274,−83958).** 32 cells of page 8 is the **Chocobo Forest signature** (seven such patches map-wide); the 71-cell patch is town-sized (Winhill 68, Balamb Town 135, Trabia Garden 184) and is the only other structure on the island. **SHUMI VILLAGE IS AT (12274,−83958).** The shipped marker (10362,−76967) was **7,248 units north-west, on no feature at all, on a DIFFERENT foot landmass** — the 7,034-cell cliff plateau `.88`–`.96` kept measuring. **THE `.92` CONCLUSION WAS WRONG AND THE NULL RESULT IS WHY: record 20 (13000,−83977), where Aaron stood for fifty seconds with nothing loading, is 725 units due EAST of the dome — exactly where the game stands you when you walk OUT of Shumi. He was outside the door.** *A null result at one point is evidence about that point, not about the hypothesis* — and the offset that explains it was in the same table the point came from. **(b) ORDINARILY. THERE IS NO BEACH TO CLIMB.** Berth **(12672,−81536), walk 2,435, clear 6**, on the **same 6,216-cell landmass the Garden already parks on for Chocobo Forest 2, 1,280 units from that berth** — ground Aaron has already driven to. **16/16 `gexec3` routes arrive, zero replans, no beach exception, no approach point. NO `beach_climb` BERTH REMAINS IN THE TABLE** (the machinery stays in source, inert; the harness prints the count so that stays visible). **BYPRODUCT, NOT CHANGED THIS BUILD:** the seventh 32-cell patch, **(51968,−64256)**, has no catalog entry and matches the walkthroughs' *"North of Trabia Garden"* forest, while `Chocobo Forest 3` (51893,−3959) has no page-8 patch within 3,936 units — **two more wrong markers of exactly the class just fixed, flagged for Aaron rather than swept into this commit.** **Verified: parity clean incl. PARTIAL/CLEAR; 25 ok / 0 bad; `beach_climb berths in the table: 0`; `Shumi plan from Balamb: OK (381 waypoints)`; `Shumi berth to Chocobo Forest 2 berth: 1280 units OK (same shore)`; aboard_test passes; regress84 runs 24 berths with NOTHING SKIPPED.** **BAT: board, confirm the catalog still says 25 with Shumi in it, and drive to Shumi Village. Expect an ORDINARY arrival — `parkBit=1 canDisembark=1`, ~2.4 km walk — and NO `[GDBEACH]` lines at all. Then step off and take the foot drive in: the village should load on the approach from the east, before you reach the dome.**

**Prior (v0.20.96 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: THE APPROACH SIDE WAS THE WHOLE PROBLEM, AND THE SCREENSHOTS SHOW IT).** Aaron drove the hull in by hand and photographed it: **Balamb Garden sitting on the snow in a cove with a cliff wall behind it**, and his logged position there was **(3150,-81780) = planner cell (703,524), reachable water, 338 UNITS FROM THE BERTH.** The automated survey, running straight at the berth with every check off, sticks at **(2294,-82228) = cell (705,521), 572 units out on the SOUTH-WEST side.** **Same berth, two sides, one of them open.** The cell map says why: the wedge (704..707, 522..524) is **WALK land the flood cannot reach, ringed ENTIRELY by PARTIAL cells**, and the ONE mouth from reachable water is **(704,524) entered from (703,524) to its north** — exactly where Aaron ended up. **Everything the planner did approached from the wrong quarter, which is why `.90` through `.95` kept measuring a wall: IT IS ONLY A WALL FROM ONE DIRECTION.** Four builds of survey iteration were measuring the closed side. **FIX: `dock_x/dock_y` — unused by anything but a drive_in until now — carry the APPROACH POINT for a beach_climb berth.** `Garden_Plan` routes to **(3200,-81792)** instead of the berth; the beach push arms on proximity to THAT (`GD_BEACH_ARM_DIST` 500) rather than raw distance to the berth, so it starts from the open side with **362 units to cover instead of 572 through a closed one.** **HONEST STATUS: each fix in `.90`/`.93`/`.94`/`.95` was a real defect — bearing from the planner cursor, full throttle, the one-cell grid wall — but none was going to work while the hull kept arriving on the sealed side. THE THING THAT BROKE THE LOOP WAS A PHOTOGRAPH, and it took ten seconds to read. WHEN A SPATIAL QUESTION RESISTS FOUR ROUNDS OF INSTRUMENTATION, ASK FOR A PICTURE.** Also: `offline/regress84.py` now **SKIPS beach_climb berths and says so out loud** — `gexec3` has no `GdBeachOpen` and no approach-point routing, so such a berth is unrepresentable offline and a silent drop would read as "everything passes". **Verified: 25 ok / 0 bad; Shumi plan OK (360 waypoints); aboard_test passes; parity clean including PARTIAL and CLEAR.** **BAT: Shumi Village. Expect the route to end at (3200,-81792) on the NORTH side, then `[GDBEACH]` from there — 362 units to cover, not 572.**

**Prior (v0.20.95 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: THE WHOLE SHUMI BEACH WAS SHUT BY ONE CELL, AND IT WAS MY GRID SHUTTING IT).** `.94` measured cleanly at last: three runs all closing **1963 -> 1194 -> 936 -> 678** and then **STICKING at 670-690 for ten seconds**, `berthOff` repeatedly brought to **11, 16, 43** — pointed straight at the berth, throttle down, **sliding sideways**, `gate=208` and `cls=0x31` throughout. Closest approach **670** (was 1,029 in `.93`, so the throttle fix worked). That is the signature of a wall — **and the wall is in my grid.** The stuck position sits on planner cell **(705,521)**: `sub(1410,1042) terr=29 h=-7 NOT Garden-masked`, the other three quarters terrain 9 Garden ground at -355..-380. A 256 cell is WALK only when ALL FOUR sub-points carry the bit, so it reads solid — **and THE ENGINE HAD THE HULL STANDING ON IT.** Everything past it — (705,522), (705,523), the berth (704,523) — is fully WALK terrain-9 at -390..-470. **The only beach onto that island was shut by ONE cell whose fourth quarter is a 128-unit sliver of shore skirt**, and `GdLineClear` refuses every chord through a non-WALK cell, so the executor would not drive the last 670 units of a ramp it was already standing on. **AARON'S MANUAL DRIVING IS WHAT PROVED IT: of 718 logged hull positions in that region, 190 SIT ON CELLS THE MODEL CALLS NON-WALK** — he had been driving across "solid rock" for minutes. **FIX, deliberately tiny: new `GDC_PARTIAL` (0x80)** = 1..3 of 4 sub-points Garden-masked, **6,939 cells map-wide**; `GdBeachOpen(idx)` lets a step or a chord cross one **ONLY when a beach_climb berth is armed and ONLY within 3 cells of it.** Open water, solid rock and every other berth are untouched — the conservative all-four rule still governs everywhere the planner plans. Mirrored in `gsim3` as `PARTIALp`, guarded by `parity_check` (6,939 both sides). **Fourth iteration on this survey and the FIRST where the obstacle was real rather than my steering. Whether the Garden climbs the ramp once the mouth is open is STILL UNMEASURED — but the model is no longer standing in the way of finding out.** **Verified: parity clean including PARTIAL and CLEAR; 25 ok / 0 bad; Shumi plan OK (360 waypoints); aboard_test passes.** **BAT: Shumi Village. Expect the approach to carry PAST 670 units this time — then CLIMBED, or an honest "did not get there" with a closest approach well inside it.**

**Prior (v0.20.94 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: THE BEACH RUN WAS ORBITING, AND THE TURNING CIRCLE IS WHY).** `.93` steered at the berth correctly and still could not converge. **The trace is an orbit and says so twice: berthOff starts at 53 and 182 — pointed STRAIGHT at it — the hull closes to 1,194 and 1,035, then berthOff blows out to 932, 1350, 1754, and BOTH RUNS settle into the same wandering loop and end at the IDENTICAL coordinate (1791,-80541).** Two independent runs converging on one attractor is an orbit, not an obstruction. **The geometry was already written down in this very file: the Garden turns at most 9 units of heading per frame and cruises at 32, so its turning circle is ~1,300 units — driving flat out at a target 1,000-2,000 units away means the circle is WIDER THAN THE APPROACH and it can only spiral past.** Throttle is now gated on the same forward cone the normal executor uses, with the `.86` pivot escape still applying since a stationary hull cannot always turn; **the probes and the wall-follow stay OFF**, which is the whole point of the run. **THREE STEERING ITERATIONS, THREE REAL DEFECTS, none of them the engine:** `.90` took its bearing from the PLANNER CURSOR not the berth; `.93` fixed the bearing but kept FULL THROTTLE and spiralled; `.94` disciplines the throttle. **`mv` has been high in every single run (189/899 on the last), so nothing observed so far is a refusal. Closest approach to date: 1,029 units.** **SPLIT: `world_garden.inl` hit 80,357 bytes, 1.5 KB from the 81,920 hard fail, with this survey still iterating** — per DEVNOTES, split BEFORE the edit that needs the room: the collision probes move to **`world_garden_probe.inl`** (4.4 KB), they read only the grid and depend on no executor state, nothing else moves, `world_garden.inl` back to 76,769. **Verified: 25 ok / 0 bad; Shumi beach plan OK (360 waypoints); aboard_test passes; parity unchanged.** **BAT: Shumi Village again. Expect berthOff to STAY small and goal to fall monotonically, then CLIMBED or an honest "did not get there" with a closest approach well inside 1,029.**

**Prior (v0.20.93 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: THE `.92` BEACH RUN SAID "REFUSED" AND WAS NOT ENTITLED TO).** The survey fired twice at Shumi and reported REFUSED both times. **The trace refutes that in its first column: `mv=23/45, 46/128, 78/294, 92/351, 110/489, 140/547` — the hull MOVED on roughly half of every frame for the whole nine seconds, so the engine refused NOTHING** — while `hd` swung 2071->1337->2261->2541->1329->865 and the distance went **1970 -> 1286 -> 591 -> 570 -> 911 -> 2277.** It closed to **591 UNITS** of the berth and then drove away from it. **CAUSE, mine: the beach-run block reused `off`/`err`, which are measured against `steerX/steerY` — THE PLANNER CURSOR — so "drive straight at it" drove straight at whatever waypoint the cursor held while the cursor kept advancing and replanning underneath it** (three `OFF-GRID` recoveries in the last four seconds). Now computes the bearing to `s_gdTargetX/Y`, the berth itself, logs `berthOff` and the closest approach, and the window goes 9,000 -> 14,000 ms. **THE WORDING IS FIXED TOO AND THAT MATTERS MORE: "That is the engine's answer, not the model's" was about to write a FALSE FACT into the record.** A moving hull is not a refusal. It now says "did not get there", with the closest approach, and states that the hull moved throughout. **This is the same error as reading the Lunar Cry cutscene as an engine block — an instrument printing a confident verdict its own data does not support. Twice in one week: WHEN A DIAGNOSTIC PRINTS A VERDICT, THE VERDICT MUST BE ENTAILED BY THE NUMBERS BESIDE IT.** What the data DOES say: the hull reaches 591 units of the beach berth on water with `gate=208` throughout, never shallow, and nothing in two runs looks like a refusal. **Whether the Garden can climb that shore is STILL UNANSWERED; this is the first build whose survey can actually ask.** **Verified: 25 ok / 0 bad; aboard_test passes.** **BAT: Shumi Village again. Expect `[GDBEACH]` lines with `berthOff` small and the distance falling monotonically, then CLIMBED or "did not get there" with a closest approach.** ⚠️ `world_garden.inl` is **78,892 bytes — 3 KB from the 80 KB hard fail. Split before the next edit that needs room.**

**Prior (v0.20.92 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: THE SHUMI COORDINATE QUESTION IS CLOSED, AND OUR COORDINATE IS RIGHT).** Aaron drove to Chocobo Forest 2, stepped off, and walked the `.91` probe point. **The foot drive reached `pos=(13000,-83984)` — SEVEN UNITS from wmsetus location record 20, the only unclaimed world-map location record in the Shumi island group — and stood there for fifty seconds. NOTHING LOADED:** no field transition, no `ChaseDetector: fieldId changed`, no dialog. **Record 20 is not Shumi Village.** So the catalog coordinate **(10362,-76967) STANDS**: Shumi Village is on the 7,034-cell island that carries the **53-polygon Garden beach**, and that island simply has no world-map location record — the record-bearing island next door is Chocobo Forest 2's. **That also removes the last doubt about the beach: the only Garden-landable shore in the area is on the same island as the marker, which is what it should be.** **THE FILE-SIDE ROUTE WAS CORRECT AND PRODUCED NOTHING, WHICH IS WORTH RECORDING.** `locID` in the trigger programs IS a field index (validated on Winhill 652->643-674, Centra Ruins 279->276-286, Dollet 327->307-345, Esthar 407-466->402-487) and **Shumi's fields are 934-945 while the table's highest locID is 705** — the trigger table is a SPECIAL-CASES table for vehicle/story-gated entries and Balamb Town, Timber and Trabia Garden are absent from it too. The field archive opened cleanly (`tmsand1.fs` and `tmgate1.fs` pulled by byte range with `dd` on the user's machine, LZS-decompressed to exact sizes, 17-file sub-archive unpacked including the `.jsm` and the `.inf` gateways referencing Shumi fields 934/936) — **but a field's gateway to the world map carries no world coordinates.** `tmsand1` = "sand", the beach field, which matches the walkthrough. **METHOD NOTE: one walk answered in fifty seconds what two hours of file archaeology could not. When the question is "what does the game do at this coordinate", the game is the cheapest instrument, and the on-foot auto-drive turns an unreachable-by-sight test into a routine one.** Probe entry removed. **ONE REAL BUG IT EXPOSED: a destination with NO FIELD TRIGGER has NO ARRIVAL CONDITION** — the drive jammed at 7 units and then said "Cannot reach the destination from here". Only diagnostic destinations can hit it, since every real one ends when its field loads; expect it if another waypoint is ever added. **Verified: 25 ok / 0 bad; beach_climb semantics OK; aboard_test passes.** **BAT: the `.90` `[GDBEACH]` run, which has still never been exercised — drive to Shumi Village and let it reach the beach.** Expect the approach, then `[GDBEACH]` lines once a second, then **CLIMBED** or **REFUSED** — no orbiting either way. Grep `[GDBEACH]`.

**Prior (v0.20.90 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: THE .89 SHUMI BAT SAID TWO THINGS, AND ONE OF THEM WAS MY MESSAGE LYING).** Aaron: *"Drove to Shumi and the mod said I was 1km out, but I could clearly hear the Garden was still in the ocean. This leads me to think our coordinates for Shumi may be incorrect."* **(1) THE MESSAGE WAS WRONG, NOT (necessarily) THE COORDINATE.** `wall-follow drift at (4203,-82788) but only 1460 units from Shumi Village` prints **`goalDist` — the distance to the BERTH — next to the name of the DESTINATION.** Everywhere else those are within 3 km and nobody notices; at Shumi the berth is a beach **12 km from the marker**, so it announced "1,460 units from Shumi Village" with the hull at sea ten kilometres out. Now reads "from the X berth (marker is N away)". **I checked the coordinate hypothesis properly rather than dismissing it as in `.65`:** our marker (10362,-76967) is terrain 17, foot-walkable, **on the village's own foot landmass**; nearest wmsetus record is Chocobo Forest 2's at **4,082 units**; and there IS an **unclaimed record 20 at (13000,-83977)** on that island — **but on a DIFFERENT foot landmass from our marker**, so swapping would move the destination off the ground it belongs to. **UNRESOLVED. Settle it the way FH was settled: walk in on foot and read the `[DRIVE] Arrival via game-mode` line.** **(2) THE BEACH — one number explains the two-minute orbit: `gate=208` on EVERY sample, so the hull never left the water, with `aim=0` throughout.** `beach_climb` opened the GOAL CELL to the planner (360 waypoints) but the EXECUTOR still has to see across the unmasked terrain-29 skirt; `GdLineClear` refuses every chord, the wall-follow engaged and never released, 1-2.6 km loops through four replans. **Opening one cell was never enough — THE CROSSING is the thing and the model does not have it.** So `.90` stops arguing with the model and **asks the engine**: inside `GD_BEACH_RUN_DIST` (2000) of a beach_climb berth the executor drives **STRAIGHT AT IT** with probes and wall-follow suppressed for up to 9 s, logging `[GDBEACH] mv/gate/cls` once a second and ending in **`CLIMBED`** or **`REFUSED`**. Either is the first real data anyone has for this shore. **Verified: 25 ok / 0 bad; `beach_climb semantics: raw=0 berth=1`; Shumi plan OK (360 waypoints); aboard_test passes; parity unchanged.** **BAT: drive to Shumi Village and let it run to the end.** Expect the approach, then `[GDBEACH]` lines once a second, then CLIMBED or REFUSED — **no orbiting either way.** Grep `[GDBEACH]`.

**Prior (v0.20.89 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: THE BEACH EXCEPTION WAS ARMED IN ONE PLACE AND ASKED ABOUT IN THREE).** `.88` BAT, Aaron: *"Did a quick test just now and Shumi did not appear in the catalog."* **The log says it outright: `catalog: 24 destinations the Garden can reach, 15 hidden` -- 24, not 25 -- and no `beach climb armed` line anywhere**, because `.88` set `s_gdBeachGoalIdx` in `Garden_StartDrive` and NOWHERE ELSE. `Garden_BuildCatalog` runs the moment you board, long before a destination is chosen, and it called `Garden_CellReachable` directly; **with the exception clear a `beach_climb` berth is unreachable BY CONSTRUCTION -- that is the whole point of it -- so the catalog hid the destination it had just been given.** The announce path had the same hole. **The exception is a property of the BERTH, not a mode the mod is in.** `Garden_BerthReachable(gp)` arms, asks and restores; the catalog, the announce and the drive all go through it, and the raw call is gone from every berth path so a caller cannot get it wrong by forgetting. **Regression guard in `garden_harness` pins the semantics outright -- `beach_climb semantics: raw=0 berth=1` -- so a future call site that asks the raw question fails a check instead of silently vanishing a destination.** **FIRST ATTEMPT DID NOT BUILD** — the forward declaration went next to `Garden_CellReachable`, which sits ABOVE the `GardenPark` struct it names (MSVC `C4430`/`C2143` at `world_map_state.inl(1025)`, two `C2664`s downstream); it is now below the struct beside `Garden_ParkFor`. **NOTE FOR NEXT TIME: `g++` passed both before AND after** — each harness defines its own `GardenPark` before including the `.inl` files, so a declaration-order error inside `world_map_state.inl` is structurally invisible to them. **The host harnesses check BEHAVIOUR; only MSVC checks the real include order.** **Verified: 25 ok / 0 bad; `Shumi beach plan from Balamb: OK (360 waypoints)`; aboard_test passes; parity unchanged (this build touches no grid bit).** **BAT: board and count the catalog -- it must say 25, and Shumi Village must be in the list. Then drive it: that is still the survey.** If it climbs, expect a park on the beach and "12.3 kilometres north -- a long walk"; if the engine refuses, expect the hull at the water's edge below (2944,-82048) with `[GDTRACE]` running. Also still worth one Winhill -> Trabia leg for the `.88` cutscene pause (`[GARDEN] cutscene at` / `cutscene over`, no reverse, no replan).

**Prior (v0.20.88 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: THE TRABIA "WEDGE" WAS A CUTSCENE, AND SHUMI VILLAGE IS RE-OPENED AS A BEACH CLIMB).** **(1) IT WAS NEVER A WEDGE.** Three BATs froze within twenty units of (60993,-44513), and `.86`'s refusal sweep read like a hard engine block: **thirteen consecutive samples over nine seconds, every one `hd=3636`, the heading never moving.** The sweep is built to rotate through sixteen headings and could not rotate at all — **I was one edit from shipping a "pinned hull" heuristic built on that.** Aaron: *"There is a spot on the world map that causes the Garden to go haywire temporarily. Nida even comments on it when you hit that spot. It is part of the game's lore as that spot is where a Lunar Cry happened in the past."* **The dialog log has it one second before the hull stopped:** `[00:12:56] [SHOW_DIALOG-TEXT] win[0] text=""Huh!?""`, then `[00:13:12] Nida "The gauge is going berserk!?"`, **window open across the whole fifteen seconds.** The engine ignores movement input while a field-dialog window is up — exactly `mv=0/57` with a frozen heading. **The FIELD drive has suspended key injection during dialog since `v05.37`; the GARDEN drive never got it.** Same predicate now, keys released, stall/throttle/route-progress/probe/announce all held at the current instant so nothing fires on time that was never ours (`[GARDEN] cutscene at` / `cutscene over at`). The `.86` pivot escape STAYS (it fired legitimately at `(-16825,-1627) off=1539` and the Winhill drive continued); the pinned-hull heuristic is DROPPED. **(2) SHUMI VILLAGE IS BACK, BERTHED ON THE BEACH.** Aaron: *"You go up the beach like any other... get as close as possible... If it runs into walls so be it, but we can at least use that data to survey."* Berth `(2944,-82048)`, inside the **53-polygon terrain-9 wedge (`b15=0xF7`) that is the ONLY Garden-masked ground among the island's 7,034 foot cells** — confirmed at polygon level, not just on the 128 grid: re-rasterising all 473,193 polygons with an ANY-covering-polygon rule gains 208 sub-points map-wide and **none on this island**, so 53 is the real number and the Garden lands and you walk. **The walk is 12,288 units** (village north shore, beach south), which is why every generator rejected it — **the 6,000 `WALK_CAP` was wrong, not the berth.** The shore reads a **295-unit step against the 200 gate** because an unmasked terrain-29 skirt sits between water and plateau; rather than loosen the measured beach rule map-wide (it is what fixed Balamb island in `.84`), a new **`beach_climb`** flag waives BOTH the water->land test and the cliff gate **for that one goal cell**, with `Garden_CellReachable` asking the flood about the water beside it and `Garden_Plan`'s O(1) refusal skipping it. **A\* routes it in 360 waypoints.** Arrivals past 5 km now announce KILOMETRES ("12.3 kilometres north -- a long walk"). If the engine refuses the climb, the drive stops at a KNOWN COORDINATE with `[GDTRACE]` running, which is the survey. **Verified: 25 ok / 0 bad including Shumi with its exception armed, plus a new harness check that A\* can ROUTE to the beach rather than merely that reachability says yes; parity clean including CLEAR; replay on the `.86` log 194/194 on every check; aboard_test passes.** **BAT: (a) Winhill -> Trabia Garden — expect `[GARDEN] cutscene at (60xxx,-445xx)` then `cutscene over`, with NO reverse and NO replan. (b) SHUMI VILLAGE from anywhere — this is the survey. Whatever happens, the log is the result: if it climbs, expect a park on the beach and "12.3 kilometres north"; if it refuses, expect the hull to stop at the water's edge below (2944,-82048) with the trace running.** **LESSON, twice this week: the user plays this game and I do not. Ask what a place IS before modelling why it refuses.**

**Prior (v0.20.86 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: A STATIONARY GARDEN CANNOT ALWAYS TURN, SO WITHHOLDING THE THROTTLE IN ORDER TO TURN CAN DEADLOCK).** `.85` BAT: Centra Ruins, Winhill and Trabia Garden all arrived, `parkBit=1 canDisembark=1` on each. **REPLAY CAME BACK CLEAN FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THE PROJECT — 309 samples, `cell`/`cls`/`clear`/`blk`/`aim` all 100.00%, "at every logged position the model computed what the game computed."** The `.85` polar fix is confirmed in the trace: rows 765, 4, 10, 15, 20, 26, 31, 36 on the Winhill->Trabia crossing all read `clear=32` where `.84` read the row index. **WHAT IS LEFT IS ONE WEDGE THAT HAS NOW APPEARED IN BOTH BATS SEVENTEEN UNITS APART** — (60976,-44518) in `.84`, (60993,-44530) in `.85` — with fourteen seconds of identical trace each time: `pos=(60993,-44530) hd=3616 clear=2 off=582 blk=0 aim=1 guard=0/0/0 mv=0/57 keys=--R-`. **`off` is 582 against a 576 cone, so the executor pressed RIGHT WITH NO THROTTLE — and `hd=3616` NEVER CHANGED.** The engine applies rotation as part of a MOVE, so a refused candidate step leaves the hull neither travelling nor turning; nothing changes `off`, nothing restores the throttle. **Turn -> no move -> no turn, broken only by the 15-second route-progress watchdog, twice.** The cone is right for steering and wrong as an absolute veto: with the bow clear the throttle now comes back on after **`GD_PIVOT_MS` = 1500 ms** of going nowhere — the same forward creep the wall-follow already uses to round a headland, and a healthy pivot finishes in ~1.1 s at the measured 9 u/frame. **WHY NO MATRIX COULD EVER HAVE FOUND IT: `gexec3` turns the hull whether or not the move succeeds (`hd = (hd + ...)` unconditional) and the engine does not, so the loop that produces this wedge CANNOT CLOSE OFFLINE.** That is why 356-run matrices kept coming back clean while the same 17-unit patch wedged twice on the machine. The escape is mirrored on both sides and **the rotation coupling is now written down in `gexec3` as a known model gap** rather than left as an accident. **Verified: replay 309/309 on every check; parity clean including CLEAR; 24 ok / 0 bad; aboard_test passes.** **BAT: Winhill -> Trabia Garden again — that is the leg that wedged twice.** Expect either no stop at all, or a `[GARDEN] pivot deadlock at ... adding throttle` line and an immediate recovery instead of fifteen seconds of silence. One ordinary leg as a regression check. **STILL OPEN: Shumi Village** — the beach is real (53 terrain-9 polys at (2368..3264,-81984..-83264), `b15=0xF7`) but the walk to the village is 12,288 against a 6,000 `WALK_CAP` and the shore step is 295 against the 200 gate; and **the engine's rotation/movement coupling is still unmodelled offline.**

**Prior (v0.20.85 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: THE CLEARANCE FIELD NEVER WRAPPED AT THE POLE, FOUND BY REPLAY IN A COMPLETELY CLEAN BAT).** `.84` BAT: *"That worked great! It successfully drove to all the locations I tried... arrived at 2-3km from each destination."* **Nine destinations, nine arrivals, two replans in the whole session** — Balamb Town 2366/0, Fire Cavern 2998/1, Centra Ruins 2887/0, Winhill 2637/0, Trabia Garden 2886/1, Timber 2929/0, Edea's House 2666/0, Tomb of the Unknown King 2049/0, and `Docked at Fisherman's Horizon`. `parkBit=1 canDisembark=1` on every one, **zero `plan FAILED`**, catalog a constant **24** at every location so Fire Cavern and Balamb Town stayed visible off-island, and reachability **662,681 on all twelve floods including cell (528,338) at the Tomb — 655,108 and outside the pocket in `.83`.** Both replans were recovery machinery working: Fire Cavern's guard-drift abort fired at 1,063 units against a 1,000 bound and re-planned in 91 expansions. **REPLAY ON THAT LOG: `cell`, `cls`, `blk`, `aim` ALL 590/590 EXACT** — the `.84` shelf-based beach model is now confirmed against the ENGINE on a real drive, not against a simulator that assumed it. **The one divergence names its own bug:** 12 `clear` samples on the polar crossing Winhill -> Trabia where the game said 27, 22, 17, 12, 6, 2, 0, 3, 11, 18, 26 — **those are the ROW INDEX.** `.69` made the world a torus in both axes and missed the clearance chamfer, which still treated row 0 and row 767 as solid wall. **Silent and exactly backwards:** below `GD_TIGHT_CLEAR` the executor switches to short bow probes and the planner adds `GD_TIGHT_PENALTY` per cell, so a 32-cell band of empty sea at the pole was priced and driven as a narrow channel — on the one route where crossing the pole is the whole point. **59,456 cells had the wrong clearance; 12,288 were below the planner's clear target and are not; 4,096 were below the tight threshold and are not.** Rows wrap now, and since a chamfer only converges in two passes on an OPEN grid the forward/backward pair repeats until stable (3-4 iterations, once, at load). **`gsim3` had the OPPOSITE error** — scipy treats the array edge as background, so the model called the pole open while the C++ called it walled — **and the two cancelled, invisibly, until a real drive separated them. `parity_check.py` now compares CLEAR too** and the harness dumps `gcheck/gdclear.bin` for it. Also: the version string still read `0.20.83` in the `.84` build. **Verified: parity clean including CLEAR on all 786,432 cells; 24 ok / 0 bad; aboard_test passes.** **BAT: one polar crossing — Winhill -> Trabia Garden or Centra -> Shumi's latitude — and grep `[GDTRACE] clear=` near rows 0 and 767; it should read 32, not the row number. Plus one ordinary leg as a regression check.** **STILL OPEN: Shumi Village** — the beach is real (53 terrain-9 polys at (2368..3264, -81984..-83264), `b15=0xF7`) but the walk from it to the village is 12,288 against a 6,000 `WALK_CAP`, and the shore step is 295 against the 200 gate.

**Prior (v0.20.84 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: `[0x203EE88]` IS THE GARDEN'S ALTITUDE, AND BALAMB ISLAND WAS A ONE-WAY TRAP).** `.83` BAT: Tomb of the Unknown King drove for the first time, **but Balamb Town would not plan and Fire Cavern vanished from the catalog**, and Shumi Village parked in open water and stranded him. **The log names it in two lines:** reachability is `657082` cells from Balamb and `655108` from the Tomb — a difference of exactly **1,974 cells, every one of them on Balamb island** — followed by `plan FAILED (-44090,-37564)->(15232,-25216) after 655108 expansions`, three times. **The Garden could leave the island the save starts it on and never get back.** **Cause: `[0x203EE88]` is not a flag.** `0x54B49F` copies the active entity's position into `0x203EE80/84/88` as `(x,-z,y)`, so the value the whitelist branch tests is the **hull's own altitude**. Measured across **296 `[GDTRACE]` samples** against the polygon under the hull: terrain 32 gives −254..−58 (**46/46 ≤ 0**), terrain 33/34 gives +208/+210 (**126/127 > 0**) — and the **mesh height is 0 on all three**. So altitude follows the terrain CLASS and `.73`'s "allowed terrain above sea level" was a proxy that coincided at the Tomb and **nowhere on Balamb's coast**: 1,285 beaches map-wide, not one of them there. **`GDC_BEACH` is now terrain 30..32 — 9,504 cells — and the flood is symmetric: 662,681 from Balamb and 662,681 from the Tomb.** Balamb Town and Fire Cavern return **without their berths moving a unit**. **Also fixed:** `Garden_CellReachable` accepted a berth if any of the *nine cells around it* was in the flood, so the catalog promised what A* then had to close a whole component to refuse — it tests the berth's own cell now, and `Garden_Plan` refuses an out-of-flood goal in O(1) instead of 655k expansions. **Berths:** `offline/gen_berths.py` applies all four constraints in one pass (reachable / ashore with a step-off / **same foot landmass** / 2-3 km standoff); **17 of 22 shipped berths still pass and are kept verbatim**, including every one Aaron has driven. 24 of 39 have a berth. **SHUMI: Aaron is right and I was wrong twice.** The walkthrough says *"use the nearby beach to take the ship up onto the land"*, and **the beach is in the data** — 53 terrain-9 polygons at (2368..3264, −81984..−83264), `b15=0xF7`, the island's only Garden-masked ground. It stays hidden **this build only** because (a) the beach-to-village walk is **12,288 units against a 6,000 `WALK_CAP`** and (b) the shore step is **295 units against the 200 gate**, with an unmasked terrain-29 skirt in between — the engine climbs it, and I cannot yet say how. **Next: drive to (3200,−81792), hold the throttle at the wedge, log `mv=n/N` and `[0x203EE88]` per frame.** **`world_garden.inl` was 86,261 bytes — over the 80 KB hard fail, and already 84,567 at `.83`, so THAT BUILD COULD NOT HAVE BEEN PUSHED EITHER.** Split at the end of `Garden_Plan` into `world_garden_plan.inl` (21.4 KB) + `world_garden.inl` (65.6 KB). **Verified: parity clean (BEACH 9,504 both sides); replay 296 samples — cell/clear/aim 100.00%, and the ONLY divergences are 44 `cls` that are the BEACH bit alone plus 7 `blk` downstream, i.e. exactly the intended change; route regression 23 berths x 4 starts x 4 headings = 356 runs, ZERO failures, including the Tomb start that broke; 24 ok / 0 bad; aboard_test passes.** **BAT: from Balamb, drive to the Tomb, then FROM THERE pick Balamb Town — that is the exact leg `.83` refused — and confirm Fire Cavern is still in the catalog after you have left Balamb island. One long regression leg (Trabia or Centra) too.**

**Prior (v0.20.69 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: THE WORLD MAP IS A TORUS IN BOTH AXES AND THE GRID ONLY KNEW ABOUT ONE).** `.68` BAT: **clear win — Deling City AND Centra Ruins both drove, so the beach model holds.** Then Centra Ruins -> Shumi Village wedged. Aaron: *"make sure the route planner understands that you can go around the world... you go down around the southern pole."* **The inconsistency was ours:** `CalculateWrappedDistance` and `TorusBearing` have wrapped in y since the beginning, but the GRID did not — every row-neighbour test was guarded by `nr < 0 || nr >= GD_ROWS` and `Garden_BuildEnd` hard-wired the last row's south edge shut. **So the planner MEASURED over the pole and could not ROUTE there** — the heuristic pulls toward a shortcut absent from the graph, which is exactly the shape that yields a long confident detour ending in a wedge. **Rows now wrap like columns:** seam is a normal edge (south edges 669,555 -> **670,579**, precisely one row of 1,024); `GdEdgeOpen`/`GdStepOpen`/`GdSnapWalk`/the flood/`Garden_CellReachable`/the A* loop all take rows mod `GD_ROWS`; new `GdRowDelta`/`GdColDelta` give the heuristic and both endpoint tests the shorter way round per axis. **THAT ALONE WAS NOT ENOUGH — the simulation caught the second half:** the route crossed the seam and the hull still would not drive a step of it, wedging 27.7 km short at `(24777,97926)` ON the seam, because **`GdLineClear`'s cell-transition check wrapped `dc` but not `dr`** — a chord over the pole read as a 767-row jump and every probe returned blocked. Both deltas now wrap. **LESSON: a wrap-around assumption has to be made everywhere at once — distance, bearing, grid edges, flood, heuristic AND the line probe — or the halves disagree and the disagreement looks like a wedge.** That is twice in two builds that fixing the planner exposed a matching gap in the executor. **Verified: Centra Ruins <-> Shumi Village arrives in BOTH directions over the pole, 0 replans; FULL MATRIX 26 destinations x 6 starts x 3 combos = 450 runs, ZERO failures; offline model and C++ agree cell-for-cell on all three grid totals (678,223 / 671,236 / 670,579); 26 ok / 0 bad; aboard_test passes.** **BAT: Centra Ruins -> Shumi Village, the leg that failed** — expect it to head SOUTH over the pole, which will sound wrong and is correct. Plus one ordinary leg (Balamb -> Trabia / Deling City) as a regression check. **STILL OPEN: the coordinate audit** — 19 catalog entries sit >600u from any wmsetus location record (Edea's House 6.4 km, White SeeD 4.5 km, Shumi 4.1 km, Esthar City 7.1 km, Lunar Gate 9.1 km, Cactuar Island 17.6 km); FH proved that class of error is real, silent and expensive.

**Prior (v0.20.68 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: YOU GET ONTO A LANDMASS BY DRIVING UP A BEACH, AND BEACHES ARE RARE).** Aaron supplied the model the grid was missing: *"to get up on land masses you must pilot the Garden up a beach coastline... once you go up the beach you pilot the Garden overland to get close to the location."* **Counted in the data: only 8,565 cells on the whole map are a Garden-navigable sea cell meeting Garden-navigable land across a step under the 200 gate.** Everywhere else the coast is a 200-400 unit escarpment the engine refuses. **Centra Ruins' berth is a LAND cell (20 of 26 are) and the nearest climbable beach is 4,574 units away at (5824,50752)** — flooding from that beach with the engine gate DOES reach the berth, so the route was always there. **Two faults stopped it being driven.** (1) **EXECUTOR cut the corner**: a beach is 1-2 cells wide and the 1536u lookahead let the hull aim past the gateway and arrive at the land cell across a boundary that is NOT climbable — engine refuses, hull holds UP at the water's edge. That is the `stalled at (3446,36029)` / `stalled at (-8879,56836)` signature from BOTH BATs. **Lookahead now collapses to one waypoint when clearance < 3 cells.** (2) **PLANNER could not get ashore at all**: `.67`'s sea-first mask banned land beyond six cells of an endpoint, and the beach is 4,574 out. **Land is now biased (x2.5), not banned** — A* stays healthy, plans 22-34 ms. **PROCESS FAULT OWNED: the offline model and the shipped C++ had DIVERGED** — `gsim3.py` kept a x12 penalty while `world_garden.inl` carried a hard sea-first mask, so the offline matrix was passing a build that did not exist. That is how `.67` shipped broken. **They now carry the identical rule, and the matrix gates the release.** **Verified: FULL MATRIX 26 destinations x 6 starts x 3 speed/turn combos = 450 runs, ZERO failures**, Centra arriving from both BAT stall points with 0 replans; 26 ok / 0 bad; aboard_test passes. **BAT: Timber -> Centra Ruins and FH -> Centra Ruins.** Expect the hull to run open water, then slow and thread a specific beach, then drive overland to the berth. Also one previously-good long leg (Balamb -> Trabia / Deling City) as a regression check. **STILL OPEN: the coordinate audit** — 19 catalog entries sit >600u from any wmsetus location record (Edea's House 6.4 km, White SeeD 4.5 km, Shumi 4.1 km, Esthar City 7.1 km, Lunar Gate 9.1 km, Cactuar Island 17.6 km). FH proved that class of error is real, silent and expensive.

**Prior (v0.20.67 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: THE PLANNER WAS ROUTING THE GARDEN OVERLAND).** `.66` BAT: FH works (confirmed twice), Centra Ruins failed from every start — stalled at `(3446,36029)` from one, `(-8879,56836)` from another. **`stalled` means the executor is holding UP and the hull is NOT MOVING: the engine is refusing.** Both points sit exactly at a water's edge with terrain rising inland (0 -> -27 -> -381 over two 128u steps in one case). **Replaying the leg offline says why: the route from (-8879,56836) was 66 of its 67 waypoints ON LAND, straight across the Centra continent.** The Garden mask admits a lot of land (47% of terrain 7 carries bit 0x20) and the planner took that literally — **but the mask says where the hull may BE, not how it may GET there.** Every Centra coast is an escarpment of 200-400 units per 128u and the 200 gate refuses the climb, so the hull drove to where the route left the sea and stopped. **Fix: the A* is SEA-FIRST** — foot-walkable cells excluded outright, with a permissive retry if no sea route exists at all. **TWO alternatives were implemented and REJECTED ON MEASUREMENT: (1) banning land globally loses ALL 26 berths** (every berth is a coastal cell that is both Garden- and foot-walkable — that is what a berth IS; 665,412 reachable cells -> 184,745); **(2) a x12 cost multiplier routes correctly but wrecks A*'s heuristic** — the search degenerates toward Dijkstra and one plan took MINUTES, a frame-rate hazard in-game, not just a slow harness. The hard mask keeps every edge cost at 1.0 so planning stays **12-28 ms**. Land is exempt within **six cells** of either endpoint because many berths ARE land cells (every walk=0 berth — the hull parks on the destination). **The failing leg: 17.5 km with 66/67 overland -> 38.8 km with 17/127, running the coast by sea.** Coordinate re-checked FIRST per the `.65` lesson — Centra matches wmsetus record 16 exactly, so this really was the route and not another bad input. **Verified: Centra Ruins arrives from 8 starts x 2 combos, 0 replans, INCLUDING BOTH BAT STALL POINTS.** 26 ok / 0 bad; aboard_test passes. **CAVEAT: the broader 26-destination matrix did NOT finish this session** — the targeted Centra run is what backs this build, so watch for regressions on other long legs. **BAT: Timber -> Centra Ruins and FH -> Centra Ruins, the two that failed.** Expect noticeably longer routes that hug the coast by sea. Also re-drive one previously-good long leg (Balamb -> Trabia or Deling City) to check nothing regressed. **STILL OPEN: the coordinate audit** (19 entries >600u from any wmsetus record) and the unfinished matrix.

**Prior (v0.20.66 LOCAL, NOT MSVC-built — #80: LIMIT-CYCLE BRAKE on the wall-follow).** `.65` BAT: **the FH coordinate fix WORKS** — Aaron drove Balamb Garden to Fisherman's Horizon, then FH to Timber, both clean.
